# Arxiv Papers in cs.CV on 2026-11-18
### PRISM: Profiling and Representation of Individual Stereotypical Motion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23646v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23646v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 00:09:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 00:09:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Austin A. MBaye, Savik Kinger, Matthew S. Goodwin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: An anatomical pose provides interpretable joint structure, but no fixed set of physical landmarks can fully specify a person's movement. We introduce PRISM, a complementary representation that characterizes repetitive movement using tracked masks and dense optical flow. Torso-relative flow measures intensity, frame-wise mask variation measures spatial extent, signed-flow recurrence estimates frequency, and persistent sliding-window geometry describes the rhythmicity of the movement. Across 1,890 synthetic conditions, the measurements recover controlled speed, amplitude, and frequency while exposing observation limits. An idealized hand-motion experiment demonstrates variation omitted by identical exact COCO-17 trajectories; controlled corruption experiments separately examine measurement sensitivity. Audits of two pose methods on the same data cohort and 36,930 external skeleton clips document incomplete observation and dependence on processing choices. In 1,404 annotated complex stereotypical motor movement bouts from six autistic participants, total flow converges with pose motion, while matched-coordinate examples and repeated-session profiles show differences in intensity, extent, frequency, and rhythmicity within behavior labels. A separate VLM comparison evaluates quantitative frequency estimation, and a profile-conditioned decoder links the measurements to illustrative skeletal movements. PRISM connects numerical profiles to visible examples, providing an interpretable way to compare how the same behavior is expressed beyond its label or event count.



### LightSEAD: Lightweight SE(3)-Aware Keypoint Detector through Selective Knowledge Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23661v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23661v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 00:32:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 00:32:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Mayowa Adebambo, Alexander D. Rast, Andrew Bradley
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The pursuit of improved performance in deep learning has often been accompanied by increasing architectural complexity and computational demands. We explore an alternative direction: achieving more with less through structured supervision. We introduce LightSEAD, a lightweight SE(3)-aware keypoint detector initialised with calibrated Harris pseudo-labels and enriched through viewpoint-aware and appearance-aware supervision and selective knowledge distillation. LightSEAD comprises only six convolutional layers and 84,289 learnable parameters, achieving competitive repeatability across HPatches, KITTI 06 and 4Seasons while demonstrating its downstream applicability to relative pose estimation on MegaDepth-1500. Further optimised for mixed-precision inference, LightSEAD Coarse achieves approximately 909 FPS on an NVIDIA RTX 4090 and 139 FPS on a Jetson Orin Nano Super.



### EyeDiT: Anatomy-Grounded Multi-Modal Eye Generation and Simulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23670v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23670v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 00:51:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 00:51:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Yonghan Lee, Zachary Willms, Kun Liu, Pushkar Anand, Ali Behrooz, Dmitri Model, Dinesh Manocha, Yatong An
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Synthetic eye data are critical for rapid development of eye tracking (ET) in AR/VR applications. However, existing synthetic eye generators are largely image-centric and lack a high-quality underlying 3D anatomy of the eye, which limits their control mainly to the gaze and provides no anatomical labels. To address this problem, we propose EyeDiT, an anatomy-grounded, multi-modal 3D eye generation and simulation model trained with optical coherence tomography (OCT), labeled anatomy point clouds and near-eye images. Our pipeline first trains a Multi-Modal Anatomy Variational Autoencoder (VAE), which encodes an anatomy-aware latent representation from all input modalities. Then, our Eye Latent DiT is trained to generate latent samples conditioned on the optical axis, the pupil position and radius, and the subject identity. Together with Gaussian decoders for novel-view simulation, our model enables control over the pupil, subject identity and viewpoint for data synthesis. We show that our model outperforms previous eye image synthesis models in novel-view synthesis (24.9 against 21.8 dB PSNR from a single view), and that a public ET neural network trained on our synthetic data shows a smaller sim-to-real gap than when trained on data from other generators (3.5$^\circ$ vs. 8.4$^\circ$ gaze error), approaches a network trained on real data, and further improves ET performance when combined with real data (2.3$^\circ$ vs. 2.6$^\circ$). More information is available on the project page: https://lyhan12.github.io/eyedit



### Image-Condition-Controlled Diffusion Network with Large-Scale CT Pretraining for CT-Free Attenuation Correction in Dynamic Cardiac PET
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23672v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23672v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 00:55:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 00:55:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhejia Zhang, Scott M. Leonard, Jinkui Hao, Ryan J. Avery, Richard L. Weinberg, Bo Zhou
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables. Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Radiation and Plasma Medical Sciences
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Positron emission tomography (PET) provides quantitative information on myocardial perfusion. In dynamic PET, tracer kinetics are tracked through a series of reconstructed time frames. Reliable attenuation correction is essential for accurate measurements. Clinical attenuation maps ($μ$-maps) are typically derived from computed tomography (CT), which adds radiation exposure and may introduce PET-CT misregistration due to respiratory and cardiac motion. Moreover, supervised CT-free methods rely on paired non-attenuation-corrected (NAC) PET and CT data, which are scarce, whereas large-scale unpaired CT data are more readily available. To address these challenges, we propose Large-scale Unpaired CT-Informed Diffusion (LUCID), a framework that transfers anatomical priors learned from 10,000 chest CT volumes in the public CT-RATE dataset to $μ$-map synthesis for dynamic cardiac $^{82}$Rb PET. First, a convolutional neural network (CNN) predicts an initial $μ$-map from each NAC PET frame. Second, the CT-pretrained diffusion backbone refines a low-noise perturbation of the CNN prediction through a single denoising step, conditioned on the corresponding NAC PET frame via ControlNet. Finally, frame-wise predictions are averaged voxel-wise to obtain the final $μ$-map. We further introduce the voxel-wise temporal standard deviation (TSD) to quantify inter-frame variability without requiring a reference $μ$-map. We evaluated LUCID on an in-house dataset of 102 patients and found that it achieved the best quantitative accuracy while maintaining competitive inter-frame consistency. Ablation studies showed that refinement with a diffusion backbone pretrained on in-house CT data from the training patients was less accurate than the CNN prediction alone, highlighting the importance of large-scale CT pretraining.



### Learning What to Degrade: A Recoverability-Aware Curriculum for Reliable Smart-Glasses Super-Resolution
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23674v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23674v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 00:58:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 00:58:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Yaopeng Wang, Qingliang Wang, Yilun Jin, Xiujin Liu, Han Yue, Chen Xia, Renjie Zhu, Chuang Sheng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Degradations in smart-glasses images obscure small text and fine-grained structures needed by vision-language models (VLMs) for visual assistance. We introduce an high-resolution (HR)-referenced semantic evaluation protocol that compares VLM outputs on restored images with those on corresponding HR references. Our evaluation reveals two failure modes: generative SR can synthesize misleading details, whereas restoration-oriented SR can leave critical evidence blurred or ambiguous. These findings motivate improving faithful detail recovery in restoration-oriented SR, where a key training challenge is to provide sufficiently difficult inputs while retaining recoverable visual information. We propose ReDiC (Recoverability-Difficulty Curriculum), which combines a model-independent recoverability proxy with model-relative restoration difficulty to adapt training degradation severity. ReDiC increases severity when inputs are recoverable and restoration is easy, maintains challenging but recoverable conditions, and reduces severity when estimated recoverability is low. It modifies only training degradation generation, leaving the SR architecture and reconstruction objective unchanged. Across NAFNet, SwinIR, and MambaIR, ReDiC improves average PSNR by approximately 2.5 dB and HR-referenced semantic scores by about 3 points over the corresponding baselines on synthetic benchmarks, with further gains confirmed by LLM-based and human evaluations on 200 real smart-glasses captures.



### Structured Extraction of Diffraction Data from Covalent Organic Frameworks
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23680v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cond-mat.mtrl-sci, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23680v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 01:10:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 01:10:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Shenghan Gao, Zhiling Zheng
- **Comment**: 66 pages: main text (19 pages, 7 figures, 3 tables) and Supporting Information (47 pages) in one file. Data and code: https://github.com/AaronG211/pxrd_PEAK ; database website: https://pxrd-peak.vercel.app
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Powder X-ray diffraction (PXRD) is the primary structural evidence for covalent organic frameworks (COFs) and many other materials. Yet decades of measured patterns exist only as images in published figures. Automatic recovery is difficult, because PXRD figures vary widely in format, from a single curve to crowded stacks with labels, insets, and neighboring plots that challenge traditional computer vision. This work presents a pipeline in which a vision large language model (LLM) interprets the layout of each figure, locating the diffraction plot and its curves and sketching a coarse trace of each, while classical computer vision (CV) refines every trace to pixel-level accuracy. Applied to 3,954 COF articles, the pipeline produces 11,445 calibrated diffraction curves, released in the open database COF-PEAK. Compared with a hand digitization of 60 panels, extracted peak positions differ by a median of only 0.07 degrees. Whereas papers usually report only a few peak angles, which limits further analysis, the digitized curves enable tracking how peak prominence, a possible marker of crystallinity, changes with synthesis conditions. Vision LLMs and classical CV can thus turn such figures into reusable experimental data.



### MoMoVL: Modality-Aware Adaptive Compression for On-Device Sparsely-Activated Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23695v1
- **DOI**: 10.1145/3862322.3863775
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23695v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 01:36:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 01:36:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Dongkeun Lee, Wonjun Lee
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 6 figures, Accepted at WSDM 2027 Findings
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The rise of the agentic web has been accompanied by growing demand for on-device Vision-Language Models (VLMs) that perform diverse cross-modal web tasks with enhanced privacy and reduced latency. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models are widely adopted in modern VLMs for compute-efficient scalability, yet their multi-expert architecture poses deployment challenges due to substantial memory demands. Mixed-precision quantization is an effective compression technique that reduces bit-widths while adapting to the heterogeneous hardware budgets of edge devices, particularly well-suited for MoE due to its inherent structural sparsity, which causes imbalanced importance among experts. While prior work has applied quantization for efficient MoE inference at the edge, we point out that in MoE-based VLMs, the modality-specific characteristics of visual and text tokens further impact expert quantization. To address this issue, we present MoMoVL, a modality-aware adaptive compression framework for MoE-based VLMs that brings vision-language inference within reach of on-device resource budgets. Motivated by our empirical findings on modality-induced disparities across experts, MoMoVL jointly exploits the per-expert activation profile and per-modality quantization sensitivity to guide bit-width assignment. Experimental results show that MoMoVL improves the accuracy-compression trade-off relative to state-of-the-art mixed-precision baselines and reduces memory footprint by up to 15.44% with a modest drop in accuracy over its uniform-precision baseline. Our code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/sco-edge/MoMoVL.



### What Do Learned 3D Models Add to Localization from Posed References?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23697v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23697v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 01:41:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 01:41:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Jaehyun Kim, Wonseok Kang, Jeongmin Lee, Tae-Wan Kim
- **Comment**: 10 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Localizing a query from a few posed reference images avoids building a 3D point map. We ask what the camera predictions of learned 3D models add to geometric estimation from the same references, and where failures arise. Across eight learned models and variants and five test sets, we compare reference budgets, swap pose components, and intervene on the references a model sees. At fine pose-error thresholds, geometric estimation with one additional reference is significantly more accurate than every model on two references on Cambridge and NAVER B1 and never significantly worse, alone or as a fallback behind a shared acceptance gate; with the same three references, the more accurate approach depends on the scene. The gain from the learned translation magnitude depends on reference selection: it improves seven of the eight models on 12-Scenes with the spread-out pairs we select, but none of the five we test with the two top-ranked retrieved references. Where retrieval also returns references from other locations, at NAVER Gangnam Station, models fail at different stages. A robust alignment brings Pi3X and MapAnything within 3.3 pp of adding references as needed. In controlled tests, aligning on the nearby references largely restores VGGT, Pi3X and MapAnything but not Depth Anything 3, and pose-conditioned Pi3X recovers only if the far poses are masked and left out of its input-pose normalization.



### A Hardware-Aware Comprehensive Study of Edge Vision Deployment with A Unified Hardware-Model-Runtime Mapping Framework
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23705v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23705v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 01:54:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 01:54:05+00:00
- **Authors**: Le Duc An, Le Duc Thanh, Vo Hoai Viet
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The rapid growth of deep learning has driven the deployment of computer vision directly on resource-constrained edge devices. However, the extreme heterogeneity of edge hardware architectures poses major challenges in selecting and designing suitable vision models. This survey bridges the gap between edge hardware constraints, model architectures, and deployment software toolchains. We establish a comprehensive taxonomy classifying edge hardware into three distinct tiers: mid-range devices (GPUs and dedicated NPUs or TPUs), dedicated embedded MPU or SoC processors, and microcontrollers (MCUs). We aggregate and analyze multi-source benchmark results for image classification and object detection across the computing spectrum, spanning from tens of watts down to milliwatt-scale bare-metal MCUs. Based on these empirical findings, we propose a two-tiered hardware-to-model mapping framework that provides concrete model selection matrices across performance priorities (Speed, Balanced, Accuracy) and actionable architectural design principles (peak SRAM budgeting, resolution-first scaling, and algorithm-compiler co-design). Finally, we highlight critical deployment challenges including toolchain fragmentation, operator incompatibility, and Vision Transformer feasibility, and outline promising future research directions in on-device adaptation and sustainable Edge AI.



### CoScan: Multi-Scale Content-Adaptive Space-Filling Scans for Causal State-Space Image Restoration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23717v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23717v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 02:19:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 02:19:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Wei Dong, Terry Ji, Yan Min, Shahram Shirani, Jun Chen, Han Zhou
- **Comment**: Accepted by NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Selective state-space models (SSMs) have recently shown strong potential for efficient image restoration. A central difficulty, however, is that a 2D feature map typically needs to be linearized into a 1D token sequence for causal state-space updates, making restoration performance highly sensitive to the imposed scan order. Existing deterministic scans follow space-filling principles but remain fixed and content-agnostic, and can yield fragmented or semantically inconsistent contexts under degradations. We propose \textbf{CoScan}, a \textbf{co}ntent-adaptive \textbf{scan}-order design for causal vision SSMs. CoScan learns image-dependent space-filling traversals via differentiable proxy supervision driven by \textbf{scan-quality objectives} that \textit{encourage short-range coherence and causal predictability, while preserving spatial adjacency}. To support hierarchical restoration backbones, we introduce an \textbf{efficient multi-scale scan construction by coarsening a fine-level minimum spanning tree}, producing coherent scans across feature resolutions. In addition, a \textbf{frequency-guided start selection strategy} is designed to \textit{place informative structures early in the sequence prefix to strengthen causal context formation}. Plugged into existing Mamba-based backbones, CoScan yields consistent improvements across several restoration benchmarks with minimal overhead and shows promising results on high-level vision tasks. Code will be released at https://github.com/dongw22/CoScan



### Do 3D Point Representations Encode Architectural Functions? Probing Supervised and Pretrained Models on Urban Facades
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23720v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23720v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 02:24:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 02:24:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Abdiaziz Muse, Jiju Poovvancheri
- **Comment**: Accepted at the NeurIPS 2026 Workshop: Physical World AI: Geometry, Characteristics, and Multimodal Sensing
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Semantic labels say what a building element is, not what it does: two identical-looking windows on one facade may be operable or fixed, a column may carry load or only decorate. We ask whether 3D point representations encode such architectural function. On 22 mobile-LiDAR buildings with 15 functional attributes per element, we hold out entire buildings and compare hand-crafted descriptors, networks trained from scratch and frozen self-supervised point transformers. Descriptors and frozen features are read by the same linear layer. No network trained from scratch, from PointNet to a 108M-parameter PTv3, clearly exceeds the descriptors (0.610 mAP on functionally ambiguous elements), whereas frozen Utonia reaches 0.732. Random-weight controls of the same architectures (0.649 and 0.652) attribute the gain to pretraining (Utonia: +0.080, 95% building interval [+0.035, +0.124]). Pretrained features improve at every tested number of labelled buildings while descriptors and random features plateau, and, in the configurations we tested, fine-tuning erodes the benefit: at today's data scale, function is better read from pretrained representations than learned from scratch.



### GTA: Generative Tower of Agents for Scalable Holonic Scene Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23727v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23727v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 02:39:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 02:39:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeng Tao, Yunuo Chen, Xiang Feng, Yumeng He, Peihao Li, Nanxiang Jiang, Yaowei Guo, Fanlian Zeng, Yin Yang, Chenfanfu Jiang
- **Comment**: https://zt-zeng-tao.github.io/GTA/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Scalable world modeling requires coordinating scene-wide goals with fine-grained objects and dynamic characters under limited time and context budgets. We introduce GTA (Generative Tower of Agents), which represents every unit at every level of an adaptive scene hierarchy as a holon, both a self-contained construction task and a composable scene unit. Parallel agents recursively plan the hierarchy top-down, generate assets, and assemble and refine them bottom-up. Uniform interfaces carry scene-wide requirements down the hierarchy, guiding local construction so that each holon fits into the larger one. GTA accelerates scalable world modeling through high-throughput parallel execution while preserving well-structured layouts and fine-grained asset details. GTA then populates the assembled environments with dynamic human characters whose appearance and motion are guided by the scene, yielding realized 4D worlds. Project page: https://zt-zeng-tao.github.io/GTA/



### AdaS-ViT: Test-Time Adaptive Routing for Spiking Vision Transformers with Ultra-High Energy Efficiency
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23728v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.NE**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23728v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 02:40:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 02:40:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Zecheng Hao, Zihan Huang, Shenghao Xie, Jiyuan Zhang, Zhaofei Yu, Tiejun Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) promise event-driven and energy-efficient vision, yet existing spiking vision transformers (S-ViTs) largely confine spiking tokens to fixed and uniform firing schemes along the temporal, spatial and channel dimensions, whereas suppressing the computational redundancy thus incurred calls for adaptive firing mechanisms that operate jointly across all three axes at test time. To this end, we propose the Adaptive Efficient Integrate-and-Fire (AdaE-IF) model, which consults the attention score of each spiking token and routes salient regions to fine-grained firing patterns while assigning energy-frugal coarse patterns to the remaining regions. Building on AdaE-IF, we further introduce Spiking Gated Self-Attention (S-GSA) and Spiking Local-Gated Unit (S-LGU), which enforce selective spike emission at token and channel granularity. We integrate the aforementioned mechanisms and modules into AdaS-ViT, a test-time adaptive-routing architecture trained with a progressive spatio-temporal curriculum that stabilizes the co-adaptation of firing precision and routing masks. Extensive experiments across benchmarks spanning diverse data scales and modalities demonstrate that AdaS-ViT consistently achieves highly competitive performance. Notably, on ImageNet-1k it consumes only 1.02 to 2.45 millijoules per image and attains an accuracy-per-energy ratio of 32$\times$ to 72$\times$, operating in an economical inference regime and thereby pushing the energy-efficiency boundary further.



### GaussLearner: 3D Gaussian Primitives as Self-Regulating Continual Learners
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23736v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23736v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 02:49:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 02:49:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Gaoxiang Linghu, Haonan Zhou, Xuanhe Liu, Weili Xu, Kaiyue Zhou, Jun Xiao, Gaoang Wang
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://gaoxiang-linghu.github.io/GaussLearner/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Continual learning of 3D Gaussian fields aims to maintain faithful reconstructions of the evolving world. However, directly fine-tuning an existing Gaussian field on new observations can cause catastrophic forgetting of previously reconstructed content. To address this challenge, existing methods employ selective local optimization, replay, or parameter regularization, yet still struggle to balance adaptation to scene changes with preservation of still-valid content. Our key insight is to view 3D Gaussian primitives themselves as self-regulating continual learners. Based on this insight, we introduce GaussLearner, which equips each Gaussian with a closed continual-learning loop connecting observation, self-regulation, and adaptation at the primitive level. At each novel state, GaussLearner first attributes pixel-wise inconsistencies in current observations to their contributing Gaussians. Each Gaussian then self-regulates through a learning weight that balances current observations against cross-state reliability. This weight governs both Gaussian parameter adaptation and optimizer memory refresh. As current inconsistencies are resolved, the adaptation outcome renews each Gaussian's reliability for future states. The resulting field is a globally coupled population of locally self-regulating learners. Across scenes from CL-Splats, WAT, and our CL-3D dataset, GaussLearner improves average PSNR by 3.41 dB over the strongest baseline. On CL-3D, it achieves the best reconstruction quality on both changed and unchanged views.



### ACUS: An Angular Calibrated Uncertainty Score for Incomplete-View CT
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23752v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23752v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:06:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:06:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Zihan Deng, Chuanzhi Xu, Quankeng Huang, Yujie Liu, Zhenzhen Gong, Junjie Huang, Tianye Niu, Lequan Yu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Sparse-view and limited-arc computed tomography (CT) are commonly treated as projection completion: a network fills an unmeasured view, and the result is ranked by similarity to one ground-truth image. That score can be high even when the query angle is far from every acquired measurement, because a learned prior may invent anatomy the scan never constrained. Existing protocols therefore leave a basic question unanswered: how does predictive reliability change with distance from the acquired views? We propose the Angular Calibrated Uncertainty Score (ACUS) to answer that question. ACUS is the Winkler interval score of a nominal 90% predictive interval on held-out projections, binned by distance to the nearest acquired view. It charges interval width and adds a large miss penalty when a held-out pixel falls outside the interval, so a narrow band helps only when coverage holds. We use collapse on acquired angles as a necessary consistency check on the predictor. On chest and dental cone-beam orbits, interval width grows with the angular gap. Empirical coverage ranges from 77% to 92%, and the peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) of the point estimate falls as the width rises. Nearest-neighbor, interpolation, and point completion, each given one residual scale, have zero reported far coverage and incur outside-distance penalties. ACUS exposes gap-dependent overconfidence that a point-estimate PSNR alone does not measure.



### Detect What You Need: Chain-of-Causal Reasoning for 3D Intent Grounding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23754v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23754v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:09:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:09:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Zihao Zhang, Aming Wu, Yang Li, Yahong Han
- **Comment**: Accepted at NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Accurately matching human intentions in 3D space is an important goal of artificial intelligence. Recently, 3D Intention Grounding (3D-IG) has emerged, aiming to localize target 3D objects given a natural-language intent. Unlike conventional visual grounding with descriptive referring expressions, 3D-IG intents are abstract and non-descriptive, making object localization substantially more challenging. This requires inferring latent functional requirements from non-descriptive intents and aligning them with object-level 3D representations. However, existing methods largely rely on implicit intent-object matching, leading to logical gaps and limited interpretability, robustness, and generalization. To address these challenges, we propose Chain-of-Causal Reasoning (CoCR), a causality-inspired functional dependency reasoning framework that explicitly bridges abstract intentions and candidate objects through intermediate functional requirements. Specifically, CoCR progressively decomposes complex intents into ordered functional requirements, thereby forming an explicit intent-function-object reasoning chain that links abstract intentions to object suitability. Building on this chain, we construct a causality-inspired functional dependency graph to model requirement-attribute relationships and introduce a causal-visual alignment module that aligns function-aware representations with the geometric-semantic features of 3D point clouds, enabling bidirectional verification between structured functional reasoning and visual evidence. Extensive experiments on 3D Intention Grounding and 3D Visual Grounding demonstrate that our method enhances intent-aware object localization. Our code is available at https://2490o.github.io/CoCR.



### GramStatTexNet: Efficient, Interpretable, and Neuro-Inspired Texture Analysis-by-Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23755v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR, q-bio.NC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23755v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:09:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:09:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Vasha DuTell, Zuyu Yun, Anne Harrington, Michael Y-S Fang, Mark Hamilton, Christian Kövesdi, Edward H. Adelson, William T. Freeman, Ruth Rosenholtz
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deep networks achieve high realism in texture synthesis but often rely on opaque, over-parameterized representations that have limited direct connection to biological vision. Neuroscience-informed pyramid models are interpretable, compact, and biologically inspired, but rely on small, hand-curated statistics sets that are difficult to generalize or extend. We introduce GramStatTexNet, a hybrid analysis-by-synthesis framework that combines the multi-scale Gabor filter structure of classical models with the flexibility of Gramian-based correlations, organized into structured filter families. This structured representation enables analyses that are challenging for deep-feature methods: each statistic carries a named filter-family identity, allowing us to decompose synthesis fidelity into per-family contributions and to learn an interpretable, low-dimensional embedding that preserves perceptual texture content. We compare our framework to classical and deep texture-synthesis baselines across diverse texture categories, and on multiple perceptual metrics, highlighting the compactness and interpretability advantages of this hybrid representation, alongside competitive synthesis quality. This same framework extends naturally to peripheral textures via spatial pooling, and to dynamic textures using a spatiotemporal filter bank. GramStatTexNet provides a unified, interpretable framework for analyzing and modeling visual information with natural extensions across space, time, and eccentricity. Project page: https://vdutell.github.io/gstn



### Mean Curvature Minimizing Foliations Of A Vector Field
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23765v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23765v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:20:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:20:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Tabish A. Syed, Marc Theveneau, Benedikt Wirth, Rustum Choksi, Kaleem Siddiqi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Streamline patterns are ubiquitous in nature. Prominent examples include white matter fibre tracts in the mammalian brain and arrangements of elongated cells, such as cardiomyocytes in the heart wall. It has been posited that such organisations foliate into locally smooth surfaces to optimise function. However, the recovery of these foliations from a single vector field aligned with the local orientation of the underlying sheets remains an open problem. We address this challenge by introducing a mathematical model of a sheet and developing an algorithm for sheet inference, from measurements of the oriented structures. Given an input vector field, our model estimates a second vector field that is orthonormal to it, while also requiring the Lie bracket of the two to vanish. To balance sheet likelihood against low surface area, we also reduce the mean curvature of the recovered local surface. We compute the first variation of an associated energy and provide a numerical method which admits an efficient, parallel implementation for sheet estimation. We demonstrate our approach on both synthetic and biological examples, motivated by applications to medical imaging. These include white matter fibre tracts from a brain atlas and confocal microscopy images of cleared heart wall tissue. For the latter we are able to reconstruct sheets aligned with local cardiomyocyte directions at unprecedented (micrometre) scale.



### Keep the Reasoning, Read the Image: On-Demand Visual KV for Multi-Turn Reasoning VLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23769v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23769v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:24:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:24:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziyi Wang, Xiyue Zhang, Bing Hu, Guangmin Song, Chenyi Zhang, Li Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reasoning vision-language models (VLMs) think for thousands of tokens before they answer, and retention policies keep the KV cache affordable by pruning it as the reasoning grows. We find that this pruning sacrifices the image in multi-turn conversations: the image and the reasoning share one budget, so the growing reasoning crowds the visual entries out. A chart question that loses 8 points to the full cache when asked first loses 24 points when asked after a round of reasoning. Protecting a small visual reserve from eviction recovers most of this loss, while adding the same slots to the shared budget recovers considerably less. The image therefore lacks protection, not capacity. Based on this finding, we propose ShelfKV, a training-free method that works on top of existing retention policies. It keeps every visual entry in CPU memory and, at each decoding step, swaps the visual entries that the current query scores highest into the attention slots of the text entries it scores lowest. The image is never evicted, yet it occupies attention slots only while it is read. We evaluate ShelfKV on four benchmarks, three of them conversational, with two retention policies and two reasoning VLMs. In conversations, it improves the strongest existing policy, DBTrimKV, by up to 20.3 points at matched GPU memory, and still leads by 8.7 points when DBTrimKV uses twice the memory. It also consistently outperforms a fixed visual reserve of matched GPU memory in these settings, without hurting accuracy on standalone questions. These results show that the image should be protected from the reasoning but read only on demand.



### DetectViT: Test-time Backdoor Detection for Vision Transformers via Inter-Head Attention Discrepancy
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23770v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23770v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:26:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:26:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Siquan Huang, Yijiang Li, Xingfu Yan, Ningzhi Gao, Leyu Shi, Ying Gao
- **Comment**: Accepted to NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) now underpin the visual encoders of most multimodal models, but this success comes at a cost: relying on third-party pretrained checkpoints leaves these systems open to backdoor attacks. Existing test-time detection methods rely on either the predicted label or intermediate embeddings, limiting their applicability across diverse tasks and incurring substantial computational overhead. In this work, we study how backdoor triggers affect the multi-head attention mechanism of ViTs and identify two distinct anomalous patterns: $\textit{complete attention hijacking}$, causing the attention to exhibit abnormally high inter-head spatial overlap, and $\textit{partial attention hijacking}$, causing an abnormally large variance in the concentration of the attention distribution. Motivated by these findings, we propose $\textbf{DetectViT}$, a test-time backdoor detection method that requires neither training data, model outputs, nor any prior knowledge of the trigger. DetectViT quantifies the two hijacking phenomena via an inter-head consistency score and an inter-head entropy variance score, with thresholds estimated from a small out-of-distribution (OOD) calibration set drawn from an arbitrary source. Across four representative attacks, diverse architectures (DeiT, CLIP, and LLaVA), and downstream tasks (image classification and captioning), DetectViT substantially outperforms all baselines with as few as 64--128 OOD calibration samples, achieving a true positive rate of $100\%$ and a false positive rate of $0.17\%$ on BadCLIP. Notably, it introduces negligible additional overhead by relying solely on attention weights already computed during inference. Code is available at: https://github.com/siquanhuang/DetectViT.



### When accuracy hides risk: risk-controlled confirmation of the critical view of safety under expert disagreement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23781v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23781v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:37:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:37:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Bowen Xiang, Xiao Yu, Mingze Xu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Automated assessment of the critical view of safety (CVS) in laparoscopic cholecystectomy has advanced rapidly, but a deployed check must decide, before the first clip, whether to confirm CVS. The standard rule calibrates a threshold so that false confirmations are rare on average. We show that this rule concentrates its errors on disputed videos, which one of three expert raters judged sufficient, and that the failure is structural. When reference labels are votes of a panel sampled from a larger expert pool, the rule that maximizes sensitivity at a fixed average false-confirmation rate also maximizes false confirmation of disputed videos, and for recognizers that recover expert judgement with less noise this rate rises with accuracy. We propose certified confirmation, a risk-controlled monitoring rule whose threshold is selected by multiple testing so that, with probability at least 1 - δ, false confirmation is at most α overall, among disputed videos, within vendor and country groups, and before CVS is visible, using disputed labels only to calibrate. In a pre-specified, hash-locked evaluation on the 300 test videos (18 countries) of the SAGES CVS Challenge, standard calibration falsely confirmed 8 of 39 disputed videos (0.21; one-sided p = 0.037 against α = 0.10), whereas certified confirmation confirmed 2 (one-sided 95% upper bound 0.15). On development data, protection held for vendor and country groups left out of calibration. The cost was confirming 10% rather than 35% of sufficient videos on time. Average accuracy hides this risk; disputed cases must be protected explicitly.



### Geometry-bridge flow matching for blind rigid-motion correction in head cone-beam CT
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23788v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **physics.med-ph**, cs.CL, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23788v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:51:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:08:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Sungho Yun, Seungryong Cho
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Rigid motion of a patient head during head cone-beam CT (CBCT) scan can degrade image quality and complicate recovery of accurate anatomical structures from the measured projections. We present a 3D flow-matching prior approach that learns the image changes induced by motion. Its geometry-bridge connects motion-corrupted and motion-free Feldkamp-Davis-Kress (FDK) reconstructed images of the same source volume through progressively reduced simulated motion. The forward projector's geometry derivative supplies analytic velocity targets along this path. A plug-and-play loop encompasses the learned image updates together with spline-based pose estimation, conjugate-gradient data consistency, and image total-variation regularization. Both the refined image volume and the FDK reconstructed image volume at the estimated poses are evaluated. On simulated 360-view scans from 30 held-out CQ500 patients with nominal per-component motion amplitudes within 10 mm / 10 degrees, the framework achieved state-of-the-art image reconstruction quality and motion accuracy in comparison with other methos such as learned autofocus and diffusion-based joint reconstruction. Mean peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) reached 37.1 dB and mean detector-plane reprojection error (RPE) was 0.27 mm. With matched endpoints at the inference settings, the geometry bridge improved PSNR by 1.6 dB and reduced RPE by 33% relative to image-linear training. With 50 prior updates and coarse-to-fine motion estimation, total processing time averaged 9.1 minutes per patient on an RTX A6000.



### ROBIN++: Unified Copyright Protection and Tamper Localization for Diffusion Models via Dual-Domain Synergistic Watermarking
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23793v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23793v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 03:59:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 03:59:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Huayang Huang, Siqi Zeng, Qian Wang, Bo Du, Yu Wu
- **Comment**: 2026 TPAMI
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Digital watermarking provides a promising solution for verifying the provenance and integrity of generated content. However, existing methods often suffer from an inherent trade-off between copyright robustness and localization sensitivity due to the entanglement of conflicting signals within a single domain. In this paper, we propose ROBIN++, a paradigm that shifts from single-domain entanglement to a Dual-Domain Synergistic design. Our key insight is to explicitly decouple robustness and fragility during injection by assigning them to distinct domains, while re-coupling them for mutual reinforcement during verification. Specifically, we introduce an Adversarial Optimization Algorithm to inject a frequency-domain copyright watermark during generation, maximizing extraction robustness without visual artifacts. Complementarily, a Content-Aware Perturbation generator is integrated into the latent space to inject the spatial-domain localization watermark, ensuring sensitivity to spatial tampering with minimal interference to the copyright watermark. During verification, we propose a dual-branch synergistic detector that facilitates Frequency-to-Spatial Synergy, where robust spectral priors act as global anchors for local anomaly detection. Conversely, a Spatial-to-Frequency Refinement strategy is employed to rectify corrupted latent features using predicted tamper masks. Extensive experiments show that ROBIN++ significantly outperforms existing approaches in both copyright recovery and tamper localization accuracy, effectively mitigating the long-standing trade-off between the two tasks. Code is available at https://github.com/Hannah1102/ROBIN.



### Sightline: Long-Window Speculative Decoding for Fast SVG Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23807v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23807v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:12:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 04:12:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Yanchao Li, Ben Gao, Jiaqing Xie, Yusu Zhang, Beilun Wang, Yanbo Wang, Xiaohua Xu, Nanqing Dong, Tianfan Fu, Zhongyu Huang
- **Comment**: 45 pages, 23 figures, 34 tables. Code: https://github.com/LiZaiyuan0619/Sightline
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Scalable Vector Graphics (SVG) generation models write a graphic as code with one forward pass per token, so drawing is slow. Speculative decoding speeds them up losslessly, and block drafters guess many tokens per pass. On SVG, many of their rounds accept the whole draft and end only at the verification window. We find that their misses are rare and clumped, and that they stay accurate far deeper into a block than on natural language. A run-length identity and three propositions show that a longer window pays once the candidates are wide, up to the drafter's horizon. We propose Sightline, which pairs a window as long as the drafter's horizon with a draft tree wide enough to fill it. Experiments cover text-to-SVG and image-to-SVG with three models on six benchmarks from MMSVG, MMSVG-2M and SArena. In every setting, Sightline is the fastest of the compared lossless decoders. On SArena, it stays the fastest when every decoder shares one loop control. It also leads in almost every setting of six models from four other families, which write SVG code, compressed SVG tokens or Lottie JSON. Analyses trace the lead to rounds that shorter windows cut off, both in loop-free content and in loops. Speculative decoding also saves time for choosing samples by rendering, which beats choosing by likelihood. These results suggest that speculative decoding on vector graphics should verify as far as the drafter sees. Code and data are available at https://github.com/LiZaiyuan0619/Sightline.



### PReMind: When Personal Memory Meets Visual Hazards
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23808v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23808v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:13:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 04:13:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Yeongtak Oh, Donghyeok Lee, Joonhee Oh, Jaewon Jeong, Sungroh Yoon
- **Comment**: Project page: https://github.com/oyt9306/PReMind
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Personalized vision-language assistants use visual observations and personal memories to provide advice tailored to the user. When the observed scene contains a physical hazard, personal context, such as allergies, medical conditions, or mobility constraints, can change what help is appropriate. Responding appropriately therefore requires recognizing the visible hazard and applying the relevant person's information. We introduce PReMind-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark that evaluates this combination across diverse hazards and personal-memory conditions. It separately measures memory access, grounding, abstention, and hazard response, and assesses joint success on interdependency cases. Existing models often satisfy one requirement while failing the other, and training only for safety or personalization leaves a gap in joint success. We therefore propose PReMind, a two-phase reinforcement learning framework that combines on-demand and forced-call interactions (memory always retrieved) to jointly train grounded memory use and hazard response. At 4B, PReMind raises joint success from 12.9% to 28.6% over its base on the interdependency set and improves hazard adequacy and correct abstention on the main test. Joint-success gains persist under forced-call evaluation and under a second LLM judge across three Phase II seeds; averaged over these seeds, joint success also improves on unseen hazard groups. These findings highlight the need to evaluate and train personal assistants to connect visual evidence with the relevant person's context.



### SWIFT: Slow World, Instant Freshness, and Timely Action for Asynchronous Autonomous Driving
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23813v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23813v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:17:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 04:17:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiazhe Wang, Tianyi Yang, Ruihang Li, Fan Lu, Xianming Zeng, Jianyun Xu, Xingliang Liu, Lei Yang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Driving scenes keep changing during model computation, making both observation freshness and action-delivery time central to world-action planning. To retain rich generative world priors in autonomous driving without stalling real-time control, we present SWIFT (Slow World, Instant Freshness, and Timely action), a deployment-oriented asynchronous world-action system that moves world updates off the observation-to-action critical path. On a background GPU, a slow Video DiT streams surround-view history into read-only predictive world context, trained with a predictive span that covers both the update interval and background world computation. On a foreground GPU, a lightweight freshness carrier encodes current surround-view images into a gated key/value bank at every step, letting a fast Action DiT combine live visual feedback with the latest completed world context without waiting. On open-loop NAVSIM, SWIFT reaches 94.2 PDMS at 198.4 ms per decision with a trajectory scorer; on closed-loop Bench2Drive, it achieves an 80.30 Driving Score at 109.9 ms foreground latency. SWIFT further transfers to the Waymo E2E hidden test (8.0050 Rater Feedback Score) and nuScenes (0.54 m mean endpoint error, 0.03% mean collision rate). Across all four benchmarks, SWIFT achieves the best results among compared world-action methods, establishing a favorable accuracy-latency trade-off.



### Mapping the Design Space of Feature Sampling for Optical Flow
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23822v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23822v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:23:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:52:26+00:00
- **Authors**: Liping Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The computation of correlation features is crucial for optical flow estimation. Existing methods (e.g., RAFT) perform fixed-radius lookups on a cost volume, yielding a set of correlation features for each position; alternatively, WAFT performs single-point feature indexing, yielding one correlation feature vector per position. None of them designs a suitable correlation feature sampling scheme for each position according to regional characteristics. Our insight is that not every position requires the same sampling scheme. Applying an identical sampling scheme to every position is either redundant or insufficient and ignores the specific characteristics of the corresponding region. This paper rethinks the design of feature sampling and investigates the following question: should the support of feature sampling---i.e., the extent and directions over which evidence is aggregated---be learned separately for each query position? To study this question, we propose SAGE (Sampling with Anisotropic Gaussian Expectation). This method replaces single-point sampling with a per-pixel anisotropic Gaussian expectation on a fixed candidate grid centered on the current optical flow estimate. A positive-definite precision matrix controls the scale, anisotropy, and orientation of the sampling support, with single-point sampling included as its limiting case. Consequently, the sampling support becomes an explicit and analyzable design variable and provides a principled way to analyze how directional feature aggregation affects correspondence learning. Extensive experiments show that SAGE, while adding only 1.5% multiply-accumulate operations (MACs), effectively improves zero-shot cross-dataset generalization. When trained only on FlyingChairs and FlyingThings, SAGE reduces the end-point error (EPE) by 24.2% and 16.4% on Sintel clean and final, and reduces KITTI F1-epe and F1-all by 7.2% and 11.6%.



### TOGBench: Benchmarking and Improving Tiny-Object Generation Fidelity in Aerial Imagery
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23840v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23840v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:45:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 04:45:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Chanhwi Jeong, Dongmin You, Seungwook Kim
- **Comment**: Accepted to WACV 2027 Round 2. 20 pages, 7 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Advances in deep generative models now enable the synthesis of realistic imagery. However, aerial imagery is dominated by tiny objects, such as vehicles, ships, and aircraft, which occupy only a few pixels owing to the overhead, wide-area nature of aerial imaging. Existing generative models struggle substantially more with tiny objects than with larger ones. This limitation has been overlooked, as prior work on generative evaluation emphasizes prompt following or human preference rather than whether small objects are rendered faithfully. To address this gap, we introduce TOGBench, a quantitative, object-level benchmark for evaluating tiny-object generation fidelity in aerial imagery. We further propose two simple train-/inference-time techniques that improve the ability of layout-to-image generators to render small objects, raising their performance on TOGBench.



### ReflectScene:MLLM-Guided Closed-Loop Diffusion for 3D Indoor Scene Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23845v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23845v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 04:50:50+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:59:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiawei Shen, Jie Feng, Junpeng Zhang, Guanbin Li, Wanyi Chen, Weisheng Dong, Guangming Shi
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 3 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-conditioned 3D indoor scene generation requires layouts satisfying geometric, functional, and semantic constraints. However, existing diffusion methods rely primarily on static textual conditions, limiting their ability to assess evolving scenes and translate semantic discrepancies into executable object-level corrections. MLLM-based post-hoc refinement provides semantic feedback but remains decoupled from denoising, potentially disrupting scene coherence. We propose ReflectScene, an MLLM-guided closed-loop diffusion framework. First, we introduce Hierarchical Prior-Grounded Scene Understanding that aligns structured layouts with multi-view visual evidence, to interpret object-specific context and scene global semantics via a frozen multi-modal large language model (MLLM). Second, we propose MLLM-Guided Semantic Reward Evaluation to distill MLLM's diagnoses and assessments on possible candidate corrections into a lightweight Reward Predictor, enabling correction decision making with reduced computation burden. Finally, Decision-Conditioned Trajectory-Consistent Diffusion is proposed to integrate selected corrections through transient conditioning and persistent clean-estimate projection, correcting object layout in the generated scene while preserving non-target arrangements throughout denoising. Experiments on 3D-FRONT demonstrate improved functional consistency and semantic alignment while maintaining competitive generation quality and diversity.



### RoboBuilder: Agentic Robot Co-Design with Real-world Video Feedback in the Loop
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23855v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23855v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:02:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:02:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Ghadi Nehme, Dahyun Nam, Akshay Govind Srinivasan, Faez Ahmed
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Can AI agents design robots that work in the real world? We introduce RoboBuilder, a benchmark in which coding agents co-design a robot's hardware and program from standard parts, motors and a custom 3D-printed part, and every design that passes in simulation is built and tested on a physical course. Simulation alone is not enough: under public part data, none of 15 initial passing designs crossed the real course, so an agent learns the simulator from measurements it requests and videos of real runs. Across four frontier coding agents, ten obstacles at three difficulty levels, 16,427 simulations of 12,774 distinct robots and 135 physical robots built in an estimated 240 hours, agents solve nearly all easy obstacles and invent mechanisms our search baselines do not reach, but falter at harder levels, when one robot must cross every obstacle, and in the real world, where 66% of simulated passes hold across all levels. Closing the loop helps: shown a video of its real failure, an agent redesigns the robot, sometimes correcting its simulator, and removes 72% of level-1 and level-2 failures. RoboBuilder measures progress toward agents that engineer real machines. Project Page: https://ghadinehme.com/robobuilder.github.io/



### Temporal Image Representations for Mouse Dynamics Authentication
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23859v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23859v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:04:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:04:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Junyi Li, Charles Devlen, Daqing Hou
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 5 figures, 5 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Mouse dynamics authentication has traditionally relied on handcrafted features or time-series representations that capture both spatial and temporal characteristics of mouse movements. More recent approaches have modeled mouse data as images, enabling the use of powerful pretrained computer vision models. However, prior work primarily focuses on spatial-only representations where the cursor coordinates are plotted as a trajectory image. These representations discard the temporal characteristics which can help discriminate users. In this work, we propose image representations that incorporate speed and velocity information for mouse dynamics authentication. We investigate two mouse data representations, a XYPlot representation that intuitively shows the path of movement, and a Pairwise Distance (PWD) representation that utilizes the entire image canvas to encode relationships between mouse events. Both representations are evaluated under both location-aware and location-free settings using pretrained 2D-CNN and ViT architectures. We found that the incorporation of temporal information improved performance over spatial representations alone, and that the location-aware variants generally outperformed the location-free. We achieve state-of-the-art performance on two datasets, Balabit and TWOS, and extend our evaluation to two other datasets. We found that in general, location-aware PWD with temporal encoding combined with the 2D-CNN architecture provided the best results, with mean EERs of 5.21% and 12.70% on the Balabit Test-1 and Test-2 sets, respectively, 4.94% on TWOS, 6.16% on ChaoShen, and 0.59% on DFL.



### MobileWeave: Program-First Synthesis of Dependency-Linked Tasks for Training Mobile GUI Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23860v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23860v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:04:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:04:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziyi He, Xian Wu, Qiyuan Chen, Haitao Li, Cehao Yang, Yinghao Zhu, Xichen Zhang, Xiao Chen, Yushi Feng, Lequan Yu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Native mobile GUI agents require large-scale training trajectories covering both individual app functions and multi-goal workflows within and across apps, which makes automatic task synthesis a promising way to scale training data. Existing synthesis pipelines, however, produce each task as free-form text, leaving implicit how its subgoals depend on one another. This matters because composition is a skill in its own right: a policy may complete each subtask of a workflow on its own, yet fail to chain these subtasks into the complete workflow. We present MobileWeave, which instead synthesizes every task as an executable program derived from the app implementations of a controllable simulator. MobileWeave abstracts each app into a typed capability graph with semantic types shared across apps, composes atomic programs into single- and cross-app composite programs through explicit value and object dependencies, and verifies each program by native execution, deriving both its instruction and its completion check from the same program. Trained on teacher trajectories collected for these tasks and on recovery supervision at the policy's own stalls, MobileWeave-8B improves its base model by 26.7 points on MobileGym and by 8.6, 21.3, and 17.6 points on AndroidWorld, AndroidLab, and MobileWorld, with comparable gains at 4B. Beyond these gains, we find that composition is learnable from task structure: explicit dependencies between subgoals act as a training signal that teaches a policy to compose skills it already masters. We release data, model, and code at https://maxxnotmarx.github.io/MobileWeave/ .

- **Authors**: Mohammed Baharoon, Mahmoud Hussain Alabbad, Sung Eun Kim, Xiaoman Zhang, Lucas Bijnens, Zirong Chen, Jiayu Wang, Xi Ouyang, Mingyang Yu, Weiqiu Jin, Xiaodan Ye, Dijia Wu, Kwang-Hyun Uhm, Inhwa Son, Rachel van 't Hull, Tim Veenboer, Cees Snoek, Kevin Groot Lipman, Daesung Kim, Junha Im, Youngjun Jun, Sangwon Lee, Katelyn Morrison, Adam Perer, Mijin Yun, Ali Piracha, Reem Alwasiah, Talal Yahyan, Abdulrahman Alamoudi, Mashael Alshehri, Rayan Alsager, Hannah Hancock, Anna M. Hunkemöller, Peter Youssef, Mona A. Alhammad, Afnan Aljumayan, Brecht Van Berkel, Eric Bijnens, Saud Alhussain, Maarten Steyvers, Rolf Symons, Bryan Eelen, Ayed Alaklubi, Maha Buraykan Alshammari, Amirah Fahad Alshammeri, Tomas Gilvydis, Ibrahim AlTurki, Monerah Almohideb, Ali Alburkani, Shahad Alhomaiani, Fatima Jabali, Maximilian Rokuss, Moritz Langenberg, Yannick Kirchhoff, Benjamin Hamm, Klaus Maier-Hein, Ibrahim Ethem Hamamci, Sezgin Er, Michael Moritz, Hassan R. Alomaish, Luyang Luo, Pranav Rajpurkar
- **Comment**: 25 pages, 13 figures, 4 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Segmenting each reported finding in a radiology scan would allow findings to be measured and tracked over time and let AI systems generate reports that are verifiable, but so far AI models have performed poorly at this task. To advance this task, we organized the ReXGrounding Challenge at MICCAI 2026, the first challenge dedicated to it. Given a non-contrast chest CT scan and the set of findings from its report, participants trained models to produce the corresponding set of 3D segmentations. The challenge used 2,992 training and 200 validation scans from the ReXGroundingCT dataset, and evaluated methods on a held-out test set of 300 scans with 582 findings, exhaustively annotated by board-certified radiologists. In total, 48 teams from 15 countries made 760 submissions, and the best method reached a Dice of 0.375 and a hit rate of 0.77, matching agreement between two independent radiologist groups (Dice 0.32, hit rate 0.76, on a 50-scan subset). On the 100 scans of the pre-challenge benchmark, the winner reached a Dice of 0.390, against 0.285 for VoxTell, the best model before the challenge, which five of the six best teams fine-tuned. Non-focal findings remained far harder to segment than focal ones, instance F1 stayed below inter-reader agreement (0.34 vs. 0.47), and models sometimes segmented the right abnormality in the wrong location. Top methods thus match radiologist agreement in Dice, but non-focal findings, findings with multiple instances, and adherence to the anatomical location described in the text remain open problems.



### On Demand, Not Uniformity: Adaptive Expert Orchestration for Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23865v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23865v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:13:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:13:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Mengxin Qin, Kun Wei, Weitao An, Jiahua Li, Xu Yang, Cheng Deng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning (MCIT) enables Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to adapt to sequential instruction data while retaining previously acquired knowledge. While recent Mixture-of-Experts (MoE)-based methods mitigate forgetting by freezing historical experts and introducing new ones, they largely overlook the varying adaptation demands across tasks. Probing incoming tasks reveals pronounced heterogeneity in layer-wise gradient pressure and token-wise prediction difficulty. This observation highlights two unresolved decisions: where additional capacity is needed and how strongly new experts should contribute to individual tokens. To address both questions, we propose the Coverage-Calibrated Expert Injection and Residual Arbitration framework (CIRA-MoE), which derives expert acceptance margins via learnable threshold routing to serve as a unified coverage signal. Specifically, historical acceptance evidence guides the selective injection of new experts into insufficiently covered layers. Meanwhile, relative evidence between historical and current experts modulates token-wise residual contributions, coupled with arbitration-consistent regularization to promote expert specialization. Comprehensive experiments on UCIT and CoIN demonstrate that CIRA-MoE outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods in MCIT.



### HOForce: Motion and Force Reconstruction of Hand-Object Interaction Using a Single-View RGB-D Video
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23871v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23871v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:23:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:23:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiahui Wang, Wanqing Du, Yuanbin Zou, Zhefeng Xu, Feng Xu
- **Comment**: Includes supplementary material. Project page: https://huggingface.co/spaces/fnoi16wjhui/HOForce
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reconstructing hand-object interactions is an important task, and achieving it with a single camera makes large-scale capture practical but challenging due to heavy occlusions. We introduce HOForce to track hands and objects, and further recover interaction forces from single-view RGB-D video and known object properties. For two-hand manipulation, we jointly model fingertip forces of both hands and the contact forces between objects when two objects are presented. The reconstructed forces also improve motion tracking by giving pose constraints in the occluded regions. HOForce recovers hand and object motion more accurately on DexYCB and TACO and produces less hand-object penetration than interaction baselines on DexYCB. On our dataset of fingertip forces recorded with tactile sensors, HOForce reduces normal-force MAE by 24.5% relative to the strongest baseline. The recovered forces reproduce object motion in MuJoCo and transfer to other engines after adaptation.



### Keep the Image in Mind: A Visual Board for Multimodal Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23872v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23872v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:24:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:24:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Haiming Zhu, Zhiwei Chen, Ding Cao, Tianxiang Zhao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-step visual reasoning suffers when early visual misinterpretations persist as incorrect premises in subsequent reasoning. We argue that how visual evidence is represented during reasoning matters as much as how much of it is gathered. Rather than appending observations and inferences to an ever-growing dialogue, we introduce a Visual Board-centered harness that separates visual observations, uncertain interpretations, and derived reasoning within a structured, capacity-bounded, and revisable workspace. This separation makes each premise explicit and auditable, so that conflicts between what is seen and what is derived become detectable even by a text-only critic and can trigger targeted reinspection of the original image. Without task-specific training, our harness consistently outperforms standalone models across 4B, 8B, Qwen3-VL-Flash, and 32B configurations on Euclid30K, SolidGeo, and ChartQA bar charts, with gains of up to 11.68, 18.42, and 24.82 percentage points, respectively. Under identical tools, critic, and interaction limits, the structured Board outperforms shared free-form notes by 6.67 to 13.00 points and no-Board retries by 7.35 to 18.80 points while using 7.9\% to 38.2\% fewer tokens. Supervised fine-tuning on harness trajectories further improves direct answering and critic-free tool use, with positive transfer to chart reasoning.



### NullTok: empirical-null token selection for training-free long video understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23881v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23881v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:32:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:32:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhang Ziyue, Juwei Jian, Baiyang Song, Yunshan Zhong, Jun Peng, Yiyi Zhou, Rongrong Ji
- **Comment**: 8 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long video understanding remains a daunting challenge for existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs), mainly due to the large number of visual tokens accumulated in long videos. Currently, frame selection and token selection are two main paradigms for addressing this issue, both aiming to select relevant visual information to form a compact input for MLLMs. A natural approach combines these two paradigms by selecting frames before pruning tokens, but noisy frame-level relevance estimates can cause irreversible evidence loss. In this paper, we study this problem from the perspective of assessing the spatiotemporal distribution of query-specific evidence and propose a novel and training-free approach termed NULLTOK. For every patch across all frames, we construct an empirical-null distribution from 64 group-matched irrelevant control queries, capturing the scores expected in the absence of target-specific evidence. Target affinities are calibrated against these same-position references into right-tail Monte Carlo p-values, which are discrete super-uniform under a conditional exchangeability assumption. Calibrated screening and a high-recall semantic guard produce candidate tokens for deterministic visual deduplication and joint spatiotemporal budget allocation. To validate NullTok, we apply it to two representative MLLMs and conduct extensive experiments on a set of video benchmarks. The experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness under a bounded visual token budget. For example, it achieves 65.81% and 68.85% on Video-MME using frozen LLaVA-Video-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B backbones, respectively, at a cap of 64 full-frame equivalents (FFE), without subtitles or audio. These results outperform all evaluated baselines on Video-MME under the same budget for both backbones. Code is available at https://github.com/potato-kitty/NullTok.



### Can AI Understand Indonesian Children's Book Illustrations? Compliance, Safety, and the Cultural Alignment Gap
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23887v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23887v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:40:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:40:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Rian Adam Rajagede, Rochana Prih Hastuti, Fitri Hasanah Amhar
- **Comment**: Accepted at Workshop on Computer Vision for Children (CV4CHL) at CVPR 2026 (Non-Proceeding Track)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Children's book illustrations carry developmental, pedagogical, and cultural responsibilities that are often formally codified in guidelines. As AI systems begin entering children's publishing pipelines, both as evaluators and as generators, a natural question arises: can they reliably meet the structural, safety, and cultural standards required? We investigate this across three criteria: guideline compliance, safety, and cultural alignment, using Indonesia's Book Leveling Guideline as a government-issued evaluation framework with explicit visual criteria for four reading levels. To understand how well VLMs can identify and apply these guideline criteria, we first evaluate them on real published children's book pages and compare their assessments against a human baseline. To then examine whether AI-generated illustrations meet the same standards, we apply the VLM judge to images produced by two generation models and cross-check them against a subset of human illustrator raters. We find that VLMs are reliable on explicit criteria such as compliance and safety, but fall short on cultural alignment, systematically missing content that human raters flag as culturally inappropriate. AI-generated illustrations show the same blind spot: they satisfy structural and safety criteria but fail to capture implicit cultural details that human raters consistently identify.



### Beyond Additive Composition: Interventional Modeling of State-Dependent Cross-Degradation Responses for Composite Image Restoration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23889v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23889v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:41:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:41:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Zongda Tang, Jie Feng, Wenda Zhao, Mingtao Feng, Yuping Liang, Ronghua Shang, Weisheng Dong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Composite image restoration aims to recover images corrupted by multiple coexisting degradations. Existing methods predominantly decompose composite corruptions into individual factors, relying on these isolated priors to guide restoration. However, coexisting degradations are inherently interdependent: the manifestation of one degradation dynamically shifts with the states of others, inducing non-additive interactions that factor-wise paradigms fail to capture. To bridge this gap, we formulate state-dependent cross-degradation responses and propose PROBE, an interventional framework that explicitly models how coexisting degradations influence one another. Specifically, we first construct unified degradation axes by aligning visual and textual degraded-clean offsets. Along these axes, a calibrated coordinate head estimates current degradation states, while bidirectional feature perturbations are injected to probe cross-coordinate responses under the current states. Contrasting these responses with corresponding intensity-matched single-degradation references quantifies intrinsic non-additive deviations, allowing the resulting response descriptors and state coordinates to jointly guide restoration. For comprehensive evaluation, we introduce SCD-15, a new benchmark covering all 15 nonempty combinations of four degradation factors at three severity levels. Experiments demonstrate that PROBE achieves state-of-the-art performance on the CDD-11, AWMM-100K, and SCD-15 composite restoration datasets using a compact model with modest GPU memory requirements.



### SixthView: A Benchmark for Hidden-View Reconstruction and 3D Assembly Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23891v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23891v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:42:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:42:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Pooya Khosravi, Jen-Feng Chang, Fnu Pramono
- **Comment**: 13 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Inferring the unseen side of a 3D assembly requires reasoning about part placement and occlusion. Different assemblies can match the observed views while producing different hidden views, leaving the target ambiguous. We introduce SixthView, a benchmark in which models predict a sixth orthographic view from five observed views and information about the component shapes. The benchmark contains 602 synthetic polycube assemblies with 2--10 parts and requires a dense grid of part labels for the missing view, allowing both exact-view and cell-level evaluation. For each item, we exhaustively verify that all legal assemblies consistent with the exact component geometry and observed label grids yield the same target view. With these inputs, the task tests symbolic assembly and coordinate reasoning. The image-only variants additionally require shape recovery from rendered views, where occlusion can make different 3D shapes look identical. We evaluate three vision-language models under four input conditions. Across 552 items with complete evaluations, exact-grid accuracy with images plus exact text ranges from 33.2% to 85.4%. Adding exact text to separate images improves accuracy by 19.7--63.0 percentage points, whereas adding images to exact text changes it by only $-0.5$ to $+2.1$ points. Separate image panels outperform a merged sheet by 6.7--11.1 points. The results show that performance depends strongly on access to exact symbolic information, with little further improvement from images once the geometry is specified. GitHub: https://github.com/facebookresearch/sixthview_benchmark



### Coverage Governs Accuracy: When to Expand the Lexicon and When to Fall Back in Low-Resource Manchu OCR
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23900v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CL**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23900v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:52:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:52:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Ruixin Yu, Zifan Wang, Mingxin Ding, Xiaoxin Sun, Dongjie Wang, Suhua Wang, Minghao Yin
