- **Comment**: 15 pages, 11 figures, 6 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Character-level OCR for low-resource Manchu often produces near-miss spelling errors. Lexicon-constrained decoding can correct such errors with lexical knowledge, but a fixed dictionary cannot cover the many unseen word forms produced by agglutinative word formation. We study whether morphological expansion alleviates this limitation, and how the expansion size and confidence fallback affect recognition. We segment the training transcriptions with a dictionary-based stem list, extract roots and suffix chains from them, generate about 510k candidates, select the expansion size through frequency ranking on the validation set, and decompose accuracy under hard lexicon constraints into the product of coverage and in-lexicon precision. Across three CRNN training seeds, adding 250k candidates to a base dictionary of about 29k words raises the type accuracy on unseen combinations of seen roots from 63.6% to 86.3%, and overall token accuracy from 82.4% to 84.2%. Adding all candidates lowers overall token accuracy to 81.5%, showing that the coverage gain comes with a loss of in-lexicon precision. A further comparison shows that the base dictionary with confidence fallback performs best overall among the CRNN systems tested, reaching 76.1% type and 85.5% token accuracy and outperforming the expanded lexicon both with and without fallback. A Transformer recognizer shows similar trends. These results indicate that morphological expansion relieves the constraint of a fixed dictionary, but a larger lexicon does not guarantee better recognition; in the present low-resource Manchu OCR setting, a selective combination of reliable lexical constraints and open recognition is more effective.



### Training-Free Domain Generation for One-Shot Federated Learning with Server-Side Pre-trained Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23908v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.DC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23908v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 05:59:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:59:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Yixuan Tong, Yuanguo Bi, Mingjian Zhi, Wenchao Xu, Yuan Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: One-shot federated learning (OSFL) has attracted increasing attention for its ability to substantially reduce communication overhead by limiting client-server interaction to a single communication round. Nevertheless, this limited interaction makes learning from heterogeneous client distributions challenging. Diffusion-based OSFL approaches seek to address this challenge by synthesizing data that approximate client distributions. However, existing diffusion-based approaches often impose substantial computational costs on clients and lack effective visual guidance to generate data aligned with client distributions. To address these limitations, we propose FedDoG, a training-free Domain Generation framework that leverages server-side pre-trained diffusion models to synthesize data aligned with client domains. FedDoG extracts compact domain representations using a frozen image encoder, substantially reducing client-side computational and communication costs. These representations are incorporated into the diffusion model through a pre-trained image-conditioning adapter, thereby providing explicit visual guidance for domain generation. To further improve the quality of synthetic data, FedDoG adapts the strength of visual conditioning according to domain concentration and filters generated samples based on class consistency and predictive entropy. Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate that FedDoG generates higher-quality synthetic images and outperforms existing OSFL baselines in classification accuracy. Meanwhile, FedDoG reduces client upload and computation time by 99.9% and 91.5%, respectively. The code is available at https://github.com/YXTong17/FedDoG.



### ProxySearch: Decoupled Inference-Time Scaling for Diffusion Models via Asymmetric Noise-Rank Transfer
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23929v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23929v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:20:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:20:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Seungwook Kim, Jongmin Lee
- **Comment**: Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. 26 pages, 7 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Noise search at inference time improves text-to-image diffusion but requires many multi-step teacher rollouts per query. Distilled few-step models are now routinely released, yet their potential as proxies inside noise search is uncharacterized. We measure proxy-teacher rank agreement across three distillation regimes and three backbones (DiT, MMDiT, UNet) and find a sharp asymmetry. On our main pool, closed-form image statistics transfer from the proxy to the teacher (Spearman $ρ$ up to 0.80), while learned semantic scorers do not ($ρ\le$ 0.12). Paired null controls trace this rank to the first denoising step for coarse statistics and to distillation for finer ones. We use the asymmetry for noise search under a joint constraint: a semantic reward specifies content, and a numeric target specifies image statistics. In PROXYSEARCH, the proxy filters candidates by statistical match in 1 step, and the teacher returns the survivor with the highest semantic reward. No weight trades one objective against the other. On FLUX-1024, PROXYSEARCH tightens the statistical target by 21-23% at the same number of teacher trajectories, with no detected loss on the optimized reward.



### TRoPE: Topology-aware Relative Positional Encoding for 4D Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23933v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23933v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:23:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:23:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Chengzhuo Gui, Zhenglin Zhou, Fan Ma, Zongxin Yang, Yi Yang
- **Comment**: Project page: https://akui1321.github.io/TRoPE
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video-to-4D mesh generation has made substantial progress in recovering dynamic 3D geometry and motion from monocular videos. However, commonly used feed-forward approaches remain prone to sticking artifacts, where geodesically distant vertices move together incorrectly when they become spatially close. We attribute this failure to cross-part motion leakage: spatially nearby yet structurally distant vertices may retrieve similar motion information when motion decoding fails to distinguish spatial proximity from mesh connectivity. To address this issue, we introduce TRoPE, a topology-aware relative positional encoding that makes mesh connectivity explicit in the interaction between vertex queries and mesh-supported motion sources. TRoPE derives spectral features from the graph Laplacian and constructs pairwise topological relations from distances between multi-scale diffusion features, using these relations to modulate vertex--anchor attention through rotary phases. We further introduce a vertex-wise tail-risk objective that emphasizes persistently high-error vertices to explicitly suppress sticking artifacts. To better characterize and quantify these failures, we introduce MeshSticking-4D, a dedicated benchmark for this failure mode. Experiments on MeshSticking-4D show that TRoPE suppresses sticking artifacts while improving overall motion accuracy. Compared with the baseline, it reduces the worst-vertex error by 39.4% and lowers the separation failure rate from 49.35% to 34.90%. Additional evaluations on Motion-80 and Motion324-Subset show lower tail errors on diverse objects, while transfer to Mesh4D demonstrates applicability across backbones.



### What Right-Angle Corners Reveal About an Uncalibrated Camera: Convex Cones, Certified Bounds and Identifiability
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23934v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, math.AG, math.MG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23934v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:23:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:23:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Michael Werman
- **Comment**: 26 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The corner of a box or a room, where three perpendicular edges meet, appears in an image as three segments meeting at a point. We ask what such junctions reveal about an uncalibrated camera's intrinsic parameters. Each junction is consistent with an explicit convex set of cameras; when only the focal length is unknown, this set is an interval in closed form. These sets are computed from the image alone, so calibrating from many junctions is a convex feasibility problem. Almost every junction bounds the focal length from below; we characterize when one also bounds it from above, and when many junctions determine it exactly. For a known vertex, the bounds can be certified against any bounded error in the measured edge directions.



### Facet-SLAM: Real-Time Triangle Splatting SLAM
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23935v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23935v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:23:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:23:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Edward Beng Wai Tan, Siew-Kei Lam
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Dense SLAM systems based on 3D Gaussian splatting build maps that render photorealistically, but their surface geometry is poor, and obtaining a mesh requires extraction or fusion stages. Triangle splatting avoids this with surface primitives whose vertices can be triangulated directly into a connected mesh, yet SLAM with this representation has so far relied on photometric tracking, which is too slow for localization at sensor rate. We present Facet-SLAM, the first real-time triangle splatting SLAM system for RGB-D cameras. Fast geometric registration is the natural alternative, but it is not sufficient alone, since the mapper's optimization displaces triangles from the observed surface and makes them unreliable registration targets. We therefore separate the two roles: the tracker sets each triangle's centroid from fused depth and registers frames to these centroids by GICP, while the mapper is restricted to non-translating updates via a change of variables. Our system achieves competitive tracking accuracy at around 100 FPS on a desktop GPU and runs at sensor rate on a Jetson AGX Orin. Compared with the photometric approach, it is over 20 times faster, matches rendering quality, and improves surface accuracy, reducing the chamfer distance of the Delaunay mesh on Replica from 1.55 to 0.86 cm. We will release the implementation at https://github.com/edward62740/Facet-SLAM.



### Angular Conditional Distillation: Overcoming Classifier Geometry Mismatch
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23941v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23941v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:28:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:28:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuefei Zhang, Ruoqi Wang, Lei Cui
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Classifier outputs provide a fundamental and univer- sally available knowledge interface for knowledge distil- lation (KD). However, standard logit objectives entangle transferable semantic relations with architecture-dependent parameterization artifacts, causing a classifier geometry mismatch that makes direct knowledge transfer unreliable. To address this, we propose Angular Conditional Distilla- tion (ACD). First, the Angular Coordinate Interface (ACI) reconstructs the student classifier using angular coordi- nates, suppressing magnitude and bias effects to provide a geometry-compatible space. Then, Scale-Invariant Condi- tional Distillation (SICD) standardizes outputs and exclu- sively transfers conditional non-target relations, filtering out untransferable absolute confidence. Together, ACI de- fines where to transfer, while SICD defines what to transfer. Extensive experiments show that ACD achieves state-of-the- art performance over existing KD methods. Moreover, as a universal drop-in replacement, it consistently improves existing distillation pipelines while requiring no feature adapters or additional inference-time cost. Code is avail- able at https://github.com/laniusnan/ACD.



### SIGNTOK: A Text-Aligned Continuous Pose Tokenizer for Sign Language Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23944v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23944v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:30:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:30:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Nabeela Khan, Gaurav Rai, Bowen Wu, Sihan Tan, Benjamin Yen, Takeshi Ashizawa, Kazuhiro Nakadai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-conditioned sign language generation (SLG) depends not only on the generator but also on the space in which signing is generated. Existing approaches generate directly in pose space, in quantized code spaces, or in learned latent spaces trained for reconstruction. We find that these spaces are organized largely by signer identity rather than linguistic content: on CSL-Daily, a test clip's nearest neighbor in raw pose space is the same sentence signed by another person in only 2% of cases. To address this, we propose SIGNTOK, a continuous pose tokenizer whose latent space is shaped by three training-only heads: a kinematics head that predicts motion dynamics, a sentence-level head that aligns signing with the overall text, and a word-level head that aligns short motion windows with content words. Together, these objectives raise the same-sentence cross-signer nearest-neighbor rate to 82%. On three sign language benchmarks, the same generator trained in the SIGNTOK latent instead of pose space raises back-translation BLEU-4 in five of six benchmark-scorer settings at comparable joint accuracy, and word-level alignment gives higher back-translation scores than sentence-level alignment on every benchmark.



### How Complete Must an Environment Be to Train a Capable GUI Agent?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23946v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23946v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:32:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:32:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Jingxing Wang, Teng Wang, Xingyu Lou, Zihan Guo, Jun Wang, Weiwen Liu, Weinan Zhang, Jianghao Lin
- **Comment**: 15pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Graphical user interface (GUI) agents are widely trained in executable GUI environments. To lower cost, build environments in batches, and reduce manual effort, each work trades away part of the environment through its construction choices. We describe these choices along three dimensions: reward coverage, the goal-reaching trajectories the verifier rewards; data coverage, the application data the agent is exposed to beyond what the task reads or writes; and feature coverage, the application features beyond what the task uses. Existing works set each dimension on engineering grounds, but they do not measure its effect on training. How complete must an environment be to train a capable agent, and along which dimensions? In MobileGym, we adjust each dimension separately, train Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct, and evaluate every policy in the original full environment. A verifier that rewards only one reference path lowers success by 10.53 points, and removing unneeded records also lowers success. Pruning unneeded features instead raises success from 51.61% to 56.13%, entirely on tasks whose environments it left unchanged. The gain reverses when each episode is weighted equally and vanishes when only episodes on the pruned tasks are balanced. Without pruning, length weighting there reinforces long detour successes that erode skills on other tasks. Following these findings, we build MobileGym-Lite, which keeps reward and data coverage complete and prunes unneeded features. It outperforms Full in the full environment (55.48% versus 51.61% success) with lower construction cost.



### VOL-Agent: Programming Your Film via Video Orchestration Language Agentic System
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23950v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23950v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:39:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:39:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongji Yang, Yucheng Zhou, Lingting Zhu, Shengju Qian, Xinye Li, Huan Zheng, Dongyue Lu, Wei Chow, Xin Wang, Cheng-zhong Xu, Jianbing Shen
- **Comment**: 14 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent video foundation models generate high-quality and limited-length clips, but orchestrating them into minute-level, story-coherent films remains an open problem. Existing video agents highly rely on natural-language planning or predefined workflows, which make their production hard to program, customize, and extend. Therefore, we introduce VOL-Agent, a programmable video agent built on a Video Orchestration Language (VOL). With static compilation and verification, followed by runtime dynamic loading, VOL-Agent decouples structural reliability from execution flexibility. Through the skill-driven executor, VOL-Agent dynamically resolves tools and skills from an open-world repository and supports user-defined skills in natural language. Moreover, VOL-Agent is capable of crafting new director skills from learned knowledge and improves itself through feedback to accumulate filmmaking knowledge. To overcome the disconnect between different segments, we apply a structural memory mechanism to record and recall world and entity states, sustaining entity and scene consistency across different shots. Critic feedback supports local revision, while reusable filmmaking knowledge and experiences can be retained for subsequent tasks. Experiments on three benchmarks show that VOL-Agent generates minute-scale videos with strong overall quality and limited cost.



### MitoWorld: A Stochastic World Model of the Single-Cell Cycle under Genetic Perturbation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23953v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, q-bio.QM
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23953v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:42:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:42:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Ting Long, Xiaojing Sun, Cheng Han, Zhicheng Ji, Yingnian Wu, Jiebo Luo, Heng Fan, Dongfang Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A cell observed after a gene knockdown has an uncertain future, both in how its appearance will evolve and when it will divide. Existing approaches predict endpoints or population-level changes without jointly modeling individual trajectories and division timing. In this work, we present MitoWorld, a stochastic world model that generates these future appearances as continuous evolution in a Gaussian reference latent, terminated by a division hazard and converted into images by a conditional diffusion renderer. The ordinary transition provably preserves its Gaussian reference, tying its noise to its dynamics; paired observations identify its discrete transition where stationary marginals cannot. The hazard turns division timing into a survival likelihood that can be tested against real cycle lengths. On 325 held-out MitoCheck tracks spanning 17 knockdowns and a control, MitoWorld lowers FID by 34.6\% and the video metric JEDi by 45.6\% relative to the strongest baselines. Its latent-space prediction intervals achieve near-nominal 90\% coverage, are 35\% narrower than those of a model with freely fitted noise. The hazard also yields cell-cycle length distributions with KS distance close to the model-based finite-sample reference. Testable single-cell stochastic dynamics thus offer a generative tool for time-lapse perturbation screens.



### SimplePro: A Simple Baseline for Proactive Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23954v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23954v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:42:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:42:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Yilin Wang, Xiangxi Zheng, Dongxing Mao, Linjie Li, Zhengyuan Yang, Alex Jinpeng Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Proactive video understanding requires vision-language models (VLMs) to decide not only what to answer as a video unfolds, but also when newly arrived evidence warrants a response and when to remain silent. Although modern VLMs excel at offline long-video understanding, they struggle in proactive streaming settings, leading prior work to introduce task-specific reinforcement learning or auxiliary triggering modules. We find that explicit reporting rules and structured streaming context substantially improve a frozen VLM's proactive response performance. We exploit this observation to build SIMPLEPRO, a training-free baseline for proactive video understanding. At each decision step, the model combines recent high-resolution frames, bounded historical observations, and prior answer records to determine whether new information warrants a response and, when needed, generate a concise answer. The system then filters consecutive duplicate answers while retaining the raw outputs as context for subsequent decisions. Without any parameter updates or auxiliary triggers, SIMPLEPRO with a 4B backbone consistently outperforms specialized proactive systems across OVO-Bench, StreamingBench-PO, and ProactiveVideoQA. SIMPLEPRO shows overall performance gains as backbone size increases from 2B to 32B. We hope this simple yet effective method will serve as a strong baseline for proactive video understanding, and we will release our code and inference pipeline to support future research.



### DORMS: Image Segmentation via Depth-Ordered Region Merge Trees
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23958v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23958v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:47:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:28:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Furkan Mumcu, Yasin Yilmaz
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We introduce DORMS (Depth-Ordered Region Merging for Segmentation), a purely algorithmic approach to image segmentation from monocular depth maps, requiring no neural networks or learned parameters in the segmentation process. An off-the-shelf monocular depth estimator provides the input depth maps. Our method constructs depth-ordered merge trees through a deterministic union-find sweep, combining component area, depth persistence, and spatial contact constraints to guide region merging. A threshold search determines the segmentation granularity according to a specified target region count, while global depth detrending improves the separation of local geometric structures. Extensive experiments on COCO Panoptic, Cityscapes, and ADE20K demonstrate consistent improvements over classical segmentation baselines and competitive performance against zero-shot neural segmentation models. Notably, our method outperforms all evaluated zero-shot models on Cityscapes, while remaining competitive with several approaches on COCO and ADE20K. These results highlight the potential of purely algorithmic geometric reasoning for image segmentation without learned segmentation models.



### SEE: Unlocking the Hidden Perception of Frozen VLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23962v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23962v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:53:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:53:22+00:00
- **Authors**: Alin-Ionut Popa, Tuo Zhang, Dimitrios Dimitriadis
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) grasp the overall content of an image but often miss the fine details a question hinges on, such as a small object in a large photo or a number in a dense infographic. Yet even when their answer is wrong, the region they point to often contains the evidence. We introduce Self-Elicited Evidence (SEE), a training-free method that lets a frozen VLM look again where it points. When the initial answer has low confidence, SEE prompts the model to propose regions where the details may appear, answers the question on these enlarged crops, and aggregates the answers with the original prediction through confidence-weighted voting. The model also answers with its own transcript, which helps on text-rich images. SEE needs no detector or OCR engine. Across five backbones, SEE improves every small-object benchmark by 2.3-13.1 points and outperforms five training-free baselines on small objects. With SEE, Qwen3.5-4B surpasses the direct answer of Qwen3.5-9B and matches the 6 times larger Qwen3.5-27B.



### Dual-Manifold Variational Autoencoders
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23964v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23964v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 06:58:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:58:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongxu Chen, Chun Wang, Yanghao Wang, Hongxiang Li, Zhen Wang, Zhixiang Zhang, Long Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In latent diffusion models, expanding the latent dimension of variational autoencoders (VAEs) is essential to push the upper bound of reconstruction quality. However, without explicit structural constraints, such unregularized expansion easily yields a rugged and ill-conditioned latent manifold, causing generated latents to drift off the manifold during generation (i.e., off-manifold problem). Recent works smooth this manifold by using external foundation models (e.g., DINOv2) as a topological regularizer. However, we argue that tying the latent space to frozen external priors inherently restricts generalization: bound by their pre-training distributions and semantic biases, they may fail to capture faithful visual details on out-of-distribution domains. Observing that low-dimensional latents naturally form a smoother, generation-friendly manifold, we propose dual-manifold VAE (DM-VAE) to leverage internal representations as a topological regularizer. Specifically, DM-VAE pairs a high-dimensional reconstructive branch with a low-dimensional branch that extracts a clean topological skeleton, anchoring the high-dimensional manifold to this smooth subspace. Building upon this framework, we further introduce topological representation alignment (TRA) to supervise intermediate generator features during training, and internal guidance with topological skeleton (IG_TS) to guide structural components while preserving high-frequency details at inference. On ImageNet-1K (256x256), our foundation-model-free DM-VAE achieves a state-of-the-art rFID of 0.12 and a generation FID50K of 1.28 (w/o CFG) and 1.20 (w/CFG), and further reaches 1.11 when orthogonally combined with DINOv2.



### TOMAS: Topology-Aware Operation Modeling and Adaptive Staging for Egocentric Hand Forecasting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23970v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23970v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:10:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:10:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Guanzhong Sun, Junyi Ma, Yuxuan Wu, Erhang Zhang, Chenyang Xu, Yanzi Miao, Hesheng Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Forecasting future hand movements from egocentric videos plays an important role in understanding human intentions and hand-object interactions. However, different manipulation intentions can lead to substantially different hand trajectories even when interacting with the same object. Existing methods have not sufficiently explored the intrinsic relationships between articulated hand movements and object semantics to capture such operation-dependent motion patterns. Therefore, we propose TOMAS, a topology-aware operation modeling and adaptive staging framework for egocentric hand forecasting. Specifically, we develop a topology-aware hand-object reasoning mechanism that associates hand skeletal structures and articulation dynamics with object semantics and spatial relationships to jointly infer potential interaction targets and latent manipulation operations. The resulting object-operation representations provide interaction conditions for future hand trajectory prediction, with supervision adapted to the annotations available in each dataset. Furthermore, we introduce an adaptive staging mechanism that organizes future hand movements into ordered latent stages with learnable temporal boundaries. Stage-specific geometric subgoals are then incorporated to guide trajectory refinement according to the underlying manipulation patterns. Experimental results on multiple publicly available benchmarks demonstrate that TOMAS achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-dimensional and multi-target hand motion forecasting.



### Agreement Is Not Accuracy: LiDAR Elevation Errors Hidden in LiDAR-Camera Extrinsics
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23972v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23972v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:12:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:12:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Yeon Kun Lee, Dongseok Yang, Deok-Kyeong Jang, Choongho Chung, Junyoung Lee, Hyeongseok So
- **Comment**: 14 pages, 6 figures, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: LiDAR and cameras provide complementary measurements for 3D vision, yet agreement between their observations does not establish the accuracy of either sensor's internal geometry. We show that systematic LiDAR elevation biases can be absorbed into the estimated extrinsics. Using synchronized LiDAR intensities and camera-estimated checkerboard poses, we jointly estimate a sensor-specific elevation correction shared across recordings and extrinsics for each recording. Ray-plane intersections locate the checker pattern without using reported ranges, preventing range errors from directly moving the lookup. Under matched intensity and correction models, this lookup gives smaller camera-consistency residuals than measured-point lookup. Across six identically designed rigs using the same sensor models, recording-excluded corrections reduce the mean translation-reference distance from 7.57 to 3.04 mm. On separate structures, they reduce inter-plane angle errors against references from independent dense camera scans and improve image-edge alignment. Rolling the rigs shifts the plane pairs exhibiting the largest angular improvements, as predicted for an elevation-only correction. Within the ray-plane model, calibration-target projections and loop-consistency residuals change little despite these geometric improvements. These findings show why agreement after extrinsic calibration need not establish correct internal LiDAR geometry. Synchronized observations of a familiar checkerboard provide a practical route to estimating and testing elevation corrections without a dedicated large calibration field. We plan to release code and data for reproducing the main results.



### SIRAZ: Sparse Inter-Layer Residual Aggregation for Zero-Shot Visual Neural Decoding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23991v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23991v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:34:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:34:31+00:00
- **Authors**:  Neha, Mudasir Ganaie
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Brain-computer interfaces aim to establish direct communication between neural activity and external systems, making interpretation of brain signals an important problem. In this context, neural visual decoding seeks to infer visual information from neural activity by learning a mapping between brain responses and the corresponding visual stimuli. It investigates how visual information emerges from neural signals and relates to perceptual processing while enabling the reconstruction or retrieval of visual stimuli from neural signals. Our brain primarily follows a hierarchical pathway to process visual information captured by the eye. However, neural representations learned by existing methods differ fundamentally from those produced by the biological visual system. To bridge this gap and mimic the human vision hierarchy, we introduce Sparse Inter-layer Residual Aggregation for Zero-shot (SIRAZ). SIRAZ is a framework that aligns neural visual representations by jointly modeling early visual processes via fovea- and magnocellular-pathway-inspired Difference-of-Gaussians transformations. To align the semantic information with neural information, it extracts multiple intermediate representations of the image at multiple depths and passes them through the Residual Gated Adapter Mechanism. To study the temporal dynamics of neural signals, the Direction-aware Multi-Scale Temporal Attention Encoder is introduced to align the learned neural representation with the final visual representation. The proposed framework achieves 92.5% top-1 and 99.27% top-5 accuracy in the intra-subject setting on the THINGS-EEG dataset, and 50.25% and 75% top-1 and top-5 accuracy in the THINGS-MEG dataset. The results indicate that combining biologically motivated visual representations with the structured modalities of neural dynamics improves cross-modal alignment and zero-shot image retrieval.



### WaveView: The Novel View Is in the Noise
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23994v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23994v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:37:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:37:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Kaiwen Wang, Jan-Hendrik Pauls, Yinzhe Shen, Frank Bieder, Christoph Stiller
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generative novel view synthesis (NVS) employs a diffusion model to render a static or dynamic scene from a target camera view. Explicit warp methods complete a reprojection of the source with video diffusion models, and implicit methods encode the camera parameters or 3D cues. Both add inference overhead, and the generated layout often drifts from the ground truth. We present WaveView, which injects the warped source into the phase of the initial noise of a pretrained generator. The Dual-Tree Complex Wavelet Packet Transform (DT-CWPT) allows local and anisotropic phase injection. The generator is thus constrained less in disoccluded regions and along the epipolar line. WaveView keeps the architecture and adds negligible overhead. On the DL3DV benchmark, it outperforms all baselines in every overlap bin. In the hardest bin, it gains 0.84 dB PSNR on the covisible region over MetaView, which shares its generator, while running 2.1 times faster. For dynamic NVS, fine-tuning AnyView with the same noise raises its zero-shot PSNR on AnyViewBench from 11.58 to 12.35 dB.



### ReSynth: Constructing Synthetic Supervision for Multi-Image Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23996v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23996v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:44:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:44:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Yilin Wang, Xin Yu, Xinyi Guo, Xiangxi Zheng, Dongxing Mao, Linjie Li, Zhengyuan Yang, Alex Jinpeng Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: People often understand the world by comparing different views of it. Vision-language models (VLMs), however, receive these views as separate images and still struggle to relate them. One problem is data. Training on these tasks requires groups of images with a known relationship: the same object from different viewpoints, or the same scene before and after a change. Such examples are hard to collect at scale from existing photo collections. We introduce ReSynth, which turns single-image perception and controllable image generation into cross-image relational supervision. Localized editing produces before-and-after pairs, viewpoint generation produces multiple views of the same instance, and verified scene contents support comparisons of counts and attributes. ReSynth combines Qwen3-VL-8B's captioning, grounding, and counting capabilities with Qwen-Image-2.1's generation and editing capabilities. It verifies single-image facts such as object counts and attributes, then combines these facts to construct multi-image QA. Using GRPO on approximately 440K ReSynth questions, with no real images during training, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B improve by 17.73 and 6.10 average points on BLINK, MUIRBench, and VLM2-Bench, while single-image performance is broadly preserved on Qwen2.5-VL-7B and improves across all four evaluated benchmarks on Qwen3-VL-8B. Notably, this supervision enables the 8B perception model itself to outperform Qwen3-VL-235B on BLINK and MUIRBench.



### ProtoBridge-CLIP: Observation-Masked Finding Prototypes for Medical Image--Report Representation Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24002v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24002v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:52:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:52:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Jinrui Wu, Yun Peng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Radiology reports distinguish affirmed findings, explicit negations, and unmentioned concepts, but conventional global image--report contrastive learning often compresses these different evidence states into a single study-level target. We introduce \model{}, which combines symmetric global image--report alignment with observation-masked finding supervision and a soft relation objective over affirmed report concepts. Text-derived finding prototypes are aligned with local visual tokens, while uncertain and unmentioned concepts are excluded from direct finding labels. We evaluate the framework on three CT benchmarks and five chest-radiograph benchmarks, together with component ablations, paired and clinical retrieval, localization, perturbation robustness, calibration, and per-finding analyses. \model{} achieves macro-AUROC values of 73.6%, 66.5\%, and 68.8% on CT-RATE, RAD-ChestCT, and INSPECT, and 80.2%, 91.4%, 78.5%, 86.5%, and 90.8% on ChestXray14, CheXpert, PadChest, RSNA, and SIIM-ACR, respectively. Relative to the matched global-only controls, the findings and relation objectives yield consistent gains across the evaluated datasets. These results demonstrate the practical value of treating observed and unobserved report concepts differently during medical image--text representation learning.



### Choosing Supervised Post-Processing for Frozen Person Detectors: Information, Object Changes, and Complete Cost
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24003v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24003v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:53:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:53:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianyi Zhou, Zijun Jia
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A frozen detector computes hypotheses that can support better final outputs through supervised post-processing. Choosing a readout requires knowing which available information improves detection and what its processing costs. We compare candidate scores, class distributions, geometry, native-set summaries and adapted neighborhood meta classification under common candidate, supervision and operating-point conditions. On 320 held-out CrowdHuman images, adding native-set context to a 97-dimensional readout yields 275 more matches and 96 more unmatched predictions. On a separate 319-image cohort, Full100 matches 6004 objects,versus 5848 for source-selected raw WBF and 6002 for a MetaDetect-style adaptation. Its gain over raw contains 300 added and 144 lost discoveries. After execution-equivalent optimization, Full100 and the meta-statistics readout both take approximately 95 ms per complete warm request on a fixed 64-image workload. Source-frozen transfer gives positive point results on 223 MOT17Det frames from seven sequences, while the main MIAP result shows no improvement. The study supports using sufficiently informative output readouts while retaining optimized meta classification as a competitive option. It also shows why feature dimension and net detection gain alone are unreliable guides to adoption.



### Freeforward4d: Annotation-free feed-forward 4d reconstruction of driving scenes
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24007v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24007v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 07:58:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:58:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Luosong Guo, Xiang Yang, Chaoyang Li, Ao Gao, Jiangming Shi, Yanyun Qu, Zhizhong Zhang, Jingyu Gong, Yuan Xie
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Feed-forward 4D reconstruction enables efficient creation of renderable driving scenes for autonomous driving simulation. However, most existing methods rely heavily on dataset-provided cameras, LiDAR depth, or 3D box annotations, limiting their applicability to unannotated driving videos. Pretrained visual models can predict cameras, depth, and object tracks from RGB alone, but their geometry is mutually inconsistent: multi-view predictions come in short windows with independent scales and frames, while monocular depth is metric only per image. Our key insight is that, once reconciled into a single metric frame, these priors can replace dataset annotations for feed-forward 4D reconstruction. We present \textbf{FreeForward4D}, which calibrates overlapping prediction windows against monocular metric depth and registers them into one frame across time. On this geometry, 2D tracks are associated across cameras in 3D, and objects move through hierarchical motion that combines a shared object velocity with local Gaussian corrections, so that both training and inference use only RGB videos. On Waymo, FreeForward4D achieves 27.66 dB PSNR and 0.836 SSIM while recovering metric-scale cameras and depth without scale alignment. It surpasses STORM, SLARM-F, UFO, DGGT, and StreetForward, which rely on dataset annotations, in full-image PSNR and attains the best RGB fidelity on 8-second windows. RGB-only fine-tuning further adapts the framework to nuScenes and Argoverse 2. Code will be released.



### HHW-Ego: DSLR-Quality Enhancement for Multi-Source Wearable Ego Imaging
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24010v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24010v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:01:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:01:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Yunfei Tong, Qianqian Sun, Jixiang Luo, Dell Zhang, Hao Sun
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Wearable ego-centric images are now in high demand for scenarios ranging from daily smart glass usage to embodied intelligence. But the image quality is far behind smart phone due to the lacking paired high-quality reference images and the dedicated enhancement systems. To solve this, a customized degradation pipeline is designed to generate paired samples matching wearable camera images, boosting the upper limit of enhancement performance. Besides a two-stage enhancement framework is further built: first, tuning an efficient model on the paired dataset to enhance real wearable images; second, using hyperspectral data to refine color temperature for better quality. Moreover we present HHW-Ego, a multi-source paired dataset integrating with Hyperspectral, High-dynamic range and Wearable Ego-centric data, which includes 5,000 image groups spanning indoor/outdoor and day/night scenes. On held-out paired data, Stage-1 fine-tuning improves PSNR, SSIM, Lab distance, and LPIPS for Real-ESRGAN and PFT-SR. On real wearable images, no-reference metrics, human preference, gamut analysis, and visual comparisons characterize the effects of Stage 2.



### WAM-Policy: World Action Models with On-Policy Distillation for Quadrupedal Loco-Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24011v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24011v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:03:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:03:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Junlin Long, Xu Deng, Yiran Wang, Yue Yang, Yang Zhao, Zeyu Zhang
- **Comment**: Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/WAM-Policy, Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/WAM-Policy/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Arm-free quadrupeds can perform loco-manipulation, walking on three legs while manipulating with a front leg, yet no existing policy for it follows language instructions. World action models (WAMs) have emerged as effective language-conditioned manipulation policies, but are pretrained for robot arms and sample slowly. WAM-Policy turns one WAM into a quadrupedal loco-manipulation policy via two stages. The first, Cross-Morphology Policy Adaptation and Acceleration, adapts the arm-centric WAM to quadrupeds and distills the transferred policy into a student with one video and two action steps by on-policy self-distillation (OPSD). This student, however, is bounded by its teacher, which only imitates demonstrations. The second stage, Task-Specific Improvement and Merging, therefore tunes the teacher by reinforcement learning (RL) and merges the slow specialists into one few-step student by multi-teacher on-policy distillation (M-OPD). On QLM-Bench, ten simulated tasks with 1,000 demonstrations synthesized without teleoperation, the transferred policy reaches 77.0%, and the OPSD student keeps 76.7% while sampling 14 times faster. Task-specific RL and M-OPD lift it to 85.0%. We also deploy the OPSD student on a real Unitree Go2 Air. WAM-Policy thus achieves a stronger quadrupedal policy at a fraction of the inference cost.



### FlyVQA: Seeing Video Frame Interpolation Quality through Fly Eyes
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24012v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24012v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:04:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:04:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Baoliang Chen, Lingyu Zhu, Zhiwei Zhong, Shiqi Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The ultimate ambition of visual quality assessment is to reproduce the neural computations of human vision and thereby achieve full alignment with human perception. However, the immense complexity of human visual circuitry makes this goal difficult to realize. Recently released fly visual connectomes provide a tractable biological starting point. Despite differences between fly and human vision, the fly's well-characterized motion pathways motivate us to investigate their potential for assessing the perceptual impact of temporal distortions. In this paper, we use a shared, frozen fly visual circuit to perform full-reference quality assessment of frame-interpolated videos, whose artifacts can disrupt temporal consistency. Specifically, compound-eye sampling maps reference and distorted sequences into the circuit, while a connectome-aware readout quantifies discrepancies in cell-type-specific activity and excitatory--inhibitory synaptic dynamics. Reference-guided temporal pooling and a lightweight regression head integrate these differences into a quality score. Experiments across multiple interpolation methods demonstrate better agreement with human ratings than CNN, RNN, and Transformer baselines with comparable parameters, supporting a biologically grounded approach to perceptual quality modeling.



### Rethinking Contrastive Targets in Radiology Language-Image Pretraining
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24016v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24016v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:08:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:08:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Yunhe Gao, Ashwin Kumar, Jiaming Liu, Chong Wang, Maya Varma, Fuying Wang, Stefania L. Moroianu, Louisa Fay, Sergios Gatidis, Akshay S. Chaudhari, Curtis Langlotz
- **Comment**: NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: CLIP-style contrastive image-text pretraining is widely used in radiology data, where each image is paired with one report and every other report in the batch is treated as a negative. Under this recipe, two failures appear, neither of which is systematically addressed by existing contrastive learning theory: zero-shot evaluation peaks then degrades while training loss keeps decreasing, and training fails to learn from large batches. We hypothesize the cause is structural rather than radiology-specific: many unpaired reports share clinical findings with a given image, so CLIP's paired-identity target is systematically biased, not merely noisy. To test this, we construct CLEVR-Radiology, a synthetic testbed that preserves only this overlapping-fact structure and reproduces both failures. With evidence that the structure is sufficient to reproduce the failures, we develop a target-misspecification theory that explains both failures from a single deterministic gradient bias amplified by batch size, and use it to motivate a minimal compatibility-based correction, CorLIP: text is treated as positive whenever its clinical assertions agree with the image, with no architecture changes. Beyond benchmark improvements, CorLIP largely removes the post-peak degradation, makes large-batch training succeed where it previously collapsed, substantially amplifies the gains from more data, and is the only configuration that remains stable up to B=2048. The correction transfers from chest X-ray to 3D CT modalities.



### Semi-Supervised GAN-Based Segmentation of CTPA Scans for Pulmonary Emboli Boundary Extraction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24018v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/CBMS69103.2026.00315
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24018v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:09:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:09:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Maria Thoma, Michalis Savelonas, Foteini Malli, Dimitris Iakovidis
- **Comment**: 2026 IEEE 39th International Symposium on Computer-Based Medical Systems (CBMS), Limassol, Cyprus
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pulmonary embolism (PE) is a pulmonary vascular disease associated with high morbidity and mortality, where delayed diagnosis can be fatal. While computed tomography pulmonary angiography (CTPA) is the clinical gold standard for PE detection, interpreting these scans manually is time-consuming and error-prone. In this work, we propose a semi-supervised segmentation method, employing generative adversarial networks (GANs) to leverage large pools of unlabeled CTPA scans. The proposed method addresses more effectively the challenges of CTPA image segmentation than previous methods by employing the dice loss and the cross-entropy loss to tackle the highly imbalanced nature of CTPA scans, the feature matching loss to encourage the generator to produce anatomically plausible segmentation maps that are indistinguishable from ground truth, as well as the self-training loss to reinforce learning from highly-confident pseudo-labeled samples. The experimental evaluation on a publicly available dataset shows that the proposed method maintains accurate segmentation, despite a limited number of labeled CTPA scans. with a Dice coefficient of 88.75% and a 95th percentile Hausdorff distance (HD95) of 1.74, outperforming fully supervised baselines.



### STAR++: A Physical-Aware Benchmark and Framework for Astronomical Super-Resolution across Multiple Filters
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24023v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24023v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:13:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:13:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Guohang Zhuang, Jiaze Li, Kuo-cheng Wu, Hao Du, Jinyang Huang, Xiang Zhang, Wanli Ouyang, Yan Lu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Astronomical super-resolution (ASR) aims to reconstruct high-resolution star fields from low-resolution telescope observations across multiple filters for astrophysical analysis. However, developing a physics-aware ASR framework faces two challenges: 1) Data view: no large-scale benchmark supports field-level ASR, especially on unseen filters. 2) Method view: Existing ASR methods often fail to preserve stellar flux across spatial resolutions. Moreover, ASR requires reconstructing high-frequency components, including accurate star locations and profiles, making it more ill-posed than natural-image SR. To tackle these problems, we introduce STAR++, a large-scale benchmark comprising approximately 1.7 million flux-consistent image pairs across 36 filters, derived from Hubble Space Telescope (HST) observations. STAR++ uses a flux-preserving data generation pipeline to ensure photometric consistency across resolutions and provides the Flux Error (FE) metric to evaluate ASR models from a physical perspective. To address method-level challenges, we propose a Flux-Invariant Super-Resolution Plus (FISR++) model that generalizes to unseen filters while accurately reconstructing flux-consistent high-resolution images. Beyond the flux-preserving modules, FISR++ introduces two novel components. Specifically, a Filter-Aware Adapter (FAA) incorporates filter metadata to adapt image representations to varying spectral and optical conditions. In addition, a Spatial Ambiguity Disentangle Loss (SADL) models spatial ambiguity as pixel-shift uncertainty, mitigating the smoothing of learned conditional mean reconstructions caused by this ambiguity. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FISR++ consistently outperforms existing ASR methods in reconstruction quality and flux accuracy, while generalizing to unseen filters, highlighting its effectiveness for photometrically faithful ASR.



### Semantic-aware Contrasting with Explicitly Disentangled Representations for AI-Generated Video Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24038v1
- **DOI**: 10.64509/jicn.24.159
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24038v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:26:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:26:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiaquan Yu, Jingyi Xie, Zeyu Zhang, Yunhang Qian, Xiaobin Hu, Jiawei Liu
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: Journal of Intelligent Computing and Networking 2(4), 18-33 (2026)
- **Summary**: The rapid proliferation of AI-generated videos (AIGVs) has raised critical concerns about content authenticity. Existing benchmarks suffer from a fundamental limitation: real and fake videos are often collected or generated from different semantic distributions. As a result, detectors may rely on spurious correlations involving objects, scenes, or actions rather than intrinsic artifacts introduced by generative models. To this end, we introduce SAVGE, a large-scale semantically aligned AIGV detection dataset comprising 2,369,822 videos, including 424,115 real videos and 1,945,707 generated videos. SAVGE organizes samples into real-fake semantic groups by using captions paired with real videos as prompts for text-to-video generation, thereby providing both group-level correspondence and textual semantic anchors. Based on SAVGE, we further propose Explicit Disentangling and Semantic-aware Contrasting (EDSC), a model-agnostic training framework for learning artifact-focused representations with reduced semantic interference. EDSC first extracts and aligns visual semantic features with textual representations, and then explicitly suppresses semantic information in the artifact branch while contrasting real and fake samples within each semantic group. By integrating caption-guided semantic supervision with intra-group contrastive learning in a two-phase optimization strategy, EDSC encourages detectors to capture generation-related cues under matched semantic conditions. Extensive experiments on SAVGE and GenVideo demonstrate that EDSC improves most evaluated backbone-metric combinations, enhances robustness under challenging evaluation settings, and strengthens cross-dataset generalization. These results highlight the importance of semantic alignment in AIGV detection and validate explicit semantic-artifact disentanglement as an effective strategy for robust detector learning.



### CURVTON-205K: A Mask-Free Difficulty-Stratified Synthetic Dataset for Robust and Scalable Virtual Try-On
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24042v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24042v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:30:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:30:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Dipan Bartaula, Anuj Shrestha, Ankit Belbase, Anish Bishwakarma, Saroj Poudel, Samip Ghimire, Shashank Bhatta, Eric Arazo, Binod Bhattarai
- **Comment**: Accepted at IEEE/CVF Winter Conference on Applications of Computer Vision (WACV) 2027. Project page: https://curvton2.github.io/curvton
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent advances in 2D virtual try-on (VTON) have largely focused on architectural innovation, yet robust performance in diverse real-world conditions remains challenging. We argue that failure modes in VTON, such as inaccurate occlusion handling, unconstrained poses, and poor coverage of under-represented garments and appearance factors, are tightly coupled to dataset design choices and long-tail data regimes. We present a scalable data generation pipeline that coordinates specialized editing and generation models with vision-language model (VLM) guidance to synthesize triplets without requiring parsing masks or dense-pose annotations. Using this pipeline, we create CURVTON-205K, a curated synthetic dataset of 205K triplets: source person, target garment, and corresponding try-on image. CURVTON-205K is explicitly designed to broaden coverage across garments, person appearance, poses, occlusions, and visual conditions. Additionally, CURVTON-205K is organized into three difficulty tiers, enabling controlled evaluation under varying levels of difficulty and supporting difficulty-aware experimentation. We analyze the dataset diversity and demonstrate its potential to improve try-on fidelity across four VTON architectures and its cross-dataset generalization.



### R$^{3}$Sieve: Suppressing Unconditional Information from Attention to Answers for High-Resolution VQA
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24060v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24060v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 08:54:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 08:54:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Yifan Cao, Yawen Zhang, Xiaocui Yang, Faxian Wan, Shi Feng, Daling Wang, Yifei Zhang, Shangshu Yu, Xianbing Zhao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: High-resolution visual question answering (VQA) requires models to identify question-relevant small objects in high-resolution images and select the correct answer based on what they recognize, demanding fine-grained perception. Previous methods focus on obtaining better visual evidence (image crops), but obtaining it alone is insufficient, using it effectively is equally crucial. Visual evidence should therefore be utilized as effectively as possible throughout inference. We argue that small-target evidence is obscured by unconditional information that remains observable in subsequent processing even after the condition on which it should depend is removed. Such information hinders the effective use of small-target evidence. We propose R$^{3}$Sieve (Residual-Region-Response Sieve), a training-free framework. We divide inference into three stages---localization, cropping, and answering---and introduce distinct counterfactual references to suppress interference at each stage. During localization, the Shared-Subspace Canceller (SSC) uses a generic image description to construct a counterfactual reference and obtain question-relevant attention maps. During cropping, the Unique-Contribution Spotter (UCS) constructs counterfactual references from visual and non-visual features to identify distinctive, complementary crop regions. During answering, the Content-Aligned Voter (CAV) constructs an image-free branch to calibrate answer responses. Experiments on three high-resolution VQA benchmarks show that R$^{3}$Sieve achieves the highest overall accuracy among compared methods across diverse settings, improving base models by up to 21.5 percentage points.



### Benchmarking Reasoning Reliability,Prior_Evidence Contrast,and Spectral Discrimination in Remote-Sensing Geological Image Interpretation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24068v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24068v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:02:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:02:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Jun Wang, Fengpeng Li, Wei Han, Yuxuan Li, Tianjin Huang, Jining Yan, Sheng Wang, Kan Sun, Lizhe Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reliable lithology interpretation requires accurate geological recognition and reasoning grounded in visual evidence. Although Multimodal Large Language Models show promise in remote sensing, their reliability in geological interpretation remains unclear. We introduce HyGeoBench, a diagnostic benchmark of 10,000 questions based on hyperspectral imagery across three dimensions: reasoning reliability, prior_evidence contrast, and spectral discrimination. It covers ten task types, with both multiple-choice and open-ended response formats. Integrating hyperspectral satellite imagery with geological annotations and verified evidence, HyGeoBench examines reasoning errors, conflicts between geological priors and visual evidence, and cross-band inconsistencies. Evaluations reveal limitations in fine-grained recognition and evidence-grounded interpretation. To address these challenges, we propose HyGeoAgent, a tool-augmented framework integrating region observation, text knowledge retrieval, same-band image retrieval, and native PCA analysis through adaptive tool use. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements over the corresponding base models, improving geological image interpretation across diagnostic dimensions.



### SemPhys: Semantic-Physical Co-Learning for Humanoid Motion Generation via Differentiable World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24074v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24074v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:03:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:03:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Zejian Kang, Yeqiang Wang, Zekai Chen, Boyang Shen, Mingyu Ma, Bingqian Wu, Fan Zhou, Jianjie Zhang, Chentao Shen, Wentao Yang, Rui Qian, Xiangru Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In human-robot collaboration, natural language lets people direct a humanoid's whole-body movements without programming joint trajectories. A common approach generates a kinematic reference from an instruction and relies on a tracking controller to execute it. However, as the controller maintains balance under contact constraints, the executed motion can depart from the reference in ways that change the action it conveys. Our central insight is that semantic alignment must be evaluated directly on the complete motion the robot physically executes. Building on this insight, we introduce \textbf{SemPhys}, a framework that trains motion generators using semantic feedback from simulated executions. A differentiable world model and a fixed tracking controller predict a short segment of a recorded execution. SemPhys inserts this prediction at its original position, scores the complete trajectory against the instruction, and backpropagates through the predicted segment. A paired anchoring loss stabilizes adaptation by keeping each generated reference close to its counterpart from the initial generator. On BONES-SEED, SemPhys largely preserves instruction retrieval accuracy from reference to execution and achieves the highest retrieval accuracy on executed motions among the evaluated methods.



### AeroRSI: Recursive Self-Improvement for Aerial Vision-Language Navigation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24077v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24077v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:07:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:07:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Shiwei Wu, Yuwen Pan, Zhi Li, Yaowen Li, Yan Zhou, Xuemiao Xu, Yu Liu
- **Comment**: 8-page main paper with references and supplementary material (15 pages); 5 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/starsholic/AeroRSI Project page: https://starsholic.github.io/AeroRSI/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models enable unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) to follow natural-language instructions, yet still fail frequently in closed-loop flight. Correcting these failures requires repairs, i.e., actions that avoid them at the states the policy itself reaches, which human pilots cannot label at scale and rule-based planners cannot supply reliably. In this paper, we present AeroRSI, a recursive self-improvement framework in which an aerial navigation policy iteratively learns from its own failures. Our key insight is that a failing policy should judge its own repairs by flying on from them. Judged in this way, its sampled actions and even rule-based proposals that hurt it when imitated directly become reliable supervision. Moreover, an episode terminates at its first failure, concealing subsequent failures until the earlier ones are repaired. AeroRSI thus renews its supervision in every round, anchoring the failures of the current policy, verifying repairs by its continuation, and distilling them while keeping its behavior elsewhere. On TravelUAV, three rounds raise the success rate of a supervised 2B-parameter policy from 46.4% to 67.5% and reduce collisions by half, surpassing the previous state of the art by 10.6 points from the same initialization without extra human labels or inference cost. Code and models are available at https://github.com/starsholic/AeroRSI.



### APS: Adaptive Partitioning-Based Frame Selection for Long Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24079v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24079v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:07:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:07:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Junxiang Wen, Yinxi Zhao, Jiawei Chen, Jingying Hu, Lijun Wang, Zhen Qin, Shuiguang Deng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent advances in large vision-language models (LVLMs) have significantly improved video understanding capabilities, yet processing long videos remains challenging due to the substantial computational cost of extensive visual inputs. Frame selection offers an efficient alternative, but many existing region-based methods rely on fixed or predefined temporal partitions, which may result in suboptimal temporal regions, compromising subsequent budget allocation and frame selection. In this work, we propose Adaptive Partitioning-Based Frame Selection (APS), a training-free framework that adapts temporal partitions to local variations in query-frame relevance. Specifically, APS formulates temporal partitioning as a complexity-regularized fitting problem, jointly optimizing partition boundaries and local models through dynamic programming. The resulting adaptive temporal partitions guide frame budget allocation based on query relevance and temporal extent, enabling informative frame selection under limited visual budgets. Extensive experiments on Video-MME, MLVU, and LongVideoBench demonstrate that APS consistently improves performance across diverse LVLM backbones, achieving the highest average gains over uniform sampling among competing methods. Further analyses validate the effectiveness of adaptive partitioning and demonstrate its generalizability across model scales, similarity encoders, and advanced LVLMs.



### CausalDrive: Causal Decomposition for Data-Efficient End-to-End Autonomous Driving
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24080v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24080v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:07:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:07:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaobao Wei, Jingxian Wu, Lirong Yang, Qing Han, Yuan Gao, Ziwei Yang, Shuai Yang, Weiwei Liu, Chang Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Efficient use of expert demonstrations is essential for end-to-end autonomous driving. However, directly imitating expert trajectories couples nominal intention, which specifies where the vehicle should go, with multimodal driving styles, which determine how that intention is executed. Different combinations of these factors can yield similar expert trajectories, obscuring their respective causal contributions and forcing the policy to learn both from a single entangled target. This ambiguity limits how effectively the policy can exploit expert demonstrations. We propose CausalDrive, a causal decomposition framework for data-efficient driving policy learning that separates deterministic nominal intention prediction from probabilistic driving style modeling. Specifically, we construct counterfactual nominal trajectories from navigation command, map topology, and ego state by removing style dependent effects, providing a Nominal Intention Planner with supervision independent of expert trajectories. Conditioned on the predicted nominal intention and surrounding agents, a diffusion-based Style Execution Policy models diverse driving styles as a multimodal distribution over residuals between nominal and expert trajectories. This causally structured supervision reduces redundant learning of shared nominal behavior in routine scenarios, directing expert supervision toward informative execution variations and improving data utilization. CausalDrive achieves 91.2 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 89.5 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, matching or surpassing state-of-the-art performance. Further experiments demonstrate strong performance with limited expert data and clear advantages in challenging interactive scenarios.



### Learning Where and How Much to Sample: Volume-Adaptive k-Space Acquisition for Accelerated MRI
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24081v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24081v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:08:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:08:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Reyhaneh Neshatavar, Mohsen Yavartanoo, Kyoung Mu Lee
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is an essential medical imaging technique, but its high costs and long acquisition times can limit accessibility. Accelerated MRI reduces acquisition time through k-space undersampling, yet conventional methods often rely on predefined sampling patterns and uniform slice budgets, overlooking anatomical variations and inter-slice relationships. In this paper, we propose a volume-adaptive k-space acquisition framework that jointly learns where and how much to sample across an entire MRI volume. Using only acquired auto-calibration signal (ACS) measurements, our method exploits volume-wide anatomical information to adaptively allocate sampling budgets across slices while identifying informative k-space lines. A learned joint slice--frequency refinement module coordinates sampling decisions while preserving an exact total volume-level acquisition budget. To reconstruct the acquired measurements, we design a sensitivity-aware synchronous cross-slice reconstruction network that estimates coil sensitivities from ACS measurements and progressively refines neighboring-slice reconstructions while enforcing hard k-space data consistency. Both networks are jointly optimized using reconstruction-based supervision without requiring fully sampled k-space at inference. Experiments on knee and brain MRI across multiple contrasts and acceleration factors demonstrate the adaptive behavior of our acquisition framework and evaluate its reconstruction performance.



### From Restoration to Redrawing: Revitalizing Ancient Chinese Characters by SVG Code Generation and Editing
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24084v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24084v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:11:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:11:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Weiguang Zhang, Linhua Dong, Bin Dong, Shanshan Jiang, Maizhen Ning, Xiaoqiang Kang, Kun Zhang, Hao Xu, Xiaowei Huang, Wei Wang, Kaizhu Huang, Qiufeng Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Hanjian manuscripts written on Han Dynasty bamboo and wooden slips are invaluable heritage of ancient Chinese writing but suffer from damaged ink and incomplete strokes. Existing image-to-image restoration methods may alter observed strokes and offer limited control over stroke geometry. Inspired by experts' manual redrawing practices, we address these issues by introducing \textbf{ReDraw}, a two-stage vision-language model(VLM) that reformulates character restoration as Scalable Vector Graphics(SVG) code generation and editing. Stage one targets faithful vectorization of observed strokes geometry or conservative tracing of mildly degraded strokes. Guided by skeleton chain-of-thought reasoning and reinforcement learning with structure-aware rewards, stage two predicts skeletons before contours to redraw severely incomplete strokes by appending paths while keeping existing SVG paths unchanged. To support training, we develop a multi-layer data synthesis pipeline that disentangles ink, material, and degradation to generate 1.2M samples, and derive up to 6,400 adaptation pairs from 320 annotated real glyphs. Experiments demonstrate synthetic restoration performance and adaptability to real Hanjian glyphs. Code and demo will be available upon acceptance.



### UCLIR: Unsupervised Continual Learning using Independent Representations
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24093v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24093v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:19:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:19:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Kamel Aouaidjia, Yanchao Duan, Zihui Zhen, Xin Li, Yongfei Lv, Chongsheng Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In continual learning, regularization-based methods aim to mimic neuroplasticity by updating network weights to learn new tasks while constraining changes to parameters that are important for previously learned tasks through penalty terms. However, as the number of tasks increases, preserving knowledge from earlier tasks becomes less effective. This is mainly because updates for new tasks are distributed across the entire network, where parameters are highly interdependent due to the dense connectivity between neurons. Inspired by the Exponential Distance Rule (EDR), which states that the number of neural connections decreases with increasing physical distance, learning is naturally locally correlated while inter-area updates remain less task-dependent. Motivated by this principle, in this paper we demonstrate that applying weight regularization to multiple small and independently learned complementary representations is more effective than regularizing a single large shared representation. By decomposing the input into independently learned components, learning new tasks induces only localized parameter updates within specific representation, which limit interference and improve knowledge retention across tasks. We employ independent autoencoders that learn different parts of the input independently through unsupervised regularization. At inference time, a linear classifier is used for evaluation. The experimental results on a sequence of tasks using 5-Splits MNIST, sequence of 5-datasets, 5-Splits CIFAR-10, 10-Splits Tiny ImageNet shows competitive results with supervised and replay-based methods. Code is available.



### Object Pose: The Link between Weak Perspective, Paraperspective, and Full Perspective
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24095v1
- **DOI**: 10.1023/A:1007940112931
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24095v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:19:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:19:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Radu Horaud, Fadi Dornaika, Bart Lamiroy, Stephane Christy
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: International Journal of Computer Vision 22(2), 173-189 (1997)
- **Summary**: Recently, DeMenthon and Davis [4,5] proposed a method for determining the pose of a 3-D object with respect to a camera from 3-D to 2-D point correspondences. The method consists of iteratively improving the pose computed with a weak perspective camera model to converge, at the limit, to a pose estimation computed with a perspective camera model. In this paper we give an algebraic derivation of DeMenthon and Davis' method and we show that it belongs to a larger class of methods where the perspective camera model is approximated either at zero order (weak perspective) or first order (paraperspective). We describe in detail an iterative paraperspective pose computation method for both non coplanar and coplanar object points. We analyse the convergence of these methods and we conclude that the iterative paraperspective method (proposed in this paper) has better convergence properties than the iterative weak perspective method. We introduce a simple way of taking into account the orthogonality constraint associated with the rotation matrix. We analyse the sensitivity to camera calibration errors and we define the optimal experimental setup with respect to imprecise camera calibration. We compare the results obtained with this method and with a non-linear optimization method.



### RoughCut-Bench: A Benchmark for Automatic Video Rough-Cut with Large Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24098v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24098v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:21:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:21:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Ruisi Zhao, You Li, Sihan Chen, Aosong Cheng, Chixiang Ma, Sen Pei, Zhen Yang, Hehe Fan, Dongliang He, Yi Yang
- **Comment**: Accepted to KDD 2027. Project page is at https://whalesong-zrs.github.io/RoughCut-Bench-Project-Page
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video rough-cut is a core stage in media production workflows, organizing raw media materials into an initial video cut. Despite recent advances in large vision-language models (LVLMs) for video-related tasks, automatic rough-cut remains underexplored, without a comprehensive benchmark for evaluation or mature workflows for producing executable cut plans. To address the evaluation gap, we introduce RoughCut-Bench, which covers two practical settings: (1) a visually driven setting over video cases, covering highlight detection and selection, source-video ordering, and adherence to user requirements when provided; and (2) a speech-driven setting over automatic speech recognition (ASR) transcripts, covering filler removal, expression deduplication, and low-value content trimming.
