Across these settings, the benchmark contains 101 visually driven cases, including 831 raw videos with 1,476 highlight annotations. It also includes 84 speech-driven cases, each consisting of an ASR transcript with annotated cleanup spans. The evaluation compares cut plans with these annotations in both settings and additionally uses a human-aligned agentic evaluator to assess rendered-cut quality in the visual setting. To apply LVLMs to automatic rough-cut, we further propose agentic workflows that guide them to produce structured cut plans from input materials. Experiments show that current LVLMs remain limited in producing expert-aligned cut plans. Our benchmark, code, and prompts are released at https://whalesong-zrs.github.io/RoughCut-Bench-Project-Page.



### IDEA: Intent-Dominance Evidence Audit for Inference-Time Backdoor Detection in GUI Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24102v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24102v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:22:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:22:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhizhuo Kou, Sizai Hou, Jian Yang, Ge Sun, Junyu Luo, Wenkai Zhang, Sirui Han, Yike Guo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Organizations increasingly deploy downloaded open-weight GUI agents. A backdoored agent passes acceptance tests on clean data and misbehaves only when a visual trigger appears on screen, turning an unvetted checkpoint into a supply-chain risk. However, existing sample-level detectors target image classifiers and CLIP-style encoders, leaving step-level backdoor detection for GUI agents unaddressed. On clean steps, the instruction drives the action; when an unreferenced screen region hijacks the step, the instruction loses control over the decision state or that region dictates it instead. We term this signature intent--evidence dominance inversion and propose IDEA (Intent-Dominance Evidence Audit) to detect backdoor activation at inference time for each step without trigger knowledge or attack data. Specifically, Intent Counterfactual Probing (ICP) measures how far redirected instructions move the decision state, and Region Causal Audit (RCA) quantifies how far ablating a candidate region moves it. A gated conformal score compares both displacements against clean steps at the same distance from the clean manifold, providing a finite-sample false-positive guarantee. Extensive experiments on Qwen2.5-VL-3B across five attack families show that IDEA consistently detects backdoor activation under a controlled false-positive rate and outperforms state-of-the-art detection methods, while localizing the trigger region for every alarm and adding only modest inference overhead.



### SCOPE: Single-Cell Omics Prediction from Histology via RNA-Protein Prior Enhancement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24104v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, q-bio.GN
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24104v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:24:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:24:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Leyang Tao, Xuan Gong, Luyuan Xie, Arjun Beniwal, Yuqi Tan, Kun-Hsing Yu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Predicting the RNA and protein content of cells from routine H&E images could extend spatial molecular profiling to existing slide archives. However, both H&E-spatial proteomics and H&E-spatial transcriptomics paired datasets are limited by the number of available markers and the insufficient availability of high-quality, cell-resolved data. We present SCOPE (Single-Cell Omics Prediction via RNA-protein prior Enhancement), a unified framework that leverages biologically complementary information between RNA and proteins to address the limited availability of single-omics supervision. Our key insight is that, although RNA and proteins represent distinct molecular modalities, their biological relationships provide valuable shared knowledge that can benefit single-omics prediction tasks. To this end, SCOPE introduces biologically informed biomarker priors to enhance shared gene-level representations, enabling knowledge transfer across RNA and protein modalities through gene-protein correspondence and molecular associations. By exploiting these complementary relationships, SCOPE enables both prediction tasks to benefit from a shared backbone enriched with cross-omics biological knowledge. Furthermore, its biomarker correlation design facilitates the prediction of unseen markers, extending spatially resolved molecular profiling beyond directly supervised targets. Across test datasets spanning 16 organs, SCOPE improves the average RNA Pearson correlation over the strongest baseline by 8.8%. On two external protein cohorts, it improves the average Pearson correlation over the strongest baseline by 9.7% for seen markers and 11.9% for unseen markers.



### EVF-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for E-commerce Visual Effects in Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24116v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24116v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:31:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:31:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Run Ling, Ke Cao, Runze He, Jie Gao, Ao Ma, Wei Feng, Zhanjie Zhang, Hao Wang, Jinghe Hu, Guibing Guo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: E-commerce visual effects (EVF) are dynamic, product-centered visual presentations in e-commerce videos, often accompanied by promotional text. They play an important role in attracting viewer attention and conveying product information. However, general measures of visual quality and text relevance alone do not establish whether generated videos preserve product appearance, maintain readable text, and produce coherent, contextually appropriate effects. To address this issue, we introduce EVF-Bench, a dedicated benchmark spanning six evaluation dimensions: visual quality and text relevance as general dimensions, and motion, product consistency, text stability, and creative rationality as EVF-specific dimensions. To complement the benchmark and support task-aligned research and model adaptation, we further construct EVF-32K, a dataset of over 32000 curated image-text-video triples tailored to e-commerce scenarios. Using this suite, we systematically evaluate representative video generation models and their domain-adapted variants. The results reveal complementary strengths, with no evaluated configuration leading all six dimensions. Together, EVF-Bench and EVF-32K provide a task-oriented evaluation framework and a supporting data resource for e-commerce video generation.



### How Well Do Generalist CT Foundation Models Differentiate Liver Tumors?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24122v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24122v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:41:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:41:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Yosuke Yamagishi, Tomohiro Kikuchi, Shouhei Hanaoka, Takeharu Yoshikawa, Osamu Abe
- **Comment**: 12 pages, 3 figures. Code: https://github.com/yamagishi0824/liver-tumor-ddx-ct-fm
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generalist CT foundation models detect many findings with high performance, but their ability to differentiate detected lesions remains largely unevaluated. This retrospective study evaluated two generalist models, RADAR and Merlin, for differentiating liver tumors using a public single-institution multiphase CT dataset (517 cases: hepatocellular carcinoma, intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, breast and colorectal cancer liver metastases, and hemangioma). The models were evaluated in a zero-shot setting using their existing outputs and with linear probing, in which only a linear classifier was trained on frozen image features (stratified fivefold cross-validation repeated five times). In the zero-shot setting, RADAR achieved an accuracy of 54.4% (95% CI: 49.9, 58.8) and a $κ$ of 0.37 for four-class differentiation at the portal venous phase and predicted 31% (129 of 421) of malignant tumors as hemangioma. With linear probing of four-phase features, its accuracy improved to 79.9% (95% CI: 76.6, 83.4) with a $κ$ of 0.72, and 1% (five of 421) of malignant tumors were predicted as hemangioma; Merlin achieved an accuracy of 60.9% (95% CI: 56.9, 65.0). Generalist CT foundation models showed limited zero-shot ability to differentiate liver tumors, which improved substantially with linear probing alone.



### PatchAdaNet: Unsupervised class incremental learning using local patches and global adapters
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24137v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24137v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:48:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:48:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Kamel Aouaidjia, Yanchao Duan, Zihui Zhen, Xin Li, Yongfei Lv, Chongsheng Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Biological learning systems acquire knowledge incrementally through broad, reusable representations established early and kept largely stable, while integrating new experience with minimal interference with previously learned knowledge. Replay-based methods approximate this by storing samples or features from previous tasks and mixing them into the current task. However, memory buffers grow with the number of tasks and may retain task-specific noise that reduces generalization. To address these limitations, we propose PatchAdaNet (Patch Adapter Network), an unsupervised class-incremental learning method for image classification that continually updates feature representations using autoencoders. Since images share structural features such as edges, corners, and shapes, we first divide each image into patches and train independent autoencoders on external data to reconstruct each patch. The layers of all patch encoders are aggregated into global frozen layers that generalize across tasks and remain fixed throughout learning. Learnable adapters then build global image representations from these features to reconstruct the entire image. To prevent feature drift without storing data, we introduce the Global Feature Centroid (GFC), a fixed-size running average of adapter outputs updated after each task to penalizes representation drift. Elastic Weight Consolidation (EWC) is applied to the learnable layers to preserve previously learned features by constraining important weights. Finally, a linear classifier is trained on the frozen latent representation and GFC for class-incremental evaluation. We evaluate PatchAdaNet in both CNN and Vision Transformer settings on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, miniImageNet and 5-Datasets, providing a comparative study of the two architectures under identical class-incremental settings. Code is available.



### Ego2X: Contact-aware Transfer from Egocentric Human Data to Any Robot
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24138v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24138v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:48:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 06:41:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Xinke Lyu, Bowen Yang, Zhicheng Wang
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 7 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied models depend on real interaction data, yet collecting it on physical robots is slow and labor-intensive. Recent ego-to-robot pipelines retarget first-person view human videos to robot data via transferring hand and arm motion. They judge fidelity mainly by visual appearance. However, trajectories often fail in terms of physics: fingers hover beside objects, and grippers close on empty space. We argue that how the object moves and the contacts that drive the motion count. Thus, we present Ego2X, an object-centric framework that converts monocular egocentric video into physically executable robot data across dexterous hands and parallel grippers. Ego2X centers on CORE (Contact-centric Object-interaction REpresentation), which records each contact on the object by its surface anchor, as well as relative force, grasp mode, and interaction state. Throughout the pipeline, CORE corrects hand-object drift via object-relative constraints, steers inverse kinematics toward contacts the robot must realize, and preserves held objects during arm erasure and robot compositing. On HOT3D, contact-driven correction reduces in-contact hand depth error by 33.7%, while on EgoDex, contact-driven retargeting realizes 62.9% of annotated contacts, surpassing the official HuRo pipeline (27.9%). In RoboTwin 2.0, post-training X-VLA on Ego2X episodes alone, without simulator demonstrations, raises task success rate from 40.5% zero-shot to 67.0% across 200 paired scenes. We will publicly release our conversion pipeline and CORE-annotated dataset for dexterous hands and parallel grippers.



### Inherited Adapter Experts for Replay-Free Domain-Incremental Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24145v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24145v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:53:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:53:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Gyutae Oh, Hyunmin Kong, Jitae Shin
- **Comment**: 21 pages (8 pages main text, plus references and supplementary material), 8 figures, 23 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Replay-free domain-incremental learning trains a model on the same classes in successive domains without keeping earlier images. Per-domain learners freeze a pre-trained ViT and train an additional expert for each new domain: the small set of parameters and the classification head learned on that domain. Test images carry no domain label, so a router selects one expert for each image. Recent work trains this router. However, a training-free Mahalanobis router costs the analyzed learners under 2.7 points of final accuracy relative to always selecting each image's own-domain expert. The experts, not this router, limit accuracy. Our learner, Lamarck, therefore uses this router and strengthens the experts. Its adapters, small trainable layers that correct every image token after each deep ViT block, add 4.4 points of final accuracy on the deepfake benchmark CDDB-Hard. In addition, each expert is initialized from its predecessor at no extra cost: the domains share their classes, so an image misrouted to a later expert loses less. Against twelve frozen-backbone baselines under one protocol, Lamarck is first on CDDB-Hard by 5.2 points, second on DomainNet and OfficeHome, and highest in mean accuracy.



### SEER: Semantic Evidence for Executable Region Propagation in Open-Vocabulary Semantic Segmentation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24152v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24152v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:56:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:56:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Junyang Chen, Xiao Li, Yiguo Qiao, Wenbin Li
- **Comment**: 33 pages, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Training-free open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) has made strong progress by adapting CLIP-like representations to dense prediction, but its remaining errors are often local, structured, and tied to the active label space. A successful correction must be visually plausible, legal under the target vocabulary, and executable in the segmentation score field. We propose SEER, a training-free test-time repair layer for Semantic Evidence and Executable Region propagation. SEER casts post-prediction OVSS repair as evidence-conditioned label propagation. The OVSS score space proposes compact candidate labels, a frozen VLM verifies closed-set regional evidence, and an execution gate certifies whether the evidence is legally and semantically actionable. The accepted label is then propagated by an OVSS-side policy: coherent high-confidence regions allow broad propagation, while object- or boundary-risk cases use conservative score-grounded propagation. Across eight standard OVSS benchmarks, SEER reaches 58.0 average mIoU without updating OVSS weights. Matched-input controls isolate the repair mechanism, while resource measurements report the cost of the complete frozen system.



### Game2Player: Scaling Gameplay Experience for Visual Agents through Environment-Player Co-Development
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24154v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24154v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 09:58:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 09:58:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Xirui Jiang, Yuxi Chen, Rui Yang, Feng Luo, Pengcheng Wang, Bingxuan Li, Zihao Zhao, Yushan Li, Han Wang, Manling Li, Yang Zhang, Shiyu Chang, Tong Zhang, Huan Zhang
- **Comment**: Project page: https://game2player.github.io
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Games are a rich testbed for visual agents, demanding perception, long-horizon planning, and precise control. However, collecting human gameplay is costly, and learning from gameplay videos requires inferring missing actions. Existing datasets also cover a limited range of games, often without explicit tasks or verifiable outcomes. To address these gaps, we introduce Game2Player, a framework that co-develops game environments and players to collect diverse and verifiable gameplay experience at scale. In the Game2Player loop, a Builder agent adapts existing web games, exposes their internal state, and defines tasks with state-based progress measures and success checks. A Coding Player and a VLM Player then attempt the tasks and report problems to the Builder for repair, so that play serves as both environment validation and training data collection. With Game2Player, we build 568 verified web games with 3,436 tasks, collect 15.7K trajectories (307 hours of gameplay), and hold out 50 games as the benchmark PlayerBench. To evaluate the collected experience as training data, we train VLM agents with progress-weighted SFT, assigning greater weight to steps that advance task progress. Game2Player-9B improves progress from 14.3% to 31.2% on PlayerBench and from 30.0% to 53.1% on the external GameWorld benchmark. More broadly, our work suggests a path for visual agents to scale their gameplay experience by building, verifying, and learning from existing game worlds.



### GenRF: Generative Registration-free Image Fusion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24157v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24157v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:01:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:41:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Linfeng Tang, Zhijun Fan, Yang Chen, Zizhuo Li, Hao Zhang, Jiayi Ma
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-modal image fusion combines complementary observations to improve scene visibility and perception. However, in real-world acquisition, differences in sensing principles and configurations introduce geometric discrepancies, while degradations further compromise source quality. Existing methods typically rely on explicit registration, yet large parallax complicates correspondence estimation, while mismatched fields of view leave some regions without cross-modal counterparts. Meanwhile, independent restoration underuses cross-modal information, whereas restoration-fusion methods assuming alignment remain vulnerable to misregistration. This study revisits misaligned and degraded image fusion in the era of generative foundation models, in which cross-attention enables position-agnostic complementary information aggregation, while generative priors support degradation recovery and inference in missing regions. Building on this perspective, we propose GenRF, which adapts pretrained FLUX through LoRA to jointly enhance degraded observations, infer missing cross-modal context, and fuse complementary information without explicitly estimating deformation fields. Extensive experiments on real-world infrared and visible captures show that GenRF delivers high-quality fusion directly from unregistered, degraded images without preprocessing, while improving downstream task performance under misalignment. Additional experiments demonstrate that GenRF can generalize to misaligned multi-exposure and multi-focus image fusion without task-specific fine-tuning.



### AVTR: Adaptive Multimodal Reasoning with Visual Imagination
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24161v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24161v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:05:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:05:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaoqian Shen, Yunyang Xiong, Changsheng Zhao, Saksham Suri, Chong Zhou, Zechun Liu, Zhipeng Cai, Zhuang Liu, Qi Qian, Wei Wen, Hongyu Xu, Hanxian Huang, Ernie Chang, Yanbei Chen, Vikas Chandra, Raghuraman Krishnamoorthi, Mohamed Elhoseiny, Lemeng Wu
- **Comment**: Project page: https://xiaoqian-shen.github.io/AVTR
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-Language Models increasingly rely on textual reasoning to solve advanced multimodal tasks. Despite its effectiveness, this paradigm faces two fundamental limitations. First, relying on textual reasoning alone can limit perceptual ability in vision-centric questions, where accurate answers depend on interpreting task-relevant visual evidence. Second, applying textual reasoning uniformly across inputs overlooks question-dependent variation in both reasoning modality and depth, often producing verbose and unnecessary traces when little logical inference is required. To this end, we propose Adaptive Visual-Textual Reasoning (AVTR), which introduces visual imagination as a complementary reasoning modality to textual thinking, enabling the model to revisit its visual observations and distill the salient evidence through generative visual reflection. Furthermore, we formulate reasoning-modality selection as a learned routing problem, allowing a single policy to adaptively harness the complementary strengths of visual reflection and textual reasoning in response to the perceptual and inferential demands of the input. Across 16 benchmarks, AVTR improves the average score by 4.5 points over Qwen3.5 baseline, while reducing inference latency by 33\%. It also scales effectively into a smaller size backbone, outperforming the evaluated 2B-scale baselines.



### Stabilizing Neural-to-Image Retrieval with Frequency-Domain Priors and Adaptive Supervision
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24163v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24163v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:06:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:06:13+00:00
- **Authors**: YaoYang Liu, ZhuoShi Tang, Haopeng Lai, Weijun Sun, Xiaozhao Fang, Jie Wen
- **Comment**: 12 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual neural decoding aims to infer perceived visual stimuli from brain activity, offering important insights into human visual cognition while enabling transformative applications in brain computer interfaces and artificial intelligence. Recent advances in pretrained vision-language models have provided a semantically rich representation space for this task. However, reliable alignment between EEG/MEG signals and visual embeddings remains challenging because neural responses are noisy, highly variable across trials, and subject dependent. To address this issue, we propose a unified neural-to-image retrieval framework that improves retrieval performance by aligning neural and image representations in a shared semantic space. The framework integrates dual-stream adaptation, frequency-domain calibration, and phase-guided temperature modulation to promote more robust cross-modal semantic alignment. Specifically, dual-stream adaptation models a low-dimensional shared subspace with correlation regularization to suppress redundant noise and enhance cross-modal consistency. Frequency-domain calibration conditionally modulates neural representations using amplitude and phase statistics. Phase-guided temperature modulation further adjusts the supervision strength in contrastive learning according to the stability of neural responses. Experiments on THINGS-EEG and THINGS-MEG under both intra-subject and inter-subject settings demonstrate that the proposed framework consistently outperforms strong baselines. These results suggest that robust neural-to-image retrieval under heterogeneous neural conditions benefits from a stable shared semantic space, frequency-informed representation calibration, and adaptive supervision. Code will be released at github.com/wanfkuabc/Geometry-Regularized-Brain-Embeddings.



### Gaussian Primitive Fusion for Any-Resolution Multimodal Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24164v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24164v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:06:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:06:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Baoshuo Cai, Xinrui Jiang, Bing Cao
- **Comment**: 15 pages, 8 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal image fusion integrates complementary observations from different modalities into a unified representation. Existing methods typically estimate modality contributions on a common discrete grid. When source modalities have different native resolutions, constructing such a grid generally involves resampling, which may introduce artifacts and affect subsequent fusion decisions. To address this problem, we propose Gaussian Primitive Fusion (GPF), which reformulates heterogeneous-resolution fusion in a shared continuous Gaussian space. Instead of first converting source images to a common resolution, GPF directly fits their native-resolution observations with shared Gaussian geometry and modality-specific content representations, and learns modality contributions within the resulting continuous representation. Once optimized, the fused representation is rendered at the finest native resolution without resampling the sources. With heterogeneous-resolution inputs, GPF produces clearer target contours and finer textures, with less blurring and blocking artifacts than resample-then-fuse pipelines. Quantitatively, it achieves the best results on multiple infrared-visible benchmarks against state-of-the-art fusion methods equipped with interpolation or super-resolution frontends.



### ViStep-RL: Stepwise Reinforcement Learning for Long-Horizon Visual Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24170v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24170v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:12:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:12:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Junyang Chen, Yao Qin, Xiao Li, Ningchao Wang, Yiguo Qiao, Wenbin Li, Xin Geng
- **Comment**: 19 pages, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long-horizon visual agents must do more than inspect images: they must turn observations into evidence that remains usable as a task unfolds. Existing active-perception methods teach agents where and how to look, while a final-answer reward gives little structure for deciding which observations satisfy dependent subgoals or should be retained for later use. We present ViStep-RL (Visual Stepwise Reinforcement Learning), which models execution as a dependency-linked graph of evidence-bearing subgoals. A verifier tracks which subgoals are supported by valid evidence, and changes in graph potential provide a net-progress learning signal; an active memory protocol preserves evidence across turns and links retrieval to downstream use. On ViInteract, ViStep-RL reaches 60.0% strict completion with Qwen2.5-VL-7B, compared with 10.0% for the base model and 46.5% after supervised fine-tuning. Controlled studies show that incremental task-state rewards outperform cumulative rewards by 3.2 points, while active evidence writing improves strict completion by 7.75 points over automatic writing. Under a common interaction framework, the task-trained ViStep-RL policy achieves 59.5% answer accuracy, 24.0 points above the strongest of six general-purpose reference models. Held-out episodes confirm the gain at both Qwen scales, and gains extend to three external visual reasoning benchmarks.



### Efficient World-Model Planning via Physics-Guided Search Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24171v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24171v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:14:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:11:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongyu Zhang, Xiaoshuai Hao, Heye Huang, Qingwen Meng, Wenjun Shi, Jianao Xu, Xin Hu, Xuanru Jin, Jingyi Zhang, Baicang Guo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World models support autonomous driving by evaluating candidate trajectories through predicted future scene evolution. However, accurate future prediction does not ensure road-compliant execution; when all candidates violate road boundaries, re-ranking cannot select a feasible trajectory. We propose Physics-Guided Search Distillation (PGSD), a single-view planning framework that combines future representations and execution feedback for verified beyond-pool repair through three components: (1) Execution-Aware Selection checks ego-vehicle road compliance under simulated execution and selects the highest-scoring feasible trajectory; (2) a Clearance-Aware Search Teacher generates beyond-pool corrections and selects demonstrations with larger minimum road-boundary clearance during execution to buffer imitation errors; and (3) a Future-Conditioned Repair Student predicts one correction from rewarder features and execution feedback for single-rollout verification. The pretrained planner and rewarder remain frozen; no future scenes are generated online. On NAVSIM v1, single-view PGSD achieves 90.97 PDMS, exceeding all compared prior single-view planners and retaining 73.2% of the search teacher's PDMS gain over execution-aware selection. On NAVSIM v2 navhard, it reaches 38.96 EPDMS, improving on WorldDrive by 11.24 points (40.5%). The student replaces up to ten search rollouts with a single verification rollout per repair trigger. HUGSIM Hard examples provide qualitative evidence of zero-shot transfer.



### ART: Bridging the Embodiment Gap in 3D Human-to-Robot Affordance Transfer
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24182v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24182v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:22:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:22:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Zichen Zhang, Wei Zhai, Xuji Zhang, Yang Cao, Yu Kang
- **Comment**: 15 pages, 14 figures, 5 tables. Project page: https://derek0907.github.io/ART/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D affordance acts as a critical link between perception and action, providing priors for robotic manipulation. While existing approaches predominantly leverage human priors as direct references, they often overlook that affordance is not an object-intrinsic property but is inherently coupled with the agent's embodiment. Due to the structural and morphological disparities between human hands and robot end-effectors, directly applying human priors causes severe incompatibility for robotic execution. Motivated by this, we introduce a novel task setting, 3D Human-to-Robot Affordance Transfer, which addresses the affordance misalignment arising from distinct embodiment structures. To support this task, we construct the Human-Robot Paired Affordance Dataset (HPAD), the first large-scale 3D affordance benchmark in which every object is annotated with both human-prior and simulation-validated robot labels. HPAD covers 2,137 objects across 80 categories and 13 atomic action primitives, yielding over 27,000 object-action pairs. On top of HPAD, we propose the 3D Affordance tRansfer neTwork (ART). ART first filters geometric false positives in the human prior via a reliability gating stream, then reconstructs the prior into a robot-actionable distribution through hierarchical reconstruction and a graph-diffusion refinement that resolves the topological mismatch between human and robot affordances. Experiments on HPAD demonstrate the reliability of the proposed task and the superiority of ART. We further validate ART on a 6-DoF arm with a parallel-jaw gripper in a 2,400-execution benchmark across 12 objects and 4 action categories; ART attains the best success rate (86.5% over its 480 trials), confirming the practicality of the transfer paradigm. Project page: https://derek0907.github.io/ART/



### VideoACE: Benchmarking Agentic Video Understanding through High-Frame-Rate Actions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24184v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.MM
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24184v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:26:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:26:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Luoyuan Zhang, Xuanyu Zheng, Yancheng Long, Haoyang Xu, Bin Wen, Tingting Gao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Agentic video understanding requires models to actively interact with video environments to gather evidence and form judgments. Evaluating this capability therefore requires assessing whether models can translate opportunities for interaction into better understanding. We introduce VideoACE, a benchmark for assessing video understanding under conditions that support sustained interaction. We focus on high-frame-rate action videos, where rapid, subtle changes make targeted observation especially valuable. The benchmark comprises 1,000 multiple-choice questions spanning six categories of fine-grained action understanding, accompanied by temporal evidence annotations and user hints screened for answer leakage. Its evaluation environment combines video inspection tools with a user simulator, allowing models to adjust temporal coverage, sampling density, and spatial detail, and to revisit evidence and revise their judgments under simulated user feedback. We evaluate single-pass observation, autonomous tool use, and tool use combined with user feedback to examine how effectively models exploit these opportunities. Evaluation of five models reveals distinct patterns in how they acquire evidence and respond to opportunities for further interaction. These findings establish VideoACE as a testbed for examining agentic video understanding beyond performance under a fixed observation setting.



### Monitoring Unplanned Construction Using Satellite Change Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24190v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24190v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:28:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:28:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Harsh Kashyap, Taranum Wasu
- **Comment**: 22 pages, 11 figures, 5 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Cities need land for homes, shops, roads, and public services. New construction changes that land. It can harm natural areas and water bodies, add to waste, and strain public services. NITI Aayog's 2021 report stated that 65% of 7,933 urban settlements in India had no master plan. That number tells us about planning coverage at the time. It does not tell us whether any single building was authorised. Artificial intelligence, which lets computers learn patterns from data, can help find physical changes by comparing satellite images taken on different dates. We test simple methods on two public datasets from outside India. First, we turn each image into a set of numbers using a pretrained neural network, a model already trained to recognise patterns in images. We feed those numbers to standard machine-learning classifiers that predict land categories and physical changes. The methods reach about 95 percent accuracy at spotting built-up land and about 83 percent accuracy at saying whether a small pair of images shows change, where each pair gets one label. We also train a small neural network on a standard change-detection benchmark. Separately, we run a preliminary descriptive check on an Agra sample of 48 patches, 7 of them positive. The results show that satellite images carry information about physical change. They do not show whether construction was planned or permitted.



### TrajWAM: Trajectory-Conditioned Future Learning for Vision-Language Driving
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24192v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24192v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:28:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:28:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuerui Fang, Gulimila Kezierbieke, Chen Wangxing, Hui Li
- **Comment**: 22 pages, including supplementary material. Code and model weights: https://github.com/FangWD520/TrajWAM
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World Action Models (WAMs) connect future scene prediction with action planning for autonomous driving. Each driving record pairs an executed ego trajectory with its observed future, providing a factual outcome for learning this connection. We present TrajWAM, a trajectory-conditioned WAM that improves vision-language driving through auxiliary bird's-eye-view (BEV) future learning. An ego-trajectory query conditions DINOv3-aligned BEV predictions from shared planner representations. Training combines factual reconstruction under the executed trajectory with one-sided feature separation under an unlabeled perturbed trajectory. Together, these objectives train the shared planner to generate driving waypoints. Native inference directly generates waypoints and bypasses the auxiliary future-prediction branch. On all 220 Bench2Drive routes, TrajWAM achieves 91.53 Driving Score and 75.45% Success Rate, exceeding a schedule-matched planning-only fine-tuning baseline by 5.31 Driving Score points and eight successful routes.



### WM^MLLM: Turning MLLMs into World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24193v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24193v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:29:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:29:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Baiang Li, Enze Xie, Jincheng Yu, Jin Wang, Haozhe Liu, Yitong Li, Juanxi Tian, Felix Heide
- **Comment**: 25 pages, 14 figures, 8 tables; project page: https://ztmotalee.github.io/wmmllm/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video world models keep the world in the context of a generator, so a revisit is synthesized again and earlier edits can drift or disappear. We present WM^MLLM, the first interactive world model built on multimodal large language models (MLLMs), for persistent, editable world generation from a single image. WM^MLLM keeps the world state outside every generator, as a panorama with a revision history, and lets the MLLMs write it: they turn each request into operations on this state, select where a local edit is written, and check every candidate before it becomes the next state. Image generators only propose views, and video only presents them. Users can walk to new places and return, edit objects through text or a box, place subjects, and change lighting, weather or season. By construction, a revisit at the same revision reproduces the earlier view exactly, a local edit leaves every panorama pixel outside its region unchanged, and each request starts from the result of the previous one. Against six world model baselines on MBench-A, WM^MLLM has the highest mean on all four environment-recovery metrics (Epipolar 91.5 versus 0.1 to 74.0) and completes the most camera round trips (85.3% versus at most 50.9%). It has the best value in all 15 CompBench columns against 15 baselines. On 112 local edits judged blind, selecting the region to write after generation raises completion from 34.8% to 83.0%. On 258 WBench and MBench-A items, all 46 revisits recover their earlier views, and all 97 local edits that pass the check leave panorama pixels outside their region unchanged.



### From Neural Tokens to Language: Dual-View EEG Semantic Bridging for Brain-to-Text Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24197v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24197v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:32:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:32:57+00:00
- **Authors**: YaoYang Liu, Jun Dai, ZhuoShi Tang, Weijun Sun, Xiaozhao Fang, Jie Wen
- **Comment**: 30 pages, 15 pages of supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visually evoked EEG-to-text generation aims to produce natural-language descriptions from neural responses elicited by visual stimuli. Existing approaches typically compress EEG signals into a single global representation and directly project it into the hidden space of a large language model (LLM), which may limit the preservation of complementary visual semantics under noisy and subject-dependent EEG signals. We propose a dual-view semantic token bridging framework that exploits raw images and sketch images as complementary visual supervision to learn raw-oriented and sketch-oriented EEG semantic token streams. To interface these structured neural representations with an LLM, we introduce a branch-specific semantic query bridge that aggregates each token stream into compact semantic slots and converts them into LLM-compatible soft prompts. A progressive adaptation strategy further transitions the learned visual-semantic interface from image-supported training to image-free EEG-conditioned generation. Experiments demonstrate strong subject-wise EEG classification performance and competitive EEG-to-text generation across multiple LLM backbones. Ablation, branch-level representation, and EEG-label disentanglement analyses further show that the two EEG streams learn complementary view-oriented representations and that EEG-derived prompts provide sample-specific information beyond category labels.



### SpiderMark: Hybrid Watermarking for Generative AI via Algorithmic Injection and Learning-Based Verification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24200v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24200v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:36:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:36:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Chihcheng Hsieh, Yang Song, Flora Salim, Martin Bell, Jacinto C. Nascimento, Catarina Moreira
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The proliferation of diffusion-based generative models has intensified the need for reliable methods to trace and verify the provenance of AI-generated images. Existing watermarking approaches for diffusion models typically rely on isotropic frequency patterns and handcrafted detection metrics, which are prone to directional vulnerability and threshold instability across diverse image transformations. We propose SpiderMark, a hybrid watermarking framework that combines algorithmic frequency-domain injection with learning-based probabilistic verification, requiring no modifications to pretrained diffusion model weights. SpiderMark embeds a structured spider-web pattern, comprising radial spokes and concentric polygonal rings, into the Fourier representation of the initial latent noise, and trains a lightweight ResNet-18 verifier augmented with auxiliary similarity cues (PSNR and L1) to produce a continuous watermark confidence score for watermark detection. Across nine evaluation conditions, SpiderMark achieves the highest fixed-threshold Accuracy in eight, while retaining strong detection on unseen COCO prompts without verifier retraining. CLIP similarity and FID measurements show that these detection gains are accompanied by small measured differences in prompt alignment and distributional image quality. SpiderMark establishes that structured watermark geometry and learned probabilistic verification can be unified into a single, practical framework, bridging the gap between imperceptibility, robustness, and real-world verifiability for generative AI content attribution.



### DetailScale-SR: Decoupling Restoration and Continuous Detail Control for Real-World Super-Resolution
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24212v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24212v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:51:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:51:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Shengchuan Gao, Zhongjie Duan, Yingda Chen, Jingjing Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Real-world image super-resolution often seeks the most detailed plausible reconstruction, although more detail is not always desirable. The same degraded image may require restrained restoration, faithful reconstruction, or enhanced visual detail according to user intent. We present DetailScale-SR, a preference-conditioned framework that continuously controls reconstructed detail for a fixed low-resolution input without altering the scene being restored. Rather than relying on post-processing or repeatedly rewriting prompts, it learns a continuous family of reconstructions while separating content recovery from detail preference. This enables a smooth transition from conservative restoration to richly textured results under consistent input and semantic constraints. Its controllable range also extends beyond conventional restoration: DetailScale-SR can enhance high-frequency structures and generate perceptually richer textures than the paired high-resolution reference. Text restoration is another distinct strength. For text-rich scenes, especially Chinese posters and signs, it recovers more legible and character-faithful text than prior generative SR methods under matched textual guidance. DetailScale-SR therefore unifies continuous detail control, beyond-reference enhancement, and reliable recovery of semantically important content. Our model is available at https://modelscope.cn/models/DiffSynth-Studio/Qwen-Image-2.1-SR



### Target-Consistent Action Guidance via Semi-Unbalanced Optimal Transport for Drifting World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24222v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24222v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:58:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:58:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Md Abtahi Majeed Chowdhury, Md Rifat Ur Rahman, Md Sakib Hossain Shovon, Jaesik Choi, Minhyuk Sung
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Action-conditioned world models let robots imagine the outcomes of their actions for planning and offline policy evaluation, but these uses need many rollouts, which multi-step diffusion makes expensive. Drifting models move the iteration from inference to training and generate samples in one network pass. DriftWorld brought this paradigm to action-conditioned world models; to strengthen action following, it repels predictions from the current frame, a construction borrowed from classifier-free guidance. We show that this accentuation has a target bias: the push persists even when a prediction matches the recorded future, in kernel, balanced, and unbalanced Wasserstein drifting fields alike. We introduce Mass-Share Accentuation (MSA) to remove this bias. MSA treats the current frame as a counterfactual destination competing with the recorded future in a semi-relaxed optimal transport problem; subtracting the closed-form share of the correct future makes the guidance vanish at the target, and the remainder is shared by all predictions. We prove that the recorded future is the unique fixed point along the motion direction for collapsed predictions, and that the guidance fades with prediction error. On Push-T, Robomimic, and BridgeV2, where trained models under-react rather than overshoot, MSA improves action ranking, response slope, and moving-region error over latent-space baselines at no inference cost, though these benchmarks do not isolate bias removal as the cause.



### Training Denoising Models of Any Architecture Without Stored Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24223v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24223v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 10:58:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 10:58:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Christos Koutlis, Ioannis Kompatsiaris, Symeon Papadopoulos
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Can a pretrained denoising model replace the dataset used to train it? We investigate this question through teacher-supervised training, showing for the first time that continuous-time diffusion and flow models of any architecture and size can be trained from random initialization without stored images. Our students regress the teacher's velocity on a rolling pool of noisy samples, advanced along trajectories of random step counts to obtain independent, dense and approximately continuous noise levels. This saves considerable storage for large datasets and applies even when the data are unreleased or private. Our experiments include 12 teachers of 6 families and 6 datasets. Our method outperforms standard training of the same network on CIFAR-10 for students 4x smaller than the teacher, comes within 0.75 to 1.92 FID of it at the teacher's size and surpasses training on teacher-generated images and step reduction methods without teacher-based initialization. Our code is available at https://github.com/mever-team/ddm.



### Psychometrics-Inspired Coreset Selection for Image Quality Assessment
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24233v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24233v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:08:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:08:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Susu Jiang, Xuelin Shen, Baoliang Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Collecting large-scale subjective annotations remains a critical bottleneck in training accurate image quality assessment (IQA) models. Coreset selection reduces this burden by selecting representative images before annotation. However, diversity in a visual feature space does not necessarily reflect diversity in perceptual quality, raising a fundamental question about which quality-related factors the representation should encode. We propose GRAM, a quality-aware representation learning framework inspired by the graded response model (GRM) from psychometrics. GRM describes how an individual's latent ability determines the probabilities of reaching successive assessment grades, with response sensitivity and grade thresholds governing this relationship. In this paper, we find that this principle naturally aligns with perceptual quality assessment, where the impact of image degradation depends on image content and distortion characteristics. Building on this connection, GRAM models quality judgments through three complementary factors: latent quality, content-dependent response sensitivity, and distortion-dependent grade thresholds. These factors jointly determine the probabilities of attaining different quality grades and the resulting expected quality score. The learned parameters provide a structured quality-aware representation that complements general visual features for coreset selection. Experiments on nine datasets demonstrate improvements across different selection strategies, downstream IQA models, and annotation budgets. The code will be made publicly available.



### ViLFlow: Parallel Vision-Language Generation with Continuous Latent Flows
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24240v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24240v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:19:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:19:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Kevin Zhai, Bian Sun, Zhenyi Wang, Song Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language generation has predominantly relied on autoregressive and discrete diffusion models. Autoregressive models generate tokens sequentially, while discrete diffusion models typically predict tokens independently within each denoising step. We propose ViLFlow (Vision-Language Flow), a continuous-flow vision-language model that combines joint modeling of output embeddings with parallel token decoding. ViLFlow adapts pretrained continuous language generation to visual question answering and image captioning through learned image guidance and prediction-aware token decoding. A visually adapted teacher provides image-guided predictions as additional denoising supervision. The denoiser learns to reproduce these predictions directly, retaining one image-conditioned prediction per generation step. We train the token decoder on denoiser predictions and paired reference text to improve the conversion of continuous outputs into words. The decoder can be adapted independently, improving answering and captioning without further denoiser training. For captioning, it also supplies text feedback during late denoising, allowing decoded words to inform subsequent refinement. ViLFlow outperforms autoregressive and masked-diffusion baselines by up to 8.46 percentage points on VQAv2 and 4.50 points on GQA without GQA training. On COCO Karpathy test, it improves CIDEr by 16.25 over masked diffusion and 17.81 over sampled autoregression.



### Joint Image Denoising and Interpolation via Mixed Graph Filtering
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24242v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV, eess.SP
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24242v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:21:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:21:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Gene Cheung, Niruhan Viswarupan, Romisaa Elsaidy
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Images often require both denoising and interpolation, yet treating the two tasks separately is suboptimal: interpolation introduces correlated noise that complicates subsequent denoising, while denoising may remove image details useful for interpolation. In this paper, we study joint image denoising and interpolation from a mixed graph filtering perspective, where denoising is modeled using undirected graph filters and interpolation using directed graph filters. We first establish that, under mild conditions, any (pseudo-)linear denoiser and interpolator can be interpreted as solution filters of maximum a posteriori (MAP) estimation problems regularized by undirected and directed graph smoothness priors, respectively. Guided by these results, we formulate graph-based joint denoising and interpolation problems using both directed and undirected graph structures. We characterize when joint denoising/interpolation reduces to a separable pipeline and when a genuinely coupled optimization is required. For the non-separable case, we recover sparse graph operators from given denoisers and interpolators, yielding an efficient linear-system formulation solvable via conjugate gradient. Experimental results demonstrate that our proposed joint framework consistently outperforms separate denoising and interpolation pipelines across multiple denoiser-interpolator combinations.



### End-to-End Landsat Land Surface Temperature Reconstruction with AlphaEarth Embeddings
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24243v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/lgrs.2026.3754195
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24243v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:21:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:21:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Shengjie Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Land surface temperature (LST) data at Landsat's 30 m resolution is important for many applications, but cloud contamination prevents its spatially continuous observations. Here, we propose A$^2$E$^2$, an end-to-end U-Net that integrates annual temperature cycle (ATC), coarse LST from Earth system modeling, and AlphaEarth embeddings for Landsat LST reconstruction. The ATC supplies a physics-guided thermal prior, the coarse LST provides a temporal background, and the U-Net converts the embedding field into day-specific fine-scale correction using observed pixels. Experimental results on four seasonally distributed Landsat 8/9 scenes over New York City in 2022 show that A$^2$E$^2$ achieves 0.78 K mean absolute error and 1.01 K root mean squared error, reducing error by 60.1% relative to baseline method without spatial correction and by 3.7% relative to the same U-Net guided by annual mean spectral reflectance. Sensitivity analysis shows stable performance regardless of distance to clear pixels and percentage of blocked pixels. Independent validation with ECOSTRESS LST shows that Earth foundation model embeddings have better performance than annual mean spectral bands for LST reconstruction.



### Allocentric Memory, Egocentric Acting: A Dual-Frame VLA for Urban Aerial Navigation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24247v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24247v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:24:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:24:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Haotian Xu, Yue Hu, Zhengqiu Zhu, Tuo Feng, Wenguan Wang, Quanjun Yin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Language-goal aerial navigation requires a UAV to reach a destination specified in natural language using observations collected during flight. A key challenge is linking changing egocentric perception with spatial evidence anchored to stable locations for long-range navigation. We propose CityNav-VLA, a dual-frame vision-language-action model for urban navigation integrating dynamic egocentric context with persistent allocentric spatial memory. The UAV incrementally accumulates visual observations on a fixed north-up map initialized with geographic and landmark priors, preserving evidence across viewpoint changes. A dynamic query conditioned on the instruction, current observations, UAV state, and motion history retrieves relevant memory evidence to predict a map-frame target. A deterministic controller converts this target into body-frame commands using the UAV's current pose, connecting global reasoning with egocentric execution. To reduce target fluctuations over long horizons, early goal commitment (EGC) fixes the execution target to the coordinate-wise median of the first $K$ predictions without extra trainable parameters. On Test Unseen, CityNav-VLA reduces NE by 21.2% and improves SR and SPL by 15.8% and 30.9%, respectively. It also reduces NE by 6.2% and improves SR and SPL by 5.6% and 23.2%, respectively, relative to the former SOTA after dataset refinement. Cumulative ablations show that navigation-map features yield the largest gains, while metric coordinates further improve endpoint accuracy. With a 4B-scale backbone, inference averages 0.68 s per decision with 8.6 GB peak memory on a single A100, presenting remarkable model efficiency.



### One Shot, Any View: High-Fidelity Video Reshooting with Geometry Guidance and Visual References
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24253v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24253v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:31:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:31:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Kai Huang, Runze Xu, Hui Ren, Taiyi Wu, Xinyuan Li, Xun Gao, Quande Liu, Dong Tang, Tongtong Shen, Xuefeng Su, Alan Zhao
- **Comment**: Technical report. 19 pages, 14 figures, 3 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Filming a scene from an additional angle after production usually requires a costly reshoot. We present One Shot Any View, a system that takes a recorded clip and generates a reshot video of the same performance along a user-specified camera trajectory. As in recent geometry-grounded methods, the system conditions generation on the source video and an explicit 3D prior, namely a point video rendered along the requested trajectory from a feed-forward reconstruction of the source video. We target human-centric cinematic videos, which are often shot with long lenses, shallow depth of field, and low-key lighting, all of which degrade feed-forward reconstruction. Existing geometry-grounded methods tend to inherit the degraded point video, and their output quality drops visibly. Two designs make such point videos usable. First, in addition to rendered videos of 3D assets, we train on real-world multi-camera videos, whose point videos are produced by the same reconstruction pipeline as at inference. This training-inference consistency enables the generator to take only the composition of people and scene elements from the point video, and their appearance from the source video. Second, a geometry-anchored RoPE offset keeps the generated video temporally synchronized with the source while largely preventing the output from copying the degraded appearance of the point video. Meanwhile, since the generator is a pretrained reference-to-video (R2V) model, the system also accepts optional character and scene reference images, which supply the appearance of regions that the source never shows. In both objective and human evaluations, One Shot Any View outperforms existing reshooting methods.



### Spark-H3: Adaptive Block Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24256v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24256v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:32:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:32:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Zecheng Tang, Jiaye Fu, Youtian Lin, Xinyao Rao, Liuxuan Sun, Jian Zhang, Yao Yao, Wei Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long video sequences make attention a substantial cost in video diffusion models. Block sparse attention (BSA) reduces this cost by computing selected blocks exactly while compressing the remaining blocks. However, fixed token grouping can place tokens with dissimilar attention preferences in the same group, while uniform key averaging can underestimate the attention mass of compressed blocks. We introduce Spark-Attn, a training-free BSA method comprising Spark-Reblock and Spark-Reweight. Spark-Reblock recursively groups tokens by attention preference while preserving regular block shapes for efficient GPU execution. Spark-Reweight uses the global mean query as an anchor to construct attention-weighted key-value representations and logit biases for the compressed blocks, calibrating their attention mass. We instantiate Spark-Attn in MiniMax-H3 as Spark-H3. At 10% exact-block density, Spark-H3 delivers a 2.49$\times$ DiT speedup and reaches 24.81 dB PSNR against the corresponding dense outputs on MiniMax-H3 (20 denoising steps, 345 frames, 1344$\times$768). Increasing the density to 20% further improves PSNR to 26.76 dB while still achieving a 2.26$\times$ DiT speedup. These results demonstrate that Spark-H3 achieves a state-of-the-art quality-efficiency trade-off for training-free BSA on video diffusion models.



### Do Thinking VLMs Need to Keep Looking at Long Videos? Write It Down, Then Look Away
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24259v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24259v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:42:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:46:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Pengyu Li, Zhitao Gao, Lingling Zhang, Heng Wang, Chengqian Ma, WenHao Wang, Wenxuan Huang, Chen Zheng, Jun Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) reason about long videos over hundreds to thousands of tokens. Every decoding step re-reads the key-value (KV) cache of the entire video. Must the model keep looking while it reasons? In LookAway, the model first writes timestamped notes on what the question needs and then puts the video away. Once the notes close, the inference engine hides the video's KV cache for the rest of the response. Across both hybrid and pure-attention VLMs, dependence on the video is concentrated early in the response. On Qwen3.6-27B, writing notes preserves accuracy better than free reasoning at the same withdrawal step. Replacing them with another video's notes harms accuracy, whereas keeping the video visible alongside the model's own notes adds no significant benefit. Without additional training, LookAway nearly matches full-video thinking accuracy at 0.12-0.13 times its modeled decode cost. To shorten this initial look, we fine-tune the model on its own correct LookAway responses. Reinforcement learning then rewards correct answers while penalizing KV reads. The trained model puts the video away after a median of 66-68 tokens. Across three held-out benchmarks, it writes more tokens than an identically trained full-video model yet reads only 27-35% as many KV positions at matched accuracy.



### Look Closer, Look Back, or Wait for Agentic Streaming Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24267v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24267v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:48:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:40:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Pengyu Li, Zhitao Gao, Lingling Zhang, Wenxuan Huang, YiCheng Xiao, Chen Zheng, Jun Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Streaming video models must answer questions about a live video under strict memory and latency limits. Feeding only the latest few frames keeps present perception sharp, whereas adding past frames dilutes attention on the current scene and triggers hallucinations. Recent cascade methods retrieve past frames when uncertain, but they still fail when current frames are blurry or when decisive future clues have not appeared yet. We introduce ActiveForager, a streaming video agent that maintains a clean short working window and gathers extra evidence only on demand. For each query, the agent either answers directly, zooms into high-resolution recent frames, recalls past events from memory, or waits for upcoming frames. When recalling history, a shared-weight sub-agent verifies candidate clips in isolation and returns a compact evidence summary. A stream-decoupled attention mask then keeps historical evidence from interfering with present visual tokens. We train this policy using counterfactual information gain, which rewards a tool call only when the gathered evidence makes the true answer more likely and prevents tool collapse across all actions. Across Qwen2.5-VL-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B on OVO-Bench, ActiveForager improves real-time perception, historical recall, and proactive response at once, lifting overall accuracy on Qwen3-VL-8B from 47.6% to 65.9%. It also doubles zero-shot accuracy on the multi-hop Video-Holmes benchmark while adding minimal visual tokens per query.



### Semantic Guidance for Joint Infrared-Visible Video Fusion and Frame Interpolation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24277v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24277v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:54:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:54:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Yushen Xu, Xiaosong Li, Huaming Wu, Dongwei Ren
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Infrared and visible video fusion at high frame rates requires recovering missing observations when camera rates differ or fall below the desired output rate. Cascading interpolation and fusion can accumulate errors and cause brightness fluctuations. Motion and occlusion further complicate content selection, particularly when missing visible frames also remove direct semantic guidance. We propose SAFI, which uses semantics at the target time to guide joint interpolation and fusion. When the visible frame rate exceeds the infrared rate, available visible frames provide semantic context directly. At times without visible observations, motion guides local matching, and propagation recovers semantics from neighboring frames. Semantic context complements aligned image features at the target time, while matching confidence helps select reliable infrared and visible information. Reconstruction and fusion share source selection within each scale, allowing supervision on missing frames to guide fusion. To preserve visible appearance and reduce flicker, we bound thermal enhancement and penalize brightness inconsistencies across frames while accounting for changes in thermal contrast. Experiments on three datasets show improved visual quality with less computation than cascaded baselines and consistent temporal gains when input rates differ. SAFI maintains strong downstream tracking performance despite missing frames. The code is available at https://github.com/XylonXu01/SAFI.



### CROMPT: Adapting CLIP via Cross-Modal Prompting for Robust Zero-Shot Generalization under Adverse Weather
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24284v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24284v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 11:59:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:59:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Aniruddh Sikdar, Arya Kishor, Snehashis Dhibar, Suresh Sundaram
- **Comment**: 17 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Domain adaptation for semantic segmentation is challenging when target-domain images are unavailable during training. Zero-shot domain adaptation addresses this by adapting models using only textual descriptions of the target domain. However, existing prompt-learning methods are not well suited for ResNet-based CLIP vision backbones or pixel-level cross-modal feature alignment. We propose CROMPT, a prompt-learning framework for zero-shot domain adaptation in semantic segmentation. CROMPT introduces Cross-Modal Feature Augmentation (CROMA), combining Random Visual Prompting with Hybrid Textual Prompting (HTP) to jointly adapt visual and textual representations. We further propose CROMA-VL, which leverages vision-language models to generate domain-conditioned prompts, and a progressive easy-to-hard adaptation strategy for improved domain invariance. Experiments on two benchmark datasets demonstrate consistent improvements over strong baselines and state-of-the-art methods, achieving 1.63-2.63% mIoU gains while maintaining inference-time complexity. These results establish CROMPT as an effective and efficient framework for zero-shot multi-domain semantic segmentation. OpenReview: https://openreview.net/forum?id=FxqFsIL685



### AnchorUp: Anchoring Feature Upsampling on a Classical Interpolation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24296v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24296v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:08:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:08:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Kotaro Horikawa, Keisuke Toida, Junya Komori, Kazuhiro Hotta
- **Comment**: Accepted to WACV 2027. Code: https://github.com/KotaroHorikawa/AnchorUp
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision foundation models produce semantically strong features on a coarse patch grid, and dense prediction needs them at pixel resolution. Existing learned upsamplers close that gap by regressing the high-resolution feature map from the low-resolution one, typically through cross-attention driven by the input image. We observe that most of what they regress needs no learning: plain bilinear interpolation already reproduces the smooth regions of the target, and recent benchmarks even report bilinear and bicubic on par with several learned upsamplers. The rest is mostly edges and fine details, which suggests that these learned upsamplers spend much of their capacity re-computing the smooth content that classical interpolation provides. We therefore introduce AnchorUp, which stops predicting the high-resolution feature map and predicts only its correction from the interpolation. It keeps a parameter-free classical upsampler as an anchor, obtains a second prediction from a small guidance network, and blends the two through a single learnable scalar gate. Because the anchor supplies the smooth content, the guidance network only has to correct what remains. An analysis of the learned correction confirms that it concentrates on edges and fine details, and so the network can be small: AnchorUp uses 167,425 parameters, a quarter of the budget of the strongest baseline, NAF, and runs at 64% of its latency. Trained once on DINOv3 with only the first of NAF's two training stages, and evaluated on that backbone plus six held-out ones, it is already on par with NAF trained through both stages on Pascal VOC and Cityscapes. Our method surpasses it on DAVIS-2017 video object segmentation; the gain holds on every backbone and is larger in contour accuracy F. Code is available at https://github.com/KotaroHorikawa/AnchorUp.



### Fuse to Recognize the Unseen: Class-Relative Infrared-Visible Fusion for Open-Vocabulary Semantic Segmentation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24316v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24316v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:22:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:22:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Zehao Gao, Jie Feng, Wenda Zhao, Junpeng Zhang, Ronghua Shang, Weisheng Dong
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visible-infrared image fusion leverages complementary thermal cues to enhance visual perception, particularly under challenging illumination. However, such perceptual complementarity does not readily translate into semantic gains in open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS), where CLIP-based models are predominantly pretrained on RGB imagery. Directly introducing IR information may instead disrupt RGB-language alignment, turning promising thermal cues into semantic interference that undermines unseen-class recognition. Our pixel-level RGB-IR semantic analysis reveals that class-relevant thermal cues can correct certain RGB misclassifications while preserving reliable RGB predictions, motivating RGB-anchored, class-relative thermal cue selection. Based on this insight, we propose EmergeFuse, an infrared-visible fusion framework for OVSS that selectively converts complementary thermal evidence into unseen-class semantic corrections. Specifically, class-relative cue selection evaluates RGB-IR semantic responses and calibrated thermal evidence relative to RGB predictions, generating class-relative correction requests through competitive selection. Text-contrast image rectification then translates these selected requests into semantic rectification directions through text contrasts, guiding evidence-gated feature updates to realize complementary thermal evidence for unseen-class recognition. Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate gains of up to 4.755% in unseen-class mIoU and 15.730% in strict complementary conversion rate over existing fusion methods, while maintaining competitive fusion quality.



### Pixel2Perspective: A Benchmark for Multi-Modal and Multi-View Generalization in Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24327v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24327v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:29:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:29:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Aditya Gandhamal, Aniruddh Sikdar, Arya Kishor, Suresh Sundaram
- **Comment**: 16 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied AI systems, such as drones and autonomous vehicles, improve navigation by enhancing perception in environments containing unseen objects. While Visual Language Models (VLMs) offer strong image understanding, their real-world application is underexplored. For effective deployment, open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) models, leveraging models like CLIP to assign pixel-level labels from text, must be robust to outdoor conditions, adverse weather, and varying viewpoints. Despite their potential, these models encounter significant challenges in cross-domain generalization, hindering their practical use in embodied AI applications. In this work, we present Pixel2Perspective, a benchmark for multi-angle object localization and open-vocabulary segmentation across both aerial and ground perspectives. This benchmark supports thorough evaluation across different viewpoints and sensor modalities. We assess foundational models like CLIP and advanced OVSS models using a curated dataset that spans varied outdoor conditions, sim-to-real scenarios, and multiple sensors, including infrared, to support the development of robust and generalizable perception systems. We also present a novel framework, Multi-Source RGB-IR Uncertainty Quantification (MSRIQ), which jointly models aleatoric and epistemic uncertainties across RGB and infrared imagery. This approach supports robust evaluation of open-vocabulary segmentation models across diverse environmental conditions. Our work sets a robustness-driven benchmark, providing key insights and laying the foundation for future research in open-world embodied AI perception. The benchmark and project page are available at https://aniruddhsikdar1.github.io/publications/pixel2perspective/



### Can Weight-Space Programming Alter Learned Ethical Priorities?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24330v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24330v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:31:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:31:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Kai Yi Ji
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We investigate whether a weight edit can alter how a model trades off honoring a commitment against benefiting more people. In a concept-supervised text model, we alter fitted choice weights so that the benefit required to override a formal pledge is larger than for an ordinary promise. Across six independently trained text readers, the edit selects the larger group in both orders on 100% of targeted ordinary conflicts and 83.3% of targeted formal conflicts. It preserves the source model's success rates on smaller conflicts and its factual accuracy. The remaining formal-conflict failures coincide with errors reading a beneficiary number. A separate classifier demonstrates why this distinction matters: reusing a factual answer passes an unconditional choice test but fails to preserve small-gap promises. In a YOLO11n pilot on COCO128, a bias edit increased small-target matches from 3/43 to 9/43, and an equivalent score calibration reproduced the retained detections. These exploratory results show a way to test selective changes to learned priorities in a model with an exposed trade-off mechanism. Whether the method can discover such changes in less structured language models remains open.



### OCTR2A: Real-Time RGB-to-BEV Trajectory Projection and Forecasting at Urban Intersections
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24352v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24352v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:47:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:47:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Emanuele Iacobelli, Simone Teglia, Francesco Pro, Claudia Melis Tonti, Irene Amerini
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Trajectory forecasting at urban intersections is often studied assuming that reliable bird's-eye-view (BEV) motion histories are available, which in practice requires costly resources such as LiDAR, aerial views, HD maps, or calibrated infrastructure. We address this upstream perception-to-forecasting problem by reconstructing and predicting agents' BEV trajectories only from paired opposite-corner RGB cameras mounted on existing urban infrastructure at variable heights at inference time.
