We introduce the Opposite-Corner Trajectory Reconstruction and Anticipation Architecture (OCTR2A), a modular pipeline integrating per-view detection with class refinement, geometric cross-camera matching, transformer-based BEV projection, and egocentric GRU forecasting, trained in cascade and mainly supervised with a soft multi-scale spatial-overlap loss. Since no public benchmark provides this sensing configuration, we introduce R3ST++, a Blender-based synthetic benchmark built from SIND trajectories that provides synchronized opposite-corner views, cross-view correspondences, and BEV ground truth, while also serving as a deployment-support simulator for site-specific fine-tuning. Experiments on these datasets show that, with 12 observed and 12 predicted frames at 10\,Hz, OCTR2A achieves projection~-~forecasting ADE/FDE of 0.144/0.140~-~0.154/0.241~meters in real time on a single consumer GPU. Evaluated on ground-truth BEV histories, the forecasting module improves over the strongest state-of-the-art methods on SIND by 66.7\%/41.7\% ADE/FDE. Transfer experiments on TUMTraf-V2X show that R3ST++ pretraining supports adaptation to real-world roadside domains with different sensing configurations.



### Towards Pose-Robust Few-Shot Vision-Language-Action Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24360v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24360v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:54:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:54:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Wenhao Li, Zhongyi Han, Yuhao Wei, Liang Han, Yilong Yin, Liqiang Nie
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 13 figures, 17 tables (including supplementary material). Project page: https://peacelwh.github.io/WACO/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Fine-tuning a vision-language-action (VLA) model on a few dozen demonstrations is the standard way to put a manipulation policy on a robot, yet the resulting policy silently assumes that the camera and the robot base stay exactly where they were during data collection: a bumped tripod or a base re-docked a few centimetres away makes it reach for the wrong place. We show that both failures are one phenomenon, a change of the camera-to-base extrinsic that the policy has memorised, and that the robot carries everything needed to undo it. We introduce WACO, a world-anchored canonicalization framework for VLA models: the arm, placed by forward kinematics at the measured joint angles, serves as a metric calibration target, and the static scene serves as the world anchor that tells a moved camera from a moved robot. From a single RGB-D frame, WACO estimates both shifts without ground-truth extrinsics or extra data, canonicalises the image to the calibration viewpoint and the state, action and start pose to the calibration base frame, and fine-tunes the unmodified policy under the artefacts of its own estimator. We also introduce LIBERO-PoseShift, the first benchmark for RGB VLAs with graded camera, base and compound shifts. Across LIBERO-PoseShift, the LIBERO-Plus camera track and four real-world tasks on a UR5e robot, WACO consistently outperforms the standard few-shot recipe and prior viewpoint-robust methods, raising shifted success by 13.2 points in simulation and by 36.7 points on the real robot, recovering 90% of the simulation gap to a ground-truth oracle and 87% of the real-robot gap, and establishing a new state of the art among methods that use no perturbed training data, with no test-time calibration. Project page: https://peacelwh.github.io/WACO/.



### Transferable Named Sparse Features for Morphology-based Gleason Grading
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24363v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24363v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 12:57:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:57:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Argyrios Zafeiriou, Athanasios Kallipolitis, Ilias Maglogiannis
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deep learning systems grade prostate biopsies at the level of pathologists, but most report a grade without the glandular architecture that justifies it. This work presents a grading system built on a sparse code over the tokens of a pathology foundation model. A sparse autoencoder pretrained on biopsies is aligned with multi-reader annotations, so that 69 of its 2048 features are named after Gleason morphologies such as cribriform glands, cords, and comedonecrosis. The same code feeds a grading network, which reads all features to predict Gleason patterns and the Grade Group, while morphology maps read directly from the named features. We evaluate the system on five public datasets that include needle biopsies, tissue-microarray cores, multi-reader annotations, and three scanners. The morphology maps remain stable across alignment runs and, without retraining, detect cribriform glands in external biopsies (area under the receiver operating characteristic curve, AUROC, 0.849) and pattern 5 in unseen cores when evaluated by up to six pathologists (AUROC 0.846 to 0.919). In an independent biopsy cohort, Grade Group agreement with three pathologists on Leica scans is close to their agreement with each other. Removing the named features reduces the corresponding Gleason pattern predictions by 49% to 98%, with effects larger than those from 20 activity-matched random feature removals. These results show that a shared sparse representation can support competitive Gleason grading while providing named morphological evidence that transfers across datasets and whose contribution to grading can be measured directly.



### SCOUT: Risk-Guided Foundation-Model Escalation for Robust Tissue Detection in Whole-Slide Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24366v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24366v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:00:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:00:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Cristian Tommasino, Antonio M. Rinaldi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reliable tissue detection is essential for whole-slide image analysis, yet high average segmentation accuracy can conceal failures involving pale tissue, small fragments, and heterogeneous staining. Pathology foundation models offer transferable representations, but exhaustive inference is computationally demanding and may introduce false-positive tissue predictions. We propose SCOUT (Selective Correction of Uncertain Tissue), a risk-guided cascade that selectively refines an initial thumbnail-based tissue mask. A lightweight audit network estimates the spatial distribution of segmentation errors and prioritizes candidate regions for processing by a frozen pathology foundation model. An asymmetric consensus rule integrates the resulting predictions with the initial mask to recover missed tissue while limiting false positives. A calibrated slide-level review score additionally controls the marginal rate of unflagged case-group failures under the calibration assumptions. Evaluation on a public multi-source, multi-stain benchmark shows that SCOUT reduces the proportion of case groups requiring mask correction from 38.1% for the best released detector to 18.6%, with particularly pronounced improvements in immunohistochemistry. Source-held-out experiments demonstrate better generalization than a segmentation network trained on the same annotations, while selective inference achieves an observed failure rate no higher than processing all candidate regions. Downstream, lymph-node classification with SCOUT masks is non-inferior to that obtained with two of the three released detectors evaluated. A variant built on an openly licensed foundation model is publicly released as an installable Python package with trained weights. These results support risk-guided foundation-model inference as an effective approach to tissue detection across heterogeneous histopathology data.



### One Affinity, Two Axes: Bidirectional Slot Attention for Iterative Aggregation across the Structure--Detail Spectrum in Visual Retrieval
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24371v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24371v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:04:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:04:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Zihang Wang, Wenkai Zhu, Lingdong Kong, Teng Yan, Alan Liang, Shuyuan Li, Xieyuanli Chen, Dong Kong, Guanyu Zong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Aggregating dense patch tokens into compact and discriminative global descriptors is central to visual retrieval. Although foundation models provide rich local features, existing aggregation heads still rely on single-pass mappings and lack an explicit mechanism to coordinate structural and fine-grained cues. Therefore, we propose Bidirectional Slot Attention (BiSA), which reformulates aggregation as an iterative coupled system. Cross-Axis Consensus (CAC) progressively refines learnable slots by interpreting a single shared token--slot affinity along two complementary axes. Along the competition axis, slots vie for each token under a temperature shared across all of them, determining token ownership and inducing a non-redundant scene decomposition. Along the selection axis, a slot-specific temperature governs the breadth of each slot's response, spanning a spectrum from broad layouts to sparse fine-grained details. Their consensus couples non-redundant decomposition with multi-granular slot specialization. Furthermore, the two axes expose intrinsic token-reliability cues at no extra cost: a token's claim strength under competition and its salience under selection. Progressive Token Filtering (PTF) converts these cues into a retention probability that down-weights unreliable tokens before the next CAC iteration, without any segmentation branch or external prior, forming a self-reinforcing loop of competitive decomposition, selective focusing, and feedback purification. Across all three retrieval tasks, BiSA leads on five of six place recognition benchmarks (+4.2 R@1 on Baidu Mall), tops all 16 metrics of drone--satellite geo-localization (+8.4 R@1 at 150 m), and improves instance-level retrieval on ROxford-Hard by 4.5 mAP points. Notably, the selection axis adds only 20 learnable scalars, yet improves average R@1 by 2.8 points.



### CanopyKV: Remember beyond the Crop for Fast Active Visual Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24373v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24373v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:05:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:05:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Lei Yang, Dayan Wu, Yiliang Zhu, Xinze Liu, Hengjie Zhu, Zihao Zhang, Ding Wang, Peng Fu, Zheng Lin
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 11 figures, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Active visual reasoning enables multimodal models to answer challenging visual questions by gathering fine-grained evidence through iterative image inspection and tool use. However, accumulating crops and reasoning tokens expands the key-value (KV) cache, making compression important for reducing memory and inference costs. Existing KV compression methods typically select historical tokens based on attention, recency, or redundancy. These criteria do not explicitly distinguish locally replaced evidence from complementary information needed for future exploration: each crop refreshes a local region, while earlier views may still support subsequent decisions. Discarding such evidence can impair accuracy or require additional search, while scoring and cache reorganization can offset the computational savings from compression. We introduce CanopyKV, a training-free KV compression framework that uses parent-child crop relationships to retain complementary evidence, keeps retained history stable, and protects the state needed for ongoing reasoning. Tool metadata provides retention signals without additional attention scoring, while compact KV storage reduces subsequent attention costs. Across VisualProbe, V*, HR-Bench, and MME-Lite, CanopyKV achieves state-of-the-art answer accuracy and end-to-end efficiency among the evaluated KV compression methods. On MME-Lite, it retains approximately one-quarter of Dense's total KV while improving accuracy by 10.5 percentage points and accelerating complete reasoning by $1.64\times$.



### Where Should a Diffusion Transformer Meet Its Teacher?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24380v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24380v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:09:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:09:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhuoqi Hu, Shiwei Li, Haozhao Wang, Xiandi Luo, Yihao Ouyang, Wenting Shen, Ang Wang, Jiamang Wang, Minyi Guo, Ruixuan Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Representation Alignment (REPA) significantly accelerates Diffusion Transformer training by guiding intermediate student features with a pretrained visual teacher, yet existing methods typically attach supervision to a single fixed layer. As student representations evolve, however, we find that the most effective layer for alignment varies across training stages. To track this evolving layer suitability online without costly search, we measure gradient compatibility between the flow-matching and alignment objectives, and propose AdaREPA, an online routing framework that adaptively selects the supervision layer as training progresses. AdaREPA uses a softmax distribution over smoothed layer compatibility scores to sample a layer for alignment at each training step. This probabilistic routing adapts teacher supervision to the student's changing learning needs, with minimal training overhead and no additional inference cost. On ImageNet-256, AdaREPA accelerates convergence and improves generation quality across model sizes, while revealing how the most suitable layers for supervision shift over training.



### Enhancing Active Learning Performance with Initial Data Selection Based on Foundation Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24384v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24384v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:15:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:15:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Mohammad Mirzaee, Mehrshad Fallah Estalkhzir, Mohammad Reza Mohammadi
- **Comment**: 26 pages, 18 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In active object detection, selecting the initial labeled pool is essential, especially in complex datasets with expensive labeling and low resource budget. This study presents an active learning framework designed to optimize the initial training cycle of active learning for the object detection task. Our approach diverges from traditional methods by utilizing feature-based clustering from the YOLO-World model and refining images based on objectness scores rather than relying on uncertainty sampling in the initial cycle. This approach focuses on finding samples with various features and emphasizes training in areas where the foundation model is most confident. Doing so helps the model better understand different types of objects. For subsequent cycles, we employ a consistency-based sampling technique to maintain the robustness of the training process. To evaluate our method, we use two complex datasets called Aquarium and NWPU VHR-10, which are well known for their diverse object sizes and challenging conditions. In the NWPU dataset, our method increases mAP50 by an average of 6.6% in image-based evaluation. Moreover, the image refinement method shows an improvement of approximately 11% in object-based evaluation. The results show improvements in model performance and labeling time, demonstrating the advantages of using foundation models in early cycles of active learning. An implementation of the algorithm is available at https://github.com/Mhrshd-fllh/ALBoost-FM



### Reliable Tree Recruitment from Scarce Annotations for Forest Inventory Expansion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24401v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24401v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:31:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:31:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Luiz Fernando Souza Luz, Pedro J. Soto Vega, Matheus Pinheiro Ferreira, Laura Elena Cué la Rosa, Hudson Franklin Pessoa Veras, Ernandes Macedo da Cunha Neto, Evandro Orfanó Figueiredo, Ana Paula Dalla Corte, Carlos Roberto Sanquetta, José Matheus Segre Moneva Viveiros, Matheus Santos Fuza, Heitor Eduardo Ferreira Campos Morato Filpi, Cristiano Vinícius Vidal, Vinícius Moura Costa, Débora Nascimento Rosário, Danilo Roberti Alves de Almeida, Paulo Guilherme Molin, Dário Augusto Borges Oliveira
- **Comment**: 20 pages, 15 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Species-level inventories of individual tree crowns (ITCs) from UAV imagery are limited by annotation cost: in tropical forests, many species are represented by only a handful of field-validated crowns. We propose a reliable tree recruitment framework that expands a small annotated inventory by iteratively self-training a multi-task DeepLabV3 network, combining semantic segmentation with a crown-distance regression head, on RGB UAV orthomosaics. At each iteration, candidate crowns enter the training inventory only if they pass a confidence filter on class probability and distance map and a morphology filter on crown area and solidity, with at most five new crowns per species, keeping recruited samples consistent with the reference inventory. We evaluate the framework at two Brazilian sites: an Amazonian rainforest in Acre (17 species, 355 annotated crowns, 4 cm GSD) and a forest site in the Cerrado of Maranhão (4 species, 51 annotated crowns, 1.8 cm GSD). Over 20 iterations, the number of retained crowns grew from about 500 to 960 in Acre and from about 700 to over 3100 in Maranhão, while crown-level F1 exceeded single- and multi-task DeepLabV3 baselines (53.5% vs. 50.0% and 45.2%; 92.2% vs. 79.7% and 83.6%). The framework offers a low-cost route to expanding species inventories with consumer-grade UAVs and limited fieldwork.



### RAPID: Reconstruction Accelerated by Polarization-Informed Depth for 2D Gaussian Splatting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24404v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24404v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:35:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:35:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Daisong Ouyang, Linlin Xia, Liangkun Yan, Song Gu, Shedong Zhao, Xin Dai, Yi Zheng
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures, 5tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Efficient and accurate 3D surface reconstruction from multi-view images remains a fundamental challenge in Gaussian splatting. Many existing methods rely on sparse geometric initialization and iterative densification, resulting in substantial optimization overhead and difficulties in balancing rendering quality and geometric accuracy, particularly in reflective and weakly textured regions. To address these limitations, we propose RAPID, a geometry-first framework that uses multiview polarization cues to recover dense surface priors. We integrate surface normals into depth maps using sparse geometric anchors. These depth maps are then fused across views. Curvature-adaptive sampling and oriented Gaussian initialization then support efficient optimization. Experiments on the PISR dataset demonstrate an approximately \(5\times\) speedup over PhyGaP, which is a state-of-the-art Gaussian-based reconstruction method. RAPID reduces the average optimization time from approximately 21 minutes (PhyGaP) to 4 minutes while maintaining competitive novel-view synthesis quality and mesh reconstruction accuracy. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of RAPID in accelerating Gaussian optimization from polarimetric multi-view inputs.



### StrideWorld: Benchmarking Scene Rigidity and Action Consistency in Interactive Video World Model
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24408v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24408v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:37:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:37:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Chuang Wang, Rui Nie, Jie Song, Junyuan Gao, Jiahe Song, Haitao Zhou, Boyu Zhu, Xing Li, Jiang Wu, Conghui He, Qian Yu, Weijia Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Interactive video world models turn user commands into the exploration of a generated scene, which requires the scene to keep its 3D structure as the camera moves and each action to keep a stable effect. Existing benchmarks mostly evaluate these properties against references fitted to the video under test, either a scene reconstructed from its own frames or a camera trajectory aligned to the command path with a free scale. Such references can absorb gradual scene deformation and changes in pace and route length, so these errors can pass undetected. We therefore introduce StrideWorld, a benchmark whose references are fixed before the motion they judge. It evaluates scene rigidity against the ground-truth geometry of the start view, and action consistency against the stride of each action, the camera motion of one command. The stride is measured on the model's first steps and then used to test the steps that follow, so each model is judged only against its own steps. Its measurements are validated on rendered videos with known camera motion, and its scores agree with human judgments. Across \nvideos{} videos generated by \nmodels{} open models from $140$ start views, all models but one deform their scenes as the camera moves, with offsets up to $7.3$ times those of a rigid rendering along the same camera path. Most models keep a steady pace when a single action is held. Along composed routes, several models often stall, and most completed routes end away from the positions predicted by each model's own strides. Only the two models that scale commands by the estimated scene depth bring most routes close to these positions. Fine-tuning two models on a few rendered clips removes most of their mid-route stalls. Code, data, and a leaderboard are publicly available at https://github.com/chuangwang123/strideworld.



### Why Multi-Source Audio Fails to Compose: A Geometric Diagnosis and Its Remedy
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24410v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.SD
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24410v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:38:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:38:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Anurag Singh, Prajwal Singh, Shanmuganathan Raman
- **Comment**: Accepted in BMVC 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-source audio-to-image generation exhibits consistent compositional failures when audio tokens occupy geometrically displaced, semantically unanchored positions in the CLIP text-token space, where the diffusion U-Net lacks the prior to reliably attend to them or ground them in distinct spatial regions. We formalize this as the geometric displacement phenomenon and expose it through a two-level clustering analysis comparing BEATs and CLAP embeddings at the raw encoder and projected CLIP token levels. Three geometric properties emerge as important: intra-class compactness, angular alignment with the CLIP text manifold, and inter-token spatial separation. We show that CLAP, whose contrastive language-audio pretraining aligns embeddings with natural language semantics, substantially outperforms BEATs on all three properties, improving the silhouette score by more than 3x and the Audio-Image Content score as well. Building on this analysis, we introduce a multi-token extension that requires no diffusion-backbone retraining and assigns each audio source to a distinct placeholder token in the CLIP tokenizer, complemented by a mixed inference-time objective that combines Attend-and-Excite with an A* spatial separation loss to resolve residual cross-token interference. For evaluation, we construct multi-audio compositional benchmarks from VGGSound (VGG2, VGG3, and VGGN) and extend six standard audio-image metrics to the multi-source setting. Across five benchmarks and four strong baselines, our method achieves strong results on the metrics most directly tied to compositional faithfulness, with AIC improvements of up to 2.25x over the strongest baseline and FID reduced by nearly 2x on two-source generation. We propose geometric displacement as a diagnostic framework for cross-modal generation systems that project learned embeddings into a pretrained contrastive space.



### PhysResponse: Probing the Physics of Video Generation with Controlled Interventions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24419v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24419v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:42:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:42:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuhan Li, Shuran Zhang, Haixin Tian, Baonuo Rao, Weipeng Li, Yujia Hou, Zhenyu Wang, Zijun Wang, Luoyang Lin, Lijing Luo, Jiehui Huang, Kaiyuan Yang, Jingbin Tan, Panwen Hu, Xiaodan Liang
- **Comment**: 11 pages. This version contains the main paper and references only. Supplementary material will be added in a later revision
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video generation models are increasingly viewed as world simulators, which requires not only plausible motion but dynamics that respond correctly to specified physical parameters. Existing models, datasets and benchmarks, however, largely target physical plausibility, leaving this physical parameter responsiveness insufficiently explored. We introduce PhysResponse, a benchmark that measures it at two levels: scene-conditioned simulator inversion recovers the effective gravity, friction and initial speed of generated motion, and event evaluation tests outcomes near and across critical transitions. Evaluating ten image-to-video models, we find that even the lowest parameter errors exceed those of physically correct videos by over an order of magnitude, that models rarely reproduce both outcomes of a transition pair, and that the two levels expose complementary failures that visual metrics such as PSNR miss. To supply the missing training signal, we construct 72,000 simulated videos of 20 multi-object interaction types with controlled parameters; fine-tuning on them lowers parameter errors, even on held-out interaction types, and transfers to real videos and an external physics benchmark. Continued training with a numerical encoder further lowers all three errors on held-out types. PhysResponse provides a foundation for world simulators that follow physical conditions, not just appearances.



### Resolution as Attention for Efficient Agent Memory Perception
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24422v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24422v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:43:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:43:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Siqi Xiang, Zhipeng Xu, Shengrui Liu, Zulong Chen, Chunyan Miao, Shijian Lu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: LLM-based agents accumulate extensive interaction histories when solving complex, long-horizon tasks, making efficient memory management critical to their performance and scalability. While existing memory management methods primarily focus on what to retain or retrieve, they often overlook how much perceptual detail should be allocated to individual past interactions. We posit that effective agent memory depends not only on the information preserved, but also on the granularity at which each past interaction is perceived. Motivated by this insight, we propose Resolution as Attention (RAA), a memory perception mechanism that uses visual resolution as a means of attention allocation across interaction histories. RAA renders past interactions as images and dynamically adjusts their resolution at every step according to their relevance to the agent's upcoming decision, preserving fine-grained information for critical interactions while reducing visual-token consumption for less relevant ones. To adaptively determine the perceptual fidelity of each interaction, we train a lightweight relevance estimator with teacher-model guidance and further optimize it to preserve the agent's decision consistency at a reduced visual-token cost. Experiments across multiple tasks and backbones show that RAA reduces token consumption by over 50% without compromising task performance. Moreover, RAA can be integrated as a plug-in perception layer with existing memory management methods, yielding further token savings and revealing adaptive perception as a complementary dimension of agent memory.



### Probing Physics in Motion Diffusion Models Against Their Own Estimate
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24423v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24423v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:44:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:44:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Saeed Ghorbani
- **Comment**: 32 pages, 12 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent studies report that video diffusion models "know more than they show": physical properties decode better from their activations than from their inputs or outputs. But a diffusion model computes its clean-sample estimate from those same activations, so such a comparison measures how easily a readout extracts the information. Whether the model hides anything is a separate question. We set out to find hidden physical knowledge in kinematic text-to-motion diffusion models, where the physics of every intermediate estimate can be computed exactly, and recorded every layer along the sampling trajectories of MDM (two checkpoints) and MotionDiffuse. Against the usual baselines the layers do look ahead of the model's own estimate on balance and flight. With every readout tuned the same way, the estimate closes the gap: for balance and flight, adding a layer gains at most 0.01, a size that a positive control shows we would detect. The fall rate of jumps is the one clear exception. Early in denoising, MDM's layers add about 0.08 in $R^2$ (up to 0.11 in a refit) that the estimate does not yet show, and the gain fades as sampling proceeds; on MotionDiffuse it is small and not robust. The estimate also flags floating samples without training, but aborting them saves under a tenth of the compute. In a video model, plausibility that linear probes miss in Wan 2.1's clean latent becomes readable after one fixed nonlinearity.



### Let a Small Model Read First: Faster Prefill for Long Interleaved Image-Text Inputs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24431v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24431v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:49:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:49:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaolin Lin, Kai-Chiang Wu, Bing Li, Grace Li Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) can answer questions about long interleaved inputs, such as documents of many pages and collections of hundreds of images, but they are slow to start: on inputs of about 100K tokens, the prefill takes most of the wait for the first output token. A common approach prunes the input by scoring its tokens with the target model itself or with a small model of the same family that first writes an answer. However, this scoring takes much of the time that pruning saves. We find that the evidence for a question lies in a few whole units (images or paragraphs), and that a small hybrid model finds them with one attention head of an early layer, even at half image resolution. Based on these findings, we propose SkimPrefill, which lets the small hybrid model read the whole input first, up to that early layer, with images at half resolution. Most layers of the small hybrid model (Qwen3.5-0.8B) run in linear time, so this pass is short. SkimPrefill then ranks every unit with that head and gives the target the top units as an ordinary shorter prompt. One untrained selector serves targets of different model families. Extensive experiments on MMLongBench at 128K tokens, with 19 datasets of five task types, show the effectiveness of SkimPrefill. With 10% of the input tokens kept, SkimPrefill speeds up the prefill of Qwen3.5-9B, Gemma-4-12B and Qwen3-VL-32B by 3.7 times, 11.6 time and 8.2 times while maintaining their average accuracy.



### MLIE-CoT: Chain-of-Thought Reasoning via Multi-Level Image Editing
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24433v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24433v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:49:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:49:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Baichuan Yang, Dongyuan Xu, Zhao Yang, Yong Liu, Bing Su
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 5 figures. Code: https://github.com/yang031119/MLIE-CoT
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning has proven effective in improving the reasoning abilities of large language models by breaking complex tasks into step-by-step solutions. In recent years, researchers have attempted to extend CoT reasoning to visual generative models to improve their ability to handle complex tasks. However, existing CoT methods for text-to-image generation still rely mainly on textual reasoning and therefore cannot fully use and preserve the correct semantic information already present in an image, as even the most detailed language cannot capture all visual information. With advances in image generation, models such as FLUX and BAGEL can generate images conditioned on visual inputs. Recent work has attempted to carry images forward as context in the chain of thought, but this introduces another limitation: unsuitable earlier images can strongly interfere with the model, while repeated whole-image regeneration introduces new hallucinations at each round and causes them to accumulate. To address these issues, we propose MLIE-CoT, a visual chain-of-thought framework based on multi-level image editing. MLIE-CoT learns to select the repair granularity from three levels, preserving correct semantics whenever possible during reasoning while reducing the effect of incorrect content. To improve the ability of multimodal models to route errors of different severity to appropriate repair levels, we construct an image-repair dataset covering different error cases and use it to train a repair-level router. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MLIE-CoT significantly outperforms both its base models and methods using fixed repair strategies, producing clear improvements across multiple benchmarks.



### Explainable and CPU-Efficient Brain Tumor Classification from MRI Using Two-Phase EfficientNetB3 Fine-Tuning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24440v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24440v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:55:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:55:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Deepro Das, Saad Kabir Uddin, Mohammad Alauddin, Bornali Sarker, Thasnim Akhter
- **Comment**: 6 pages, 5 figures, 6 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Brain tumors are among the most lethal neurological disorders, with over 308,000 new diagnoses annually worldwide. Accurate classification of tumor type from Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) scans is critical for treatment planning; however, manual radiological interpretation is time-consuming and prone to inter-observer variability. This paper proposes an automated deep learning pipeline for four-class brain MRI classification (glioma, meningioma, no tumor, and pituitary tumor) using EfficientNetB3 transfer learning with a custom classification head. A two-phase training strategy is adopted: Phase 1 trains the head with a frozen backbone (best validation accuracy: 92.77%), and Phase 2 selectively fine-tunes the top 37 layers at a 100x reduced learning rate (best validation accuracy: 94.91%). On an independent balanced test set of 1,600 MRI images, the system achieves 90.62% overall accuracy and a macro-average AUC of 0.9854. Gradient-weighted Class Activation Mapping (Grad-CAM) is integrated for class-discriminative visual explainability. The proposed system simultaneously addresses three dimensions absent in prior work: high accuracy, CPU-only computational efficiency, and clinical interpretability.



### NativeSeg: Native Vision Language Segmentation of 3D Computed Tomography via Spatial Visual Tokens and Verified Local Zoom
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24443v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24443v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:56:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:56:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Chengqian Ma, Gaole Dai, Zijie Meng, Weixuan Jin, Yijie Du, Yuexiao Ma, Xiawu Zheng, Fei Chao
- **Comment**: 13 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/step-out/NativeSeg ; Weights: https://huggingface.co/ChengqianMa/NativeSeg
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text prompted 3D computed tomography (CT) segmentation requires both full slice context and fine boundary precision across volumes where over $80\%$ of organ prompted slices lack the queried target. Existing medical vision language models (VLMs) are largely non native segmenters: they delegate pixel delineation outside the VLM by compressing reasoning into a 1D [SEG] vector for an external Segment Anything Model (SAM) decoder, quantizing masks into lossy discrete codebooks, or issuing box prompts to stateless external SAM tools without empty slice screening or spatial state memory. We introduce NativeSeg, a native vision language segmentation framework on Low Rank Adaptation (LoRA) adapted Qwen3.8-27B that performs dense mask decoding, volumetric triage, spatial error self diagnosis, and multiround local zoom directly over the VLM's 2D spatial visual tokens without external SAM decoders. NativeSeg decodes confidence maps from upper layer 2D spatial tokens fused with a five slice U-Net pathway, halts at zero zooms on $>91\%$ of standalone evaluations via a native presence gate (<pres>), localizes uncertain boundaries via a 20 cell multiscale error gate (<err>), and accepts zoomed updates only when predicted error decreases. Trained solely on adult CT, NativeSeg improves Round $0$ DSC by $+19.81$ and $+7.04$ points over discrete codebook and 1D [SEG] readouts on the identical backbone, reaches $86.81\%$ adult and $87.04\%$ zero shot pediatric DSC, and safely refines 2D nnU-Net ($89.52\%$ adult; $86.86\%$ pediatric), 3D SegVol ($86.81\%$), and 3D VoxTell ($89.85\%$).



### Retroactive Concept Bottleneck Models for Exemplar-Free Class-Incremental Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24445v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24445v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 13:56:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 13:56:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Yang Ji, Ying Sun, Yuanxin Zhuang, Yuting Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Exemplar-free class-incremental learning (EFCIL) requires models to learn new classes sequentially without retaining training samples from previous tasks. Continual concept bottleneck models (CBMs) make their predictions interpretable by identifying visual concepts in an image and using them to score each class. However, these methods typically focus on preserving learned concepts or approximating past classes with generated features, which can leave new concepts underused in old-class predictions. We propose RetroCBM, which retains a small set of vocabulary-independent statistics relating frozen visual features to the embedding space of a pretrained vision-language model. Even after earlier training images have been discarded, we can use these statistics to supervise new concepts. To do so, we develop a retroactive regression rule that yields the same predictor for each new concept as class-balanced ridge regression on all images seen so far. We then reproject the stored statistics to recover the full-history squared classification loss and thus learn how much each new concept should contribute to old-class predictions. Since the classifier may use systematic concept errors to distinguish nearby classes, we further introduce a graph-weighted penalty on the margin shifts caused by those errors. Across six datasets, RetroCBM outperforms state-of-the-art interpretable methods in all class-incremental settings, with an 18.1\% relative gain in mean average incremental accuracy over the strongest exemplar-free baseline.



### EviScope: Evidence-Conditioned Sampling for Long-Range 3D Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24451v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24451v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:00:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:09:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Mingyang Lyu, Yinqian Sun, Gongce Cao, Chujie Tang, Yuhao Zhang, Lu Jia, Qian Zhang, Bo Wang, Pin Lyu
- **Comment**: 29 pages, including appendices; author metadata simplified; paper content unchanged
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Query-based camera-radar detectors choose where and over which frames each object query samples from the query's position or feature. We test whether radar evidence around the query should also set these choices at the 150 m range of MAN TruckScenes, where recall differs across object-level radar-return counts within every range band. EviScope is a minimal test: the current frame's returns form a non-learned bird's-eye evidence field, which each query reads at its decoded centre to scale its radial sampling extent and to weight eight frames. It adds 5,450 parameters (0.009%) to RaCFormer, with no latency increase in our timing. Our module-free recipe exceeds the published TruckScenes mAP (25.9 over seven runs against 24.1), so we measure the module only against its own baseline at matched seeds. The evidence a query reads rises with its object's return count (Spearman 0.51) but captures zero-return objects only in part, and the learned radius tracks it ($ρ\approx-0.91$ in four runs). Over seven four-epoch pairs the module changes NDS by $+0.31\pm0.36$ and mAP by $-0.27\pm0.22$ (mean $\pm$ SE). Against a geometry-only conditioner with the same parameter count, trained at three matched seeds, it is higher by $+1.25\pm0.72$ NDS and $+0.87\pm0.21$ mAP, on mAP in every pair, and the control's mean NDS is near the baseline's. An exploratory far-range gain failed an internally pre-specified held-out test, and large inference-time substitution penalties also occur without query-local evidence: neither learned allocation nor inference-time dependence substitutes for matched end-to-end comparisons.



### ShoWorld: Shape-Faithful Multi-Hypothesis Occupancy World Model
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24457v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24457v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:02:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:02:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhiqiang Yan, Guangfa Wang, Yuan Wu, Jian Yang, Gim Hee Lee
- **Comment**: 10 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Occupancy world models struggle to forecast moving objects due to motion uncertainty and shape distortion. A single trajectory can average plausible futures into an implausible path, and repeated re-voxelization can erode or inflate object shape even under correct motion. We introduce ShoWorld to address these challenges through separate motion and shape representations. The motion forecaster predicts probabilistic trajectories conditioned on scene layout, nearby agents and the future ego trajectory. Each hypothesis is rendered into occupancy and fused according to its probability to retain alternative futures. The shape component predicts sub-voxel points with occupancy probabilities and offsets from local geometry. These points move rigidly with the object, and voxel occupancy follows the probability that at least one occupied point falls within it. This representation reduces shape distortion under motion. ShoWorld achieves state-of-the-art results on Occ3D-nuScenes. With ground-truth occupancy and ego trajectory, it improves mIoU and IoU by 6.56 and 9.16 points overall and by 6.06 and 5.53 points on dynamic classes.



### Anchoring with Visual Evidence: Explicit Semantic Anchors for Joint Change Detection and Captioning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24462v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24462v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:03:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:03:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Kai Zheng, Hang-Cheng Dong, Puyi Fang, Junjia Pan, Zhenkai Wu, Wei Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Joint change detection and captioning seeks to localize changes and describe them from bi-temporal remote sensing images. A key challenge is translating dense visual evidence into the semantic conditions that guide description generation. We propose Explicit Semantic Anchors (ESA), a supervised interface that exposes entity presence, temporal appearance variation, and spatial extent. A prediction-guided readout combines visual features and predicted change probabilities, and three factor heads estimate the corresponding anchor activations. These activations guide generation through two complementary routes. The context route maps the three factor activations to three separate memory tokens, one per factor, and appends them to the dense visual memory, while the lexicon route produces an additive vocabulary bias at each decoding step. Dense visual memory remains available for spatial detail and information beyond the explicit factors. Anchor targets are derived from existing images and change annotations, requiring no additional anchor-level language annotation. At inference, all conditions are computed from the input image pair and the model's own predictions. Experiments on LEVIR-MCI and WHU-CDC show strong joint detection and captioning performance, supporting the use of supervised visual attributes as explicit conditions for change description generation.



### EgoPass-Bench: Identifying Objects That May Affect the Wearer's Passage in Egocentric Video
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24995v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24995v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:16:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:16:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongyu Wang, Chuhan Li, Congjin Xu, Xuandong Zhao, Hua Shen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Understanding an egocentric environment requires reasoning about how surrounding entities affect the observer's actions. Existing evaluations of multimodal foundation models (MFMs) examine observer localization, motion trajectories, and action feasibility, but leave the discovery and interpretation of evolving observer-entity interactions insufficiently assessed. We introduce EgoPass-Bench, a benchmark for observer-relative interaction understanding in real-world egocentric video. EgoPass-Bench comprises 284 manually reviewed evaluation clips recorded using Ray-Ban Meta (Gen 2) smart glasses and 2,201 distinct questions across five tasks assessing motion perception, interaction judgments about specified entities, and relevant-entity discovery without target cues. Our evaluation of eight frame-based and six native-video configurations reveals a pronounced discrepancy between motion perception and interaction understanding. The strongest evaluated model, GPT-6 Astra, correctly recognizes object motion in 91.37% of 394 comparisons involving the same targets, but correctly identifies changes in passage constraints in only 66.50%. Across all 574 entity-discovery questions, it trails the two-person human baseline by $23.65 \pm 7.45$ percentage points (paired 95% CI), while exceeding the always-empty baseline (36.76%) by at least 18.9 percentage points. Further analysis reveals frequent inclusion of entities that neither pose a plausible physical conflict nor constrain passage, showing that accurate motion recognition does not ensure reliable relevant-entity discovery.



### Every Query Routes Itself: Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers with Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24479v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24479v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:17:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:17:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhen Huang, Shuwei Li, Ruizhe Yao, Xinrui Chen, Danyi Liu, Haoyuan Liang, Zhiyu Ye, Qiuyan Sun, Bingwei Chen, Yiyuan Li, Xu Zhou, Haohuan Fu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Self-attention over long token sequences dominates the inference cost of video diffusion transformers. Clustering-based sparse attention clusters both queries and keys and lets all queries in a cluster share one selection of key clusters. We show that this sharing limits accuracy: with key clusters and density fixed, attention grows more accurate as the query clusters are refined, and is most accurate when each query selects on its own, which needs no query clustering at all. We therefore propose Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention (PQRS), a training-free method that clusters only keys, lets each query select its own key clusters, and compensates for the unselected ones with their centroids. Per-query selections leave no query tile with a shared key list, so PQRS traverses key clusters instead of queries, buckets queries with identical selections into dense tasks, and merges their partial results in one pass under a fixed softmax offset carried from the previous denoising step. On four text-to-video and image-to-video models from the Wan and HunyuanVideo families, PQRS is the fastest of all the sparse attention methods we evaluate and the closest to the dense output, reaching $28.9$ dB PSNR on Wan2.1-T2V and $35.4$ dB on HunyuanVideo at $1.66$--$2.10\times$ end-to-end speedup.



### RECLAIM: Robot-Vision-Informed Adversarial Patch Defense via Embodied Attention Feedback
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24494v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CR, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24494v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:24:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:24:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiayou Zhu, Peng Guo, Litian Zhang, Xinze Shi, Yuting Zhang, Zelong Yu, Yize Tian, Jirong Liu, Zifeng Kang
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 2 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language-action policies control embodied agents through a perception-action loop grounded in physical interaction, where adversarial patches can corrupt action predictions and thereby alter subsequent observations. To mitigate these threats, many existing defenses rely on policy adaptation or auxiliary models, but require additional training or inference. We introduce RECLAIM, a training-free, inference-time defense that uses a frozen VLA's own attention response to local visual interventions to guide purification during closed-loop embodied manipulation. Our key insight is that attention changes induced by local edits provide feedback for choosing where and how to purify as the robot interacts with its environment. RECLAIM combines this feedback with robot geometry to refine patch localization and select local treatments, then revalidates them as the agent's embodied actions change the observed scene. We evaluate RECLAIM against patches generated by four adversarial attack methods across multiple manipulation task suites under frozen VLA control. Under adversarial patch attacks, RECLAIM succeeds in at least one rollout on 95% of the evaluated tasks and raises rollout success on LIBERO-Object from 0% without defense to 70.0%. Its mean decision time is only 548.9 ms per control step. These results highlight policy-guided visual purification as an effective approach to defending embodied agents against adversarial patches and recovering closed-loop task execution.



### Comparing Each Generation with Independent Siblings Gives Video World Model Post-Training a Proper Objective for Outcome Frequencies
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24512v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24512v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:34:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:34:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Xin Li, Yiwen Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video world models are used as simulators, so when a first frame leaves several physical outcomes open, repeated generations should produce each outcome as often as it occurs in the data. Physics post-training scores every generation against the single recorded future of its history, which makes single videos more faithful, but the expected score is linear in the model distribution and is maximized by always producing the most likely outcome. The log-likelihood of the recorded outcome is maximized by the data distribution, and its gradient weights agreement with the record by the inverse of how often the model produces the generated outcome, a frequency that other generations of the same history with independent noise, its siblings, can estimate. To train with this weight from a finite group, we propose SibPO, which performs Sibling-paired Policy Optimization: siblings estimate a truncated inverse-frequency weight for agreement with the record, and a second term subtracts the same weight times the agreement with the siblings, which makes the expected objective a strictly proper divergence over outcome cells at every group size. On PisaBench, SibPO lowers the KS distance to simulated landing times by 9.1-10.2% relative to the strongest method outside its reward family on three of four video models without degrading single videos, and it lowers the error of estimated robot policy success rates by 13%.



### RAID: Representation Autoencoder for Intrinsic Decomposition
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24521v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24521v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:38:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:38:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Claudia Cuttano, Tamy Boubekeur, Elena Garces, Giuseppe Vecchio
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Single-image intrinsic decomposition is an inherently ill-posed problem, requiring the disentanglement of strongly coupled appearance factors from a single RGB observation. Recent methods address this ambiguity with diffusion priors, at the cost of large, iterative, and often stochastic models. We show that accurate decomposition does not require a large generative model. Our key idea is to decouple reconstruction from decomposition on top of a strong pretrained representation. First, an $X \rightarrow X$ teacher learns to reconstruct each intrinsic map from DINOv3 features using a shared decoder, without paired RGB-to-intrinsic supervision. Then, a DINOv3-based student predicts the corresponding teacher representations from RGB through lightweight, modality-specific projections, enabling training on partially annotated datasets and predicting all intrinsics in a single forward pass. \textbf{RAID}, predicts intrinsic properties in the structured latent space, enabling a compact 165M-parameter model, 100x faster than diffusion-based methods. Despite its compact size, it achieves competitive or superior decomposition quality, with improved robustness to input resolution. Additionally, our learned projections yield interpretable, modality-specific feature spaces.



### SCRiD: Spatial and Contextual References for Generalizable AI-Generated Video Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24524v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24524v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:39:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:39:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Hao Tan, Zekai Zuo, Boyang Chen, Jinghui Qin
- **Comment**: 14 pages,10 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generalizable AI-generated video detection remains challenging because natural motion and content variations can resemble synthesis artifacts, making isolated video discrepancies unreliable indicators of authenticity. To address this challenge, we propose Spatial and Contextual Reference-informed Detection (SCRiD), a framework that interprets video variations through complementary spatial and contextual references. Specifically, we introduce Matched-Coordinate Residual Response (MCR) to characterize temporal residual associations under motion and coordinate references with matched subpixel sampling conditions. We further develop Real-Conditioned Score Adaptation (RCSA), which learns a content-conditioned denoising reference from authentic videos and probes its responses across multiple noise scales and quality views to guide feature adaptation. To integrate these complementary sources of evidence, we design Reversible Evidence Exchange (REE) to enable information exchange through reversible symplectic updates. We train SCRiD using synthetic videos from only 4 generators and evaluate it across 88 generator or task subsets spanning 6 benchmarks. Our method achieves average ACC and AP of 72.72% and 74.23%, respectively, outperforming the strongest baseline by 3.24 and 0.81 percentage points.Ablation and robustness experiments further validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and the stability of SCRiD under common post-processing operations.



### Fuse Before Reconstruct: A Novel Reconstruction Paradigm for Multimodal Industrial Anomaly Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24541v1
- **DOI**: 10.1016/j.inffus.2026.104644
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24541v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:49:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:49:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhi Li, Hanyu Wang, Qiyu Chen, Fei Shen, Zhengtao Zhang
- **Comment**: Preprint before peer review. Includes supplementary material. Code: https://github.com/LiZhi-CASIA/FBR
- **Journal**: Information Fusion 137 (2027) 104644
- **Summary**: Industrial anomaly detection plays a pivotal role in precision manufacturing, where joint analysis of multimodal data emerges as a critical approach to enhance detection accuracy. Feature reconstruction, a dominant paradigm for such joint analysis, is primarily implemented through intra-modal and cross-modal methods, both of which are plagued by significant limitations. Intra-modal reconstruction neglects complementary cross-modal information, while its cross-modal counterpart is prone to projection errors and ambiguous one-to-many mappings (e.g., same textures mapping to multiple geometries), leading to detection failures. To address these limitations, we propose a novel feature reconstruction paradigm, Fuse Before Reconstruct (FBR), which first fuses multimodal features and then performs reconstruction for anomaly detection. Firstly, we propose a bidirectional cross-modal attention mechanism to achieve deep feature complementarity between image and point cloud features, mitigating projection bias through channel alignment and dynamic spatial prior integration. Besides, a learnable pixel-wise gated fusion strategy is adopted to preserve multi-modal structural consistency while optimizing local feature combination. Finally, dual-path collaborative reconstruction is implemented to recover original modal features from the fused representations, where cross-modal consistency constraints effectively eliminate mapping ambiguities. Extensive experiments show that FBR achieves 98.3% I-AUROC on MVTec 3D-AD and 92.6% I-AUROC on Eyecandies, surpassing previous state-of-the-art methods by +2.9% and +4.1%, respectively.



### SCR: Detecting Backdoor-Poisoned Samples through Clean-Reference Residuals
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24558v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24558v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 14:57:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 14:57:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Yi Yang, Jinyang Huang, Feng-Qi Cui, Yu-Tong Guo, Sirui Zhao, Haiming Jin, Weihua Li, Quan Bai, Meng Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deep neural networks are vulnerable to backdoor attacks, in which adversaries manipulate model predictions by injecting poisoned samples into training data. Detecting these samples remains challenging because legitimate semantic variation can obscure poisoning-related signals in learned representations. Consequently, class-level statistics used by existing detectors may flag benign feature deviations while overlooking poisoned samples that blend into the class distribution. In this paper, we propose Semantic Counterfactual Residualization (SCR), which uses a small set of clean reference samples to account for benign variation through sample-specific feature reconstruction. SCR identifies poisoned samples by detecting coherent deviations from these clean baselines, without requiring prior knowledge of the attacks. We evaluate SCR through experiments covering twelve dirty-label and clean-label attacks across CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet. With a small clean-reference set, SCR achieves attack-averaged true positive rates above 99.5\% and false positive rates below 2\% on each dataset, outperforming eight representative detection baselines in macro-TPR.



### Beyond Appearance: Evidence-Guided Programmatic 3D Scene Reconstruction with Agentic Lighting Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24565v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24565v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:01:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:01:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeting Luo, Yifan Li, Shuai Yang
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://logic-al-al.github.io/LightHarness Code: https://github.com/lyf1212/LightHarness_Code
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Image-to-code methods for 3D scene reconstruction produce executable code that generates editable digital content and supports visual simulation. However, existing methods primarily rely on appearance similarity, which provides limited guidance for distinguishing errors in geometry, materials, and illumination. Light shapes scene appearance through its interactions with geometry and materials, influencing shading, cast shadows, and reflections. We therefore present LightHarness, a VLM-based agentic framework that incorporates explicit lighting reasoning into iterative scene reconstruction. To be specific, we leverage complementary geometric, intrinsic, and illumination cues to jointly refine scene structure and appearance, and propose Lighting Reasoning Memory to maintain light-source hypotheses, which supports evidence, and revision history throughout iterations. Together, they support reasoning about partially visible and unseen sources, with evidence-guided verification directing subsequent revisions. Our framework reconstructs explicit scenes through coherent reasoning about geometry, materials, and illumination without additional training or differentiable rendering. Extensive experiments demonstrate superior image-to-code reconstruction accuracy under challenging shadows and complex illumination, further supporting downstream scene editing, object insertion, and versatile relighting.



### BaseRSI: Recursive Self-Improvement of a Base Vision-Language Model via Training-Free Harness Internalization
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24578v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24578v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:10:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:10:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Mingda Zhang, Yuanhao Dong, Qiang Huang, Rui Qian, Yanjin Li, Zhenyu Liu, Xiao Huang, Zihao Yang, Jian Wang, Pinjia He, Xiaoying Tang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recursive self-improvement (RSI) is moving from the harness of prompts and tools around a base model toward the model itself. However, existing RSI still faces key challenges, including harness-bound gains that stay outside the base model, black-box supervision that reads only the model's outputs, and imprecise edits whose site and strength are fixed in advance. To address these challenges, we propose BaseRSI, a new paradigm of Recursive Harness Internalization: the base model improves its harness, then writes what the harness adds into its own weights through training-free edits, round after round on its own responses. To support this paradigm, we introduce ReGap, a mechanism that supervises every transformer block with the high-dimensional activation difference that the harness makes at that block alone, and updates the block's weights along this signal in a low-dimensional subspace. Building on this mechanism, we further propose ReKey, an algorithm that writes this signal by regression into a few key-value neurons of every block, and measures the site and strength of each edit on the model's own responses. Moreover, the base model fills every role of this recursion with forward passes alone, and its edits, written at different sites, compose in one checkpoint without refitting. Experimental results on ten visual reasoning benchmarks show that BaseRSI consistently improves a base vision-language model both in distribution and on held-out benchmarks. Our code is available at https://github.com/beita6969/BaseRSI.



### ALIVE: Bring Foundation Features to Life with Events
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24579v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24579v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:10:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:10:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Rong Zou, Haiqing Hao, Davide Scaramuzza
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual foundation models provide powerful representations for diverse tasks, but their features become stale as visual content changes between image observations. Refreshing them requires new frames and repeated runs of a computationally expensive image encoder. We introduce ALIVE, an event-based method that incrementally updates native foundation model features between image observations. Given an anchor image, ALIVE uses subsequent events to transport and correct existing multi-layer features extracted by a frozen DINOv3 encoder. Its lightweight updater is trained end-to-end using only synthetic data, without downstream task supervision, and transfers directly to real-world event streams without any adaptation. Across three real-world datasets and five task-dataset settings spanning object detection, semantic segmentation, and depth estimation, ALIVE outperforms all evaluated event-based methods in every metric. On DSEC, when the anchor frame is 200,ms old, ALIVE improves over anchor-image predictions by 30.9,mAP in detection, 11.6,mIoU in segmentation, and 15.9% in depth AbsRel. Compared with image re-encoding, ALIVE reduces FLOPs by 82.4% and latency by a factor of 2.5.



### DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24597v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:23:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:23:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Salman Mohammad, Furkan Kasım, Manuel Günther
- **Comment**: Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.



### EgoJev: Finding Evidence in Egocentric Video by Reading VLM Decisions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24616v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24616v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:34:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:34:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Chunggi Lee, Hanspeter Pfister
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Questions about long egocentric video often depend on one brief moment, such as the second a hand puts an object down. A vision-language model (VLM) that sees a few evenly spaced frames usually misses it. Training-free methods rank frames with a separate image-text model or ask the VLM to write a time. In a controlled test, the VLM itself locates the moment better when it chooses among labeled frames, and we read this choice from its output distribution. With the same candidates, this choice lands on the evidence more often than image-text scores or a written time span, and it compares all candidates in one decision. Based on this finding, we propose EgoJev, a training-free frame selector for wearable assistants. EgoJev reads where the evidence is from labeled thumbnails and looks again at the windows it points to. It places its few answer frames at the quantiles of the resulting distribution. One frozen VLM selects, answers and returns the evidence time, with no second model. A wearable device can encode its coarse thumbnails while it records. With Qwen3.5-4B and 8 answer frames, EgoJev raises QAEgo4D accuracy from 51.2% with uniform sampling to 62.0%, and it outperforms recent training-free frame selectors on QAEgo4D and MyEgo. A single-level version runs on a Mac and an iPhone.



### H3Remover: Reinforcing Video Object Removal without Paired Data
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24633v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24633v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:45:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:45:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Kangjie Chen, Xiangyu Li, Jinhao Deng, Dongbin Zhang, Chaoda Zheng, Shijia Chen, Yuqi Zhang, Choo Sin Wai, Boyi Sun, Puyi Wang, Yu Zhang, Xianming Liu, Boyang Wang
- **Comment**: Project page: https://chenkangjie1123.github.io/H3Remover/ , code at: https://github.com/chenkangjie1123/H3Remover
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pretrained video editors possess rich removal and completion priors, yet may retain targets or alter unrelated content. We present H3Remover, which reinforces MiniMax-H3 Base Ref2VA for video object removal without paired object-free training targets. To discourage superficial edits such as recoloring, a target-removal reward favors calibrated depth changes consistent with revealing farther content. To limit unintended scene changes, a background-geometry reward encourages preservation of relative depth ordering. To preserve background appearance without retaining unwanted shadows or reflections, an appearance reward constrains RGB changes in source-derived protected regions while exempting targets and estimated effects. Frozen HPSv3 complements these constraints with perceptual-quality feedback for synthesized content. Evaluation regions remain fixed across candidates, and DiffusionART optimizes LoRA adapters with the backbone frozen. Experiments show substantial reconstruction gains over original H3, with qualitative comparisons demonstrating more effective object removal and better preservation of surrounding scene content.



### Hypnos: A Dual-Phase End-to-End Platform for Scalable Continual Learning at the Edge
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24637v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AR**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24637v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:46:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:46:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Guilherme Guedes, Marco Apolinario Lainez, Charlotte Frenkel
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Continual learning (CL) enables neural networks to adapt to non-stationary data streams by mitigating catastrophic forgetting, offering a path to robust, private, and adaptable edge devices. Yet, porting CL to real-world edge devices remains challenging for two reasons: (i) online operation, where sensor data arrives one sample at a time and the device must adapt on-the-fly, and (ii) the low footprint of edge devices, which makes traditional error backpropagation training prohibitive. Inspired by the brain's wake and sleep cycles, the former for rapid adaptation with minimal resources and the latter for long-term consolidation, we introduce Hypnos, an open-source dual-phase platform for efficient CL at the edge. In the wake phase (e.g., daily operation), Hypnos relies on a low-footprint system-on-chip with a RISC-V microcontroller unit (MCU) targeting efficient inference and supporting lightweight gradient-free prototype updates. In the sleep phase (e.g., night-time charging), where wall-plug energy and larger memory budgets are available, a smart charger performs rehearsal-based gradient updates for consolidation. Deployed on a ZCU104 field-programmable gate array (FPGA) and synthesized in a 28-nm process, Hypnos is benchmarked on the CORe50 NICv2-391 protocol. It sets a new state-of-the-art accuracy of 78.6%-81.6% for edge CL systems, with an on-device latency of 7.79s-10.54s per sample depending on activation precision, and requires $3.8\times$ fewer learning steps and $22\times$ less learning compute than quantized latent replay at higher accuracy and comparable latency.



### AggCLIP: Aggregating CLIP for Zero-Shot Multimodal Anomaly Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24643v1
- **DOI**: 10.1145/3767308.3835551
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24643v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:49:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:49:05+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhi Li, Hanyu Wang, Fei Shen, Zhengtao Zhang
- **Comment**: 13 pages, 4 figures, 6 tables. Author version of the ACM Multimedia 2026 paper
- **Journal**: Proceedings of the 34th ACM International Conference on Multimedia (MM '26), 2026, pp. 5739-5748
- **Summary**: Zero-Shot Multimodal Anomaly Detection (ZSMAD) aims to identify both appearance and geometric defects in novel categories without relying on target-specific training data, making it especially valuable in real-world scenarios constrained by data scarcity, privacy concerns, or high annotation costs. While recent approaches successfully adapt vision-language models like CLIP to achieve this open-vocabulary generalization, they still face significant challenges: (i) severe geometric information loss during 3D-to-2D point cloud rendering; (ii) spatially noisy 2D feature maps produced by raw CLIP patch embeddings, compounded by shallow late-stage modality fusion; and (iii) severe cross-modal semantic misalignment due to decoupled processing streams. To address these limitations, we propose AggCLIP, a novel framework that adapts frozen CLIP representations for ZSMAD by explicitly incorporating local geometric structure and visual spatial context. Specifically, to accurately localize fine-grained defects, we design a Geometry-Aware Spatial Aggregation (GSA) module that refines back-projected 3D point features via density-adaptive neighborhood weighting, alongside a Context-Aware Spatial Aggregation (CSA) module that smooths multi-scale 2D patch embeddings using a lightweight trainable adapter. Furthermore, to prevent semantic drift between modalities, we introduce a Dual-Stream Prompt Contrastive (DSPC) loss, which aligns the abstract concepts of normality and abnormality across the point cloud and RGB streams. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed AggCLIP framework achieves state-of-the-art performance on the MVTec 3D-AD and Eyecandies benchmarks under both one-vs-rest and cross-dataset zero-shot settings, notably surpassing the strongest competitive methods by up to 2.9% in pixel-level AUPRO.



### Piccolo: Co-Evolving Decision Models and Data through Recursive Self-Improvement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24651v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CL**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24651v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-18 15:53:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 15:53:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhenqing Ling, Qirui Jiao, Daoyuan Chen, Cheng Chen, Xinji Mai, Haowen Gao, Zhuo Zhang, Xuchen Pan, Mengsu Ding, Jiaji Deng, Yuexiang Xie, Xiaoming Qin, Weirui Kuang, Zhaoyang Liu, Yaliang Li, Bolin Ding, Jingren Zhou
- **Comment**: 22 pages, 13 figures, 11 tables. Project page and blog: https://decisionplayground.agentscope.io/piccolo.html
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present Piccolo, a family of multimodal decision models (0.8B to 27B) that coevolves model capabilities and training data via Agentic Data Engineering and recursive self-improvement (RSI). The models predict probabilities over candidate answers for general-purpose text and visual judgments, agent harnesses, data workflows, and interactive games. Piccolo-27B tops the Decision Index Vision Full leaderboard with a score of 75.59, while Piccolo-9B and Piccolo-4B achieve 68.35 and 67.46, respectively. All three rank first on the Private benchmark in their respective model-size categories. Research agents analyze model behavior, construct training data, conduct experiments, and use the results to refine subsequent proposals. Semantic slices and a shared research memory connect these actions across the model family's development. With training recipes comprising hundreds of thousands of examples, data selection and feedback-driven refinement are central to the self-improvement process. Our large-scale RSI exploration yields three counter-intuitive insights for decision-model development: (1) Backbone-recipe co-adaptation outweighs raw model recency: earlier backbones can surpass newer generations on visual tasks under identical recipes; (2) Targeted data curation enables 4B-9B models to rival 27B baselines on specialized workflows; and (3) Continuous loss reduction can decouple from and even degrade discrete decision accuracy. Together with the released weights and tools from the Data-Juicer and AgentScope ecosystems, Piccolo provides an actionable blueprint for building frontier decision models and scaling data-driven RSI.
